# Architecture — GFit: Generative Information Modeler (industrial first)

Living document. Records decisions and how information flows.

## Goal

A web *test fit* platform: place masses on sites (imported from DXF), with movable parameters and rules.
**One core app, many typologies**: industrial is the first; retail (Plaza-Practiko), residential or a blank
project share the same core. The engine must be callable by the UI, by an MCP server and by multi-objective
optimizers.

## Decisions

| Date | Topic | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-13 | Stack | React + Vite + TypeScript + Zustand + Tailwind + Vitest (same as Plaza-Practiko) |
| 2026-10-13 | Site | Imported from DXF (closed polylines). Topography (contours) comes later |
| 2026-10-13 | Language | UI, code, comments and docs in English |
| 2026-10-13 | Units | Metric (m, m²) |
| 2026-10-13 | Repo layout | The app lives at the repo root (no `app/` subfolder) |
| 2026-10-14 | Priority | Site massing before circulation; circulation is derived from masses, then optimized |
| 2026-10-14 | Contract | zod schemas are the single source: TS types, runtime validation and JSON Schema (for MCP) |
| 2026-10-14 | Platform | One core app + typology templates, not one app per project type. A typology = preset programs + site rules (+ later, typology-specific engines). Blank = from scratch |
| 2026-10-14 | Geometry | `Contour` from Plaza-Practiko (vertices with corner radius + arc edges by bulge) for sites and free footprints. Footprint = `rect` (center, width, depth, rotation°) or `contour` |
| 2026-10-14 | Reuse | Features from Plaza-Practiko are **ported** (translated to English and our types, with their tests), not copy-pasted. Bottom-up: geometry → action → view |
| 2026-10-14 | Setback | Inward offsets subtract edge strips (+ disks at concave corners) instead of moving edges: no flipped ghosts on narrow sites; concave corners get a true-distance fillet |
| 2026-10-14 | Program kinds | (Phase 3) Programs become a zod discriminated union on `kind` (`warehouse`, `parking`, `free`…): each kind carries its own default `params` and selects which engine `derive()` runs for its masses. A mass may override them (`Partial` params). Same idea as Plaza-Practiko's `modo`, but typed per kind. Not added before an engine reads it: 3.0 needed only a size per program (`Program.size`), so `kind` waits for the first engine that runs per kind (parking and truck courts, Phase 4) |
| 2026-10-14 | Browser save | (Phase 9) The current `Project` (never `Derived`) is also saved in browser storage, so closing the tab does not lose work. A toggle picks the mode: **autosave** (after each change) or **manual** (only on Ctrl+S, which overrides the browser's "save page"). Exporting a `.gft` file through `io/` stays the way to keep or share a project |
| 2026-10-14 | Snap | Object snap ported from Plaza-Practiko's *imán*. Moving a mass tries, in Plaza's order: a **point pair** (own endpoint / midpoint / center onto another's, or onto an intersection) → **walls** onto parallel walls → the **grid** (the first corner lands on whole metres). Shift = free. Apertures are screen px (12 points, 8 walls), not metres: Plaza's fixed 0.5 m is invisible at site scale. Perpendicular, nearest, quadrant, ortho and tracking come with 1.3d |
| 2026-10-14 | Alignment | Unlike Plaza (only walls *in front*), a dragged mass's walls stick to the **line** of any parallel wall nearby: other masses, the site and the **setback line**. Warehouse fronts line up across a yard; a dashed guide shows what aligned |
| 2026-10-14 | Snap settings | Per-browser UI state (`localStorage`, key `gfit.snap`), never in the `Project` or the undo history. F3 = snap, F8 = ortho, F9 = grid, as in AutoCAD |
| 2026-10-14 | Corner snap | A dragged corner resolves like Plaza's `resolverIman`: point → perpendicular (from the previous corner) → ortho → tracking (the mass's axes through its corners, wall extensions) → nearest → grid. **Ortho starts from the corner's own start** (it moves straight, as AutoCAD's grip stretch), not from the previous corner as in Plaza: otherwise a corner level with its neighbour could never move up or down. Drawing tools will pass the previous point instead. World axes only: Plaza's rotated work plane, acquired points and fixed angle wait until a site or a tool needs them |
| 2026-10-14 | Shape editing | Port of Plaza-Practiko `edicion.ts` + `muros.ts` (`engine/geom/edit.ts`, `state/actions/shape.ts`). A **rect stays a rect** when a wall or corner is dragged (it resizes, the opposite side stays): its five numbers stay easy to optimize. Adding, deleting or rounding a corner turns it into a free `contour`. Left out: Plaza's fixed radii (every radius shrinks to fit) and shared walls (warehouses stand apart) |
| 2026-10-14 | Drag | One generic drag (`views/plan/useDrag.ts`, port of `useArrastre`) for the mass body, its walls and its corners; `views/plan/editing.ts` says what each one does. A rejected edit returns `null` to `store.preview`: the view stays at the last valid shape and the handle turns red |
| 2026-10-14 | Name + file | The app is **GFit**. Exported projects are `.gft` files: JSON text inside, with a `format: 'gfit'` header (added in `io/`) next to the `Project`'s `version`. Load = check the header, then validate with zod. The extension is only a hint; the header and zod are the real check |
| 2026-10-14 | Program graph | (Phase 3) A **graph mode** in the core, so every typology has it. Node = a mass (its program is the node's use); edge = a `Relation` in `Project.relations`. The graph is a **view of the Project, never a second model**: nothing to keep in sync. Graph edits call the same pure actions as the plan, and those verbs are the MCP tools. It comes before circulation because it is massing. See *Program graph* |
| 2026-10-14 | UI port | Practiko's UI and export modes are **ported as they are** (top bar, 3D, KPI strip, toolkit, menus, PDF, presentation, `.3dm`, viewer), translated to English and our tokens. We stop to design only at the steps marked **⏸ Layout**: where buttons go for what Practiko does not have (graph mode, typology controls). The UI frame (Phase 2) comes before the graph, so later phases build their buttons into the final frame |
| 2026-10-14 | KPI strip | (Phase 2) Like the top strip of Practiko and IZA (buildable area, coverage…), but **reconfigurable**: each metric is an entry in a registry (id, label, unit, how to read it from `Derived`). The user picks which KPIs the strip shows and drags them into order; the typology gives the default set. The choice is saved **in the `Project`** (an ordered list of metric ids, never the values), unlike snap settings: it is a decision about what the client sees, so it travels in the `.gft` and the client viewer link (10.2) shows the same strip |
| 2026-10-14 | Analysis tools | (Phase 6) Tools that measure what the geometry does: clearances, routes, vehicle fit, safety, visibility. They are **deterministic and pure** (`engine/analysis/`): the LLM and MCP call them and explain the result, never compute it. Each returns KPIs (they join the KPI registry, so the strip can show them), findings (an Issue + a fix) and overlays. Thresholds (vehicles, widths, radii, distances) are **editable parameters** (`Project.standards`), read by the code, never pasted into prompts: norms change with the site and the client. No external data APIs for now (little value). team_03 *Spatial Flow* (AIA26) and MELI are **ideas only, never ported**: unlike Practiko, their code is not a reference |
| 2026-10-14 | Workspaces | (Phase 2 frame) One workspace per design stage, from macro to micro and back: Portfolio → Site → Brief → Graph → Massing → Analysis. All of them edit the same `Project`, so going back to any stage updates everything downstream. Separate from the Plan · 3D · Split switch. See *Workspaces* |
| 2026-10-14 | Portfolio | (Phase 9) Portfolio → project (one parcel + one typology option) → versions (history, as in Practiko). Projects are georeferenced and shown on a map and in a same-scale lineup, with KPI snapshots kept outside the `Project`. Parcel selector, and an LLM assistant over the portfolio's KPIs. OSMnx-data-scraper is an idea source only. See *Workspaces* |
| 2026-10-14 | Interiors | Out of scope until the massing phases are done; then an interiors configurator (Phase 11), the most micro workspace |
| 2026-10-14 | Portfolio storage | (Phase 9) **One designer at a time** for now: the library lives in the browser (IndexedDB) and is exported as files. **Ready for a shared team library later (Azure)**: the views only talk to a `Library` interface (list, load, save version, delete), never to IndexedDB, so an Azure implementation can replace it; ids are globally unique (projects made apart can be merged); versions are append-only with date and author. Even when shared, a project is edited by one designer at a time (a lock, not live co-editing) |
| 2026-10-14 | Units and currency | Refines *Units*. Defaults: **metres and USD**. Later each project picks its display units (m / ft) and currency. Values are always stored in metres; conversion happens only at the edge (input fields and labels in `views/`), never in `engine/` or in saved files. Money is stored in the project's currency; the portfolio compares currencies with a rate the user enters (no live rates) |
| 2026-10-14 | State graph | A **dev-only** page (`/state-graph`, not in the production build) shows the modules and, live, each store call: the verb, its args, what changed, `derive()` time, checks. Views call verbs only through `state/actions/index.ts`, where `traced()` wraps each module: same args and result, plus one entry in the store call's frame (`state/trace/calls.ts`). Wrapping instead of action objects keeps `apply((p) => …)` as it is (`addMass` returns an id, `moveWall` how far it went) and costs nothing in production. A test fails if a view imports an action module directly or a module is not registered. The page is a second Vite entry (`state-graph.html`, served at `/state-graph` by a dev-only middleware; the build only takes `index.html`) drawn with React Flow (`@xyflow/react`, a devDependency). Its flow colours are tokens (`--flow-call`, `--flow-engine`, `--flow-data`, `--flow-reject`): informational, like program colours. Its log is kept in `sessionStorage`, so a reload (Vite reloads when a file is deleted) does not lose the trace |
| 2026-10-14 | Layout | (2.0, agreed) Practiko's frame: **top bar · KPI strip · Parameters · viewport · Summary** (two side panels: see *Side panels*). Top bar: left the Parameters toggle · `GFit` · project name (opens its data) · ⋯ (files; State graph in dev) · later Versions and Compare; **centre: the workspace switch** (Site · Brief · Graph · Massing · Analysis, read left to right as the process; one not built yet shows disabled, with its phase); right: undo / redo · Export ▾ (full screen inside) · the Summary toggle. **Plan · 3D · Split moves into the viewport** (top centre): only the plan workspaces have it (Brief is a table). Floating on the viewport, as in Practiko: program palette top left, selection card top right, tools rail on the left, command line + snap bar + measures bottom left, warnings chip bottom right. Practiko's calculator button becomes the Brief workspace |
| 2026-10-14 | Graph workspace | (2.0) The program graph is a **workspace** in the switch, not a toggle over Massing: the plan with the bubble diagram on top, graph tools in the rail, relations in the side panel |
| 2026-10-14 | Side panels | (2.0, revised the same day at the user's request; the Summary's tabs: *Summary tabs*) **Two panels, inputs left and outputs right**, so the layout mirrors the data flow and the figures change in view while a parameter is edited: **Parameters** (left; fixed tabs Program · Site, Circulation (4) and Standards (6) join) and **Summary** (right; figures and issues, the dashboard of 2.3). Practiko has one panel on the right; we add the left one. Each panel **hides** with a toggle at its own edge of the top bar (or its header button) and comes back as it was: hidden, it stays mounted (its tab, its drafts); which panels show and the Parameters tab are remembered in the browser. A workspace only picks which Parameters tab opens. Below 880 px the same toggles open each panel as a sheet over the viewport, from its own side (from below on a phone) |
| 2026-10-14 | Typology in the UI | (2.0) Chosen in ⋯ → New project and shown in the project's data (click its name). No typology button: what is typology-specific is data inside the tabs (its programs, its standards) |
| 2026-10-14 | Open | (2.1b) Opening a project (a `.gft`, ⋯ → New project, the demo) goes through a store door, `open(p)`: a fresh history, nothing selected, and the plan fits the new site (an `opened` counter, as Practiko's `aperturas`). Replacing a project with changes asks first, inline (Cancel / Continue), never `window.confirm`; a file that fails the header or zod changes nothing and the notice says why. Renaming is a verb (`renameProject`), so it is one undo step |
| 2026-10-14 | 3D view | (2.2) Port of Practiko's `vistas/tresd` (`views/scene/`): masses extruded by `height` from the ground (a mass whose program is not built is a 0.15 m slab), the site boundary and the dashed buildable line on the ground, three-quarter framing, orbit controls, IZA's lights and shadows. It reads the same store as the plan, so **the selection is shared**. three.js loads **lazily** in its own chunk (only when 3D or Split opens; a failed download says so in place, with Retry). three cannot read CSS variables: the scene reads its colours from `--scene-*` tokens at runtime, never hex in components. The camera frames by itself only when another project opens, the site changes or the first mass appears; the plan and the 3D both frame again when their shape changes by more than 25 % (Plan → Split, a panel hidden) |
| 2026-10-14 | .3dm export | (2.2) Port of Practiko's `exportar3dm` (`io/export3dm.ts`): each footprint as an exact curve (rounded corners and arc edges are arcs) in `GFit::<Program>`, its **extrusion** by height in `GFit::<Program>::Volumes`, the site in `Site` and the buildable line in `Site::Buildable`; User Text `gfit.program` and `gfit.mass`, object name = mass name, metres. rhino3dm (WebAssembly) loads from its CDN only when exporting; Node (tests) uses the npm package. Export ▾ also saves a PNG of the 3D view |
| 2026-10-14 | Stats | (2.3) The KPI registry is **engine code** (`engine/kpis.ts`: id, label, unit, `read(project, derived)`, optional limit and status), so MCP lists and reads the same figures the strip shows. Static KPIs (site, buildable, built footprint, coverage, open area, built volume, buildings, issues) + one `program:<id>` footprint per program. `Project.kpis` is **optional** (absent = `DEFAULT_KPIS`, so old files stay valid); each typology has its own `kpis`, copied by `newProject`. Choosing and ordering are verbs (`showKpi`, `hideKpi`, `moveKpi`, `setKpis`): one undo step, saved in the `.gft`. Every figure goes through `views/format.ts` (m², m³, %, compact USD). The Summary panel is the dashboard (site figures, footprint bars by program, issues); a **warnings chip** bottom right lists the issues and *goes to* one: selects its mass and points the plan and the 3D at it without changing the zoom, with a pulsing mark (port of Practiko's `irA`) |
| 2026-10-14 | Toolkit | (2.4) Ports of Practiko's tools, on the world axes. **Tools rail** (left of the viewport, with the plan): Select (V) · Mass (B) · Corner (Q) · Dimension (D); a tool's key again goes back to Select; a tool owns the plan's presses through a transparent capture surface, so masses do not move under it. **Mass**: a drag makes an axis-aligned `rect` footprint (five numbers, easy to optimize), clicks make a `contour` (Enter / double click closes; Backspace or Ctrl+Z removes a point; Escape cancels, then leaves); every point goes through the snap from the previous point; the new mass has no program and stays selected; a crossing outline is refused (Plaza's curve following and self-crossing cleanup are left out). **Corner**: pick a corner, the pointer gives the radius (0.1 m steps; Shift free). **Dimension**: two points and an offset, saved in `Project.dimensions` (optional), selectable with Select, Delete removes; "Dims" shows or hides them (per browser). **Ruler** (M): temporary, never saved. **Command line** (above the snap bar while a tool places points): `x,y`, `#x,y`, `@x,y`, `d`, `d<a`, `<a`, `LxW`; a digit typed while drawing goes to it. **Copy / paste / duplicate** (Ctrl+C / V / D): an in-tab clipboard; paste centres the copy on the pointer on whole metres; duplicate puts it to the right at the site's minimum spacing, so it does not start as an overlap. **Program palette** (top left): drag a program onto a mass in the plan or the 3D (a ray finds the mass), or click it with a mass selected. New masses take the first free name |
| 2026-10-14 | UI state | Workspace, view, panels and the Parameters tab live in a small store of their own (`views/layout/ui.ts`), like the snap settings: never in the `Project`, the undo history or the store doors the state graph traces. The panels shown and the Parameters tab persist in `localStorage` (`gfit.layout`) |
| 2026-10-14 | Layout by function | (2.5, agreed; revises *Layout* and *Toolkit*) The controls are grouped by what they do, as design software does, instead of floating where each one arrived. **Programs** (Parameters → Program): the palette is gone, each program's row is dragged onto a mass (plan or 3D) or, with a mass selected, clicked to give it that program; **+ Add** stays. **Properties** (the selection card): the lower part of the Parameters panel, below a divider that drags (Revit's Properties palette); hiding the panel hides it too. Below 880 px, where the panels are sheets, it stays a card over the viewport. **Tools rail**: icon over name, in groups: Select · Mass, Corner (shape) · Dimension, Ruler (measure); the Ruler leaves the bottom row because it is a tool. **Drafting bar**: a docked bar under the viewport (only the centre column), in the panels' paper, never black: command line · Snap + its kinds as icons, Align, Ortho, Grid · Dims, Fit (AutoCAD's and Rhino's status bar). On is the segmented control's white pill, off is grey ink. It stays in 3D, with what only the plan uses disabled, so the frame does not jump |
| 2026-10-14 | Associative dimensions | (2.6) Port of Practiko's anchored dimensions (`estado/acotacionesAsociativas.ts`). A dimension end placed on a mass corner (within 1 mm; the Endpoint snap puts it exactly there) is **anchored** to it: `anchorA` / `anchorB` = `{ massId, vertex }`, optional, so old files stay valid. A corner shared by two masses anchors to the mass of the other end. The **store** runs `followAnchors(before, after)` (`state/dimensionAnchors.ts`, pure) after `apply` and `preview`, so every verb gets it for free and the dimension follows while a mass is dragged: same index while the mass keeps its corner count (move, resize, rotate, rect → free shape), the corner still in its place when one is added or deleted elsewhere; its corner or mass deleted, the end lets go where it was. `a` and `b` are always kept up to date, so drawing never reads anchors. Practiko's lock and typed length arrive with 2.7 (`state/settle.ts` runs both on every edit); midpoints and centres do not anchor |
| 2026-10-14 | Locks, typed measures, levels | (agreed, revises the 3D plan) Order: locks → typing while dragging → levels. **No editor in 3D** for now: as in Practiko, the 3D shows and selects, the plan edits (Split shows both live). **Locks**: a dimension's (port: keeps its length, a moved mass drags the other along, an edit that breaks it is refused with a notice) and, new, a mass's (its geometry cannot change). **Typed measures**: a dimension's length (port: typing it moves the geometry) and, new, a typed distance while dragging a mass, a wall or a corner. **Levels** (2.9, port of Practiko's `Nivel`): a rail top right of the viewport (or IZA's vertical slider), highest on top, *All* above; a **Levels** tab in Parameters (name, elevation, height, ×N); a level **repeats ×N** as a typical floor (3D and floor area multiply it); the plan shows the levels below faded and a dashed outline of the level above or below (a toggle, as Practiko's *Ref.*) |
| 2026-10-14 | Typed edits | (2.8, agreed) AutoCAD's grips and SketchUp's Measurements box. With the Select tool, **whenever something is selected the command line is its edit** and digits go to it (so `3` switches to 3D only with nothing selected): a mass → *Move* (`d` along the drag, along its main axis with Ortho; `@x,y`; `d<a`; `#x,y` = where its centre goes), a corner → *Move corner* (the same, from where it was), a wall just dragged → *Move wall* (how far along its normal), a dimension → its length. Typed **during** a drag, the value replaces the drag and ends it; typed **just after**, it replaces it too, as one undo step (the drag is undone and the exact edit applied); anything else done in between ends that. A typed value is exact (no snap) and goes through `settle`, so locks hold. After it applies, the field lets go of the focus (`oneShot`), so Ctrl+Z and Delete are the app's again. The drags tell the command line what they move (`views/plan/command/editCommand.ts`) |
| 2026-10-14 | Levels | (2.9, agreed; port of Practiko's `Nivel`) **Model** (`version` 2; a v1 file opens with one level, *L1* at 0, and everything on it): `Level { id, name, elevation, height, repeat }`, `Mass.levelId`, `Dimension.levelId`. `repeat` (×N) makes it a **typical floor**: N identical floors stacked, so the 3D draws N copies and floor area and volume count N times. A mass with no `height` of its own takes its level's (as Practiko's `altura: null`): a new mass does, and follows the level when its height changes. Changing a level's height or ×N moves the levels above it by the difference; its elevation is typed freely. Names: *L1, L2…* up, *B1, B2…* (basements) down. **Engine**: overlap and spacing are checked within a level; the setback, on every level. Coverage is the projection of the levels above ground (a level whose top is at or below 0 is a basement); GFA (a new KPI) is each level's built floor area × its ×N. **View** (state, not in the file): the active level and *All*; drawing, editing, snaps and dimensions belong to the active level; selecting a mass on another level goes to it; *All* only shows (a click picks a mass and goes to its level; a drawing tool goes back to the active level). The active level's masses sit on paper, so the levels below show only around them, fainter the farther down (Practiko's `lejania`). **Rail** (top right of the viewport): Practiko's list of levels, highest on top, *All* above with 2+ levels, **plus a mini-section at elevation scale beside it** (IZA's rail as an indicator: each level's span, ×N included, the active one marked, ground at 0); *Ref.* toggles the dashed outline of the level above or below. A **Levels** tab in Parameters: + above / + below, name, elevation, height, ×N, area; a level with masses, or the only one, cannot be removed. Properties: copy a mass to the level above or below (port of `duplicarMasa`: the first level where it overlaps nothing, or a new one) |
| 2026-10-15 | Buildings and options | (Phase B, agreed; revises *Levels* and *Portfolio*) Project-wide levels were a mistake: two separate buildings on one site do not share floors. **Levels belong to a building**: `Building { id, name, levels[], levelHeight, color, context }`, and a mass still points only to its `levelId` (level ids are unique in the project, so the level says the building: one source of truth, nothing to keep in sync). A new mass goes to the **active building**, on its active level; a new project starts with *Building 1*. A building is a named group, like a layer (*Building 1*, *Train station*, *Context*), not a construction phase. **Context** (a switch): the building is drawn but left out of KPIs, its own checks and analysis; design masses are still checked against it (an overlap with the station is an issue). **Color** never repaints a building's masses by default: programs keep their colours, so they read across buildings; a view toggle *Color by: Program · Building* uses the building's colour (an informational token, like program colours). `levelHeight` is the height a new level of that building takes. Cost and rent per building wait for the Brief (3.0): not added before something reads them. **Design options**: `Option { id, name, buildingIds }`. A building in two options is **the same building** (linked): editing it in one changes it in both; *Make unique* copies it so it stops being shared. The plan and 3D show only the active option (other options hidden); which option and building are active is view state, as the active level. Overlap, spacing, coverage, GFA and the KPI strip are computed **per option** (two options overlap on the site on purpose), and the Summary breaks them down by building. Options live inside one `.gft`; several projects on one parcel are still possible (another typology). Hiding a building is view state and only hides it; counting is the *Context* switch. **Hierarchy** (the Buildings panel is a tree): **option** (parent) → **building** (child, the layer) → **mass** (grandchild, shown with its level). A shared building shows under each of its options, marked as linked. **Assign**: with masses selected, the panel gives them an option and a building (the building joins that option if it was not in it); nothing else assigns. Named *Phase B* so the later phases keep their numbers |
| 2026-10-15 | Multi-selection | (B.0) Shift+click adds or removes a mass; with the Select tool, a drag on empty ground draws a window: left → right selects what is fully inside, right → left what it touches (AutoCAD). The selection is only a selector: it never assigns, groups or saves anything (it is view state, not in the `Project`); grouping is the Buildings panel's *Assign*. It is a list of masses (a corner only when one mass is selected), across the buildings of the active option: the masses of the active level and of the option's other buildings (B.3); one of another level of the active building, or of another option, is picked alone. Dragging one moves them all (the snap works from the one grabbed; a click without moving picks it alone), Delete / R (around the centre of their bounds) / copy / paste / duplicate / typed move take them all, one undo step; Ctrl+A selects the level. Properties shows the values they share (mixed = —) and edits them all. Group verbs: `moveMasses`, `removeMasses`, `rotateMasses`, `updateMasses`, `lockMasses`. **The pan moves**: with a window on the left button, the plan pans with the middle button, Space + drag or a finger (touch has no window) |
| 2026-10-15 | Options in the engine | (B.1) `derive(project, option?)` measures and checks **every** option (`Derived.options`); `metrics` and `issues` are the asked one's (the store passes its active `option`, view state like the level; absent or unknown: the first), so the KPI strip, the Summary and the warnings follow the active option without knowing about options. `Derived.buildings` holds each building's own figures (footprint, floor area per level, GFA, volume), context ones too. Each `DerivedMass` carries its `buildingId`, `context` and `span` (its stack's bottom and top, ×N included). **Clash rule**: in one building, masses of the same level (a level over another is its floors); between two buildings, masses whose spans share some height; two context masses never. Built volume, built masses and buildings are now metrics, so a KPI never reads the whole project: `buildings` counts the option's buildings with a built mass (it counted masses before), a new `masses` KPI counts masses. Level ids stay unique in the project, so the active level says the active building: the rail, the Levels tab, the faded levels and *Ref.* read its levels (`buildingLevels`). Level verbs take the building last and optional (absent: the first), as `addMass` takes its level. *Assign* puts a mass on the target's level with the same name, else the same elevation, else a copy of the level it leaves, so the mass stays where it was in height. Building verbs are `state/actions/buildings.ts` |
| 2026-10-15 | Buildings layout | (B.2, agreed) **Option switch**: in the top bar, right of the project name (`Option 1 ▾`): it changes the KPIs, the Summary and what shows, so it belongs to the whole project; its menu lists the options (the active one marked), then *New option*, *Duplicate (linked)*, *Rename*, *Delete* and *Compare…* (B.5). Versions join it there later. **Buildings tab** (Parameters: Program · Buildings · Site; it replaces Levels): on top *Color by: Program · Building* and, with masses selected, the *Assign* bar (`Assign N selected → Option ▾ · Building ▾ (+ New building) · Assign`); then the tree **option → building → mass** (a mass with its level: `Warehouse 1 · L1`); a building row has its colour, name, 🔗 when 2+ options show it, ⓒ when it is context, and an eye to hide it; *+ Building* · *+ Option* under it. A click on an option makes it active; on a building, the active building (the plan goes to its ground floor); on a mass, selects it. | 2026-10-15 | Relations | **Replaced by *Rules* (3.5): relations are no longer drawn by hand between masses; the brief's rules between programs make them.** (3.1, agreed with the user: three rules and a link, `aligned` later) `Relation` is a zod union on `kind` with `id`, `a`, `b`, `weight` (default 1) and its own parameter, flat: `adjacent.minShared`, `near.max`, `apart.min`; `attached` has none (it passes A's move on to B, 3.3, instead of storing an offset). `Project.relations` is **optional** (absent = none), so files stay version 3; the schema checks unique ids, ends that are masses, and two different ones. **Measured in plan**, on the footprints (`engine/graph/relations.ts`): the shared wall is the overlap of edges on one line within 5 cm; the distance is 0 when they touch or overlap. A broken rule is a **warning** Issue (`relation`) about both masses, in each option that shows both; `Derived.relations` has every result (met, value, gap). An **adjacent or attached pair is one building**: `minSpacing` does not apply between them (overlap still does). Verbs (`state/actions/graph.ts`): `addRelation` (no twin of the same kind and pair; attached is directed), `updateRelation` (another kind starts from its defaults), `removeRelation`; deleting masses or a building with its masses drops their relations. Copies (paste, *Make unique*) do not carry relations |
| 2026-10-15 | Graph mode | **Replaced by *Graph mode, abstract* (3.5): the bubbles over the plan gave way to an abstract diagram.** (3.2, agreed with the user: bubbles over the plan) The **Graph workspace** keeps the plan (the view stays on it: 3D and Split are off) with the masses faded, without their labels and handles, and draws the program graph over it (`views/graph/GraphLayer.tsx`): a **bubble** per mass of the active option (hidden buildings left out) at its centroid, about half as wide as a circle of its area (never under a finger's size), in its program's colour (context grey, dashed), named under it; a **line** per relation, green when met, red when broken, dashed for a link, with what it asks at its middle (*near ≤ 50 m*). The **tools bar** swaps the plan's tools for the graph's (`GraphTools.tsx`): **Select** (V: a bubble picks and drags its mass, as in the plan; a line picks its relation) · **Node** (N: a click adds a mass of the chosen program at its size, picked) · **Relation** (L: drag from a bubble to another, or click one then the other; a release elsewhere or Escape lets it go) · the relation's kind (*Adjacent · Near · Apart · Attached*; picking one takes the Relation tool) · the node's program · **From brief** (the masses the targets still ask for, at the programs' sizes, each in free buildable ground of the active level, nearest the middle first, `engine/graph/place.ts`; one undo step; a notice says how many, and how many found no room). **Properties** shows a picked relation (`RelationProperties.tsx`): its masses, met or broken by how much, its kind, its parameter, its weight and *Delete* (also the Delete key; the masses stay). One thing is picked at a time: a mass lets go of the relation and the other way round. The graph's UI state (tool, kind, program, picked relation, the line being drawn) is `graphState.ts`, never in the Project |
| 2026-10-15 | Links | **Kept, as the *moves with* rule (3.5, Decision *Rules*): the followers come from the rules, not from relations drawn by hand.** (3.3) `attached` B follows A, **in the verbs**, so the plan, the graph, a typed move, a dimension that moves a mass and MCP all get it: `moveMass` / `moveMasses` move the masses with their followers (`engine/graph/links.ts` `withFollowers`: link after link, each once, a locked follower left where it is), and `rotateMass` / `rotateMasses` turn the followers around the same point as A (its centre, or the group's). Moving B alone leaves A; a change of A's shape (a wall, a corner) does not move B. **Adding a link snaps B to A** at once (`snapTo`): across the wall of A it is most outside of until it touches its line, then along it until they share some of it (a locked B stays). A pair that is attached is one building (no minSpacing between them, Decision *Relations*). While a mass is dragged, its followers are not snap targets |
| 2026-10-15 | Reorganize | **Renamed *Arrange* (3.5, Decision *Rules*), on the rules' relations; who moves changed with *Solve* (3.6): the smaller of two, not half and half.** (3.4) `engine/graph/reorganize.ts`, pure and deterministic (no randomness; the same project gives the same answer), the seed of the Phase 8 optimizer. It **only slides** an option's masses, from where they are, in rounds: a broken `near` pulls the pair together, a broken `apart` pushes it away, a broken `adjacent` brings B against A's nearest wall (`snapTo`); two built masses that clash and are closer than `minSpacing` (and are not one building) are pushed apart; a built mass with corners outside the buildable area is pushed back in, towards its middle. Each pair shares a correction half and half; a **locked or context** mass takes none (the other takes all of it). A relation's **weight** scales its pull. An **attached group moves as one** (its leader is the top of its chain of links; if the leader cannot move, nor can the group). Each correction asks 5 cm more than is missing, so the pass ends with the rules met rather than creeping towards them; it stops when nothing asks to move, when nothing moves any more (rules that cannot all be met) or after 200 rounds. Positions are rounded to the centimetre; nothing moved, the same project comes back. The Graph's **Reorganize** button runs it as one undo step and says how many rules are met, before and after |
| 2026-10-15 | Rules | (3.5, asked by the user: "the graph's relations should be made automatically from the rules… the rules are set in the brief, the graph makes them on the elements and checks them, the massing shows them, and if the massing changes the rules update"; MELI's list of rules, required flag and reason as ideas) **Rules live in the brief, between programs** (`Project.rules`, optional): a sentence each — *Office must be adjacent to Warehouse*, *Outdoor storage within 40 m of Warehouse*, *Guard house at least 30 m from Warehouse*, *Office moves with Warehouse* — with a `weight`, `required` (broken, an error; else a warning) and a `reason` (why, in plain words). **The graph derives the masses' relations** (`engine/graph/relations.ts` `applyRules`, in every option, on its design masses): each mass of the first program with its **nearest** mass of the second (adjacent, near, moves with), or with **every** one (apart); a program with itself makes one relation per pair. Nothing about them is stored: moving a mass finds the pairs again. They are measured as before (shared wall; gap) and broken ones are Issues in a sentence. **They act on the geometry**: *moves with* passes a leader's moves on to its followers (the move and rotate verbs); **Arrange** (`engine/graph/arrange.ts`) slides the masses to meet them; **+ Node** and **From brief** place each new mass where its rules ask (`placeByRules`: against a wall of a mass it must be adjacent to, within reach of one it must be near, far from the ones it must keep apart from). **The massing shows them**: arcs over the plan (drafting bar *Rules*, on by default), green or red (3.8: only the broken ones by default, and chips where they break, Decision *Issues on the massing*). Industrial starts with three example rules (adjacent, near, apart); the demo's office breaks the first on purpose (a warning) so the graph has something to show. Editing: the Brief's *Rules* list (selects for the programs and the kind, the distance, *Required*, *Why*, how its masses stand, ×, *+ Rule*), or the Graph's Programs level (3.7: the list moved under the graph, Decision *Rules under the graph*) |
| 2026-10-15 | Graph mode, abstract | **Its Masses level gave way to the graph over the geometry in Massing; the diagram of programs is the Rules step's (3.9, Decision *Steps*).** (3.5, asked by the user: "the graph should be abstract… a 3D view of the graph, or a section… the lines not only straight… easier to understand how to use") **The Graph workspace's plan is an abstract diagram** (`views/graph/Diagram.tsx`): no site, no rectangles. **Programs**: a bubble per program (sized by its target area) and a line per rule, green when all its masses meet it, red when some do not, grey with none yet; *Draw rule* (R) drags from one bubble to another to add a rule of the chosen kind. **Masses**: a bubble per design mass of the active option (sized by its area; a click picks the mass) and a line per relation, green or red, with what was measured. Bubbles are a white ring round the program's colour; a force layout places them (`diagram.ts`: pure and deterministic, from the masses' places on the site; a dragged bubble stays where it was put, in the diagram only; a double-click lets them all go). **Lines** are curved (default), straight or orthogonal (per browser), styled by kind (adjacent thick, near thin, apart dashed, moves with dotted) with a **legend**. **Diagram · 3D · Split**: the view switch keeps its places; in 3D the masses are ghosts and the graph floats over them (`scene/Graph3D.tsx`: a sphere per mass at its first floor's middle, tubes arching between, no depth test), so relations between levels read. **Properties**: a picked rule (its sentence, how its masses stand, its fields, Delete) or relation (its masses, the rule that made it, measured, *Edit the rule*). **Tools bar**: *Programs · Masses* · *Draw rule* [kind] or *+ Node* [program] · *From brief* · **Arrange** (A) · lines ▾ · **?** (3.6: *From brief* and *Arrange* became **▶ Solve**, Decision *Solve*). **How it works**: a card with the four steps (rules → the graph applies them → Arrange → the massing follows), open the first time, ? brings it back |
| 2026-10-15 | Solve | (3.6, asked by the user: "the arrange button should be more visible… one button like solve, with another tone or a play button" and "arrange always moves the geometry of smaller area and leaves the biggest still; with more than two the rule is the same; with several floors the areas add up, that is the dominant one") **▶ Solve** replaces *Arrange* and *From brief* in the Graph's tools bar: the one filled (ink) button, at the bar's end, with a play icon (S). It is the step from the brief and its rules to geometry (`state/actions/graph.ts` `solve`): the masses the brief still asks for are placed where their rules ask (`addFromBrief`, **the biggest programs first**, so the smaller ones are placed against them), then the option's masses slide to meet the rules (`arrange`); one undo step; a notice says what was placed, how many relations are met and what found no room. **Who moves** (`engine/graph/arrange.ts`): of the two ends of a correction, the **smaller takes all of it and the bigger stays**, so the dominant mass anchors the layout (no more half and half). **Size is floor area**: the footprint × its level's floors (×N); masses of one building on different levels that overlap in plan are a **stack**: one unit, moved as one, its floor areas summed. Equal sizes: the one drawn later moves. Locked and context masses still never move (the other end takes it). **Placement without a partner** (`placeByRules`): nearest the masses of its own program, or, the first of its program, nearest the buildable area's top-left corner (it packs from there; in the middle a first warehouse left no room for a second). `arrange` and `addFromBrief` stay verbs (MCP) |

Rules and graph go hand in hand, the graph changing each time you edit the rules… one under the other" and "brief generates program; when approved, it generates the graph and loose floating elements; rules adjust the graph's relations; the graph with rules generates geometry with arrange… for someone who has no idea what to build"; agreed: names stay *Brief · Graph · Massing*, graph above and rules below) **The pipeline**: *Brief* (the program table only, `views/brief/Brief.tsx`) → **Approve brief → Graph** (a filled button at its foot; no state is stored: it opens the Graph at the Masses level) → *Graph*: the diagram (or 3D, Split) **above** and the **rules list below** (`views/graph/Rules.tsx`, moved from the Brief), a row divider between them sharing the height (`graphState.share`, 0.2–0.8, default 0.62, per browser; double-click puts it back) → **▶ Solve** → *Massing*. **Rules and graph go together**: a click on a row picks its rule (its line thickens, Properties edits it; the row is marked) and a line picked in the diagram marks its row (scrolled into view); an edit redraws the diagram at once; *+ Rule* picks the new rule. **The brief's floating elements**: in Masses, each mass a program's target count still asks for floats as a **dashed, faded bubble** "to place" (`ghost:<program>:<n>`, at the program's size; no lines; derived, never stored), with a note that ▶ Solve places them; in Programs each bubble says *n of N placed*. | 2026-10-15 | Issues on the massing | (3.8, asked by the user: "in massing there should be a way to turn the relations, the issue curves, on or off; or when the relation is met, its curve goes out" and "massing is edited by hand and checks the rules, with warnings placed where the conflicts are, like chips beside the geometry; a click opens the message; and the rules can be edited again") **Chips where it breaks** (`views/plan/issueMarkers.ts`, pure, and `IssueMarkers.tsx`): one round chip per place on the active level: a mass's own issues at the top-right corner of its bounds; a pair's (a broken rule, an overlap, too close) at the middle of the arc the plan draws between them, so a broken rule's chip sits on its red arc. Its count (or !), solid red for an error, a red ring for a warning; the same size at any zoom; hidden while a drawing tool is active. A click (or Enter) opens its **messages** beside it (`IssuePopover`, over the plan): each issue's sentence, **Edit the rule** for a broken rule (the Graph, Programs level, the rule picked and its row marked in the list; `Issue.rule` carries its id) and **Select it / them**; ×, Escape or a press elsewhere closes it, and a chip whose issues are solved goes. **The arcs** (`RulesLayer.tsx`): the drafting bar's **Rules ▾** says which: *broken* (default: a rule that is met goes out), *all* or *off*; and **Issues** shows or hides the chips (both per browser in `gfit.graph`; the old on/off `overlay` reads as broken/off). With the chips off, a broken arc says what was measured at its middle. Also, so the graph reads in the shorter room over its rules: the diagram's text keeps its size against the real height, the layout spreads across a wide room (`layoutDiagram` `aspect`), a line's label moves along its line off names and other labels (`labelSpot`), and the legend is one low strip (each kind's meaning in its tooltip) |

| 2026-10-15 | Exterior circulation | (Phase 4, agreed with the user, who asked for roads they can edit and add, and for access points before any road; its docks' walls revised by *Docks turn with the building*; reference TestFit's industrial configurator, ideas only; the discussion is `docs/ideas/exterior-circulation.md`) **Order**: frontage (the boundary's stretches that face a public street) → existing roads inside the parcel (not buildable, usable by the network) → site gates → docks → derived roads → edited roads. **Gates** belong to the site (width, turning radius, flow in · out · both, vehicles, guard house, queue), placed by `t` on the boundary's perimeter as Practiko's `AccesoVehicular`; an option can have **its own** (*Own gates for <option>* copies the site's, *Use the site's* drops the copy), because the client may change their mind per option. **Docks**: a default per program (the first per-kind `params`, Decision *Program kinds*; warehouse: the long faces toward the court, a door per so many m or m²), a mass can override it with its own faces (`t` ranges on its perimeter). **Roads** belong to the option (they follow its masses): derived from gates, courts and parking, a ring road per building that can be switched off; a **manual road** is a polyline + width + vehicles (Practiko's `CalleCajones`); **dragging a derived road makes it manual** where it is dropped (*Reset* turns it back); removing one stores a point, not an index (Practiko's `sentidos.ts`), and stops applying when no derived road passes there. Derived, never stored: the network, truck courts, parking, and the Issues (a warehouse no gate reaches: the `access` relation; a gate that leads nowhere; a dead end; a road narrower than its vehicle). **Layout** (4.0, agreed): the Site step's tools bar adds *Frontage · Gate · Existing road*, and its panel an **Access** section (port Practiko's *Accesos desde la calle*: a row per frontage and gate; the gate's handle slides along the boundary, port `ManijaRampa` + `flechaDeAcceso`), whose header says whose gates they are (also in the option's tree menu). Massing's tools bar adds a **Road** group (by points, `@x,y`, `w` for the width); derived roads drawn lighter than manual ones; Properties of a road (width, vehicles; *Remove* · *Delete* · *Reset*), of a mass (*Docks*: its program's or *Own docks*, a click on a wall turns its docks on or off), of the active building (*Ring road*); a **Circulation** tab on the Parameters rail after Buildings (docks by program, road width and court depth defaults until 6.0's Standards, totals that can go to the KPI strip). Not chosen: gates edited in Massing; a Circulation workspace of its own. Phase 4 comes before Phase 5. **Model** (4.1): all optional, so no new version. An existing road is a centreline with a width (a closed polygon of the DXF waits for Phase 5); a mass's own docks are its docked walls, each stored as the `t` of the wall's middle (it keeps naming that wall when the outline is resized); a program's docks say their walls (*one long* · *both long* · *short* · *all* · *none*) and the doors (one per so many m of wall or m² of floor; the warehouse: one long wall, a door per 700 m²); the circulation's defaults (road width 8 m, court depth 40 m, ring clearance 7 m, a gate's 12 m and 15 m radius, the stall, parking on both sides) are `Project.standards`, the first of 6.0's, copied from the typology; gate ids are unique across the site's and the options' copies. **Site access** (4.2): *Frontage* is a press on the boundary dragged along it (or two clicks), the shorter way between the ends, which stick to its corners; a picked frontage's round ends slide along the boundary. *Gate* places one with a click on a frontage (elsewhere a notice says where gates go); its square handle slides along its frontage and stops half an opening from the ends. A gate is drawn as its lane in (as long as its queue, at least a road's width; Practiko's `filaDeAcceso`), its posts, its guard house and its arrow (in · out · both). An existing road is drawn as the street's paving with a dashed axis and leaves the buildable area with the setback around it. The Access section's rows and the plan pick each other; the picked row opens its fields; Delete removes what is picked, Escape lets it go. A gate off every frontage warns (code `gate`), when the site has frontage. Massing draws the access under the masses, without handles. **Docks** (4.3): a design mass that is built and stands on the ground docks on its own walls or its program's: the long walls come from the footprint's minimum-area rectangle (port of Practiko's `rectangulo.ts`), *one long* takes the side with the most room in front (to the next mass or the boundary); a wall under 8 m takes none. Doors stand at least 4 m from a wall's ends and 4 m apart: by length, one every so many m, centred; by area, the doors the floor asks spread over the docked walls by their length; a mass with its own docks whose program has none gets one every 12 m. A truck court as deep as the standards say stands in front of each docked wall; one that runs into another built mass or out of the site warns (code `court`). The plan draws the courts under the masses and the docked walls over them, a tick per door; *Own docks* (Properties) starts from what the mass has, then the marks beside its walls (drawn over the wall handles, clear of them) turn each wall's docks on or off. The Circulation tab: docks by program (walls, doors per m² or m, how many), the defaults (road width, court depth, ring clearance, a gate's width and radius) and the totals (dock doors, truck courts). **Derived network** (4.4; its rings and joins revised by *Circulation stress pass*), found again on every change (about 2 ms on the demo): (1) each building's *ring road*, unless the option switched it off: every grounded mass's minimum rectangle grown past its court where a docked wall faces that side and by the ring clearance elsewhere, plus half a road; the building's rectangles merged and kept half a road inside the site; each ring's sides are its roads, so one side can be removed alone. (2) *Links*: a court no road passes gets one from its mouth, a gate whose lane meets no road gets one from its lane's end (with no road at all, to a court's mouth); straight ahead when the way is clear, else the shortest way around the masses (a visibility graph over their rectangles grown by half a road and a metre, inside the site). (3) *Joins*: a part of the network no gate reaches is joined to the nearest part one does. A removed road is a point kept while a derived road passes there. Then the `access` relation: a mass with docks is reached when a truck gate (trucks or all, in or both) is on the part of the network that serves its court; the Issues: no gate yet, no gate lets trucks in, a gate that leads nowhere, a mass no truck gate reaches (code `access`), and for a road drawn by hand: narrower than its vehicles (trucks 7 m, cars 5.5 m), over a mass, a dead end (code `road`). A warning about a place (a gate, a dead end) has its chip there. On the plan the roads lie over the courts and under the masses, derived ones lighter; a click picks one: Properties shows its name, width and length and *Remove* (Delete too); the Circulation tab adds the road area and *Restore removed roads*; the building's Properties have *Ring road* (per option). The demo gets a south street (15 m) and a gate between the warehouses, so it is reached from the start. **Roads by hand** (4.5): the *Road* tool (W), the last group of Massing's tools bar, draws a centreline point by point like the Existing road tool (Enter or a double click ends it; the command line takes points and `w 10` for the width) at the standards' width; the road is picked and the tool stays on. A road drawn by hand is dragged whole or by its points (handles when picked); its width and vehicles are in Properties, with *Delete*. A derived road dragged becomes a road drawn by hand where it is dropped, its derived one kept out by a point (one undo step), and *Reset* brings that one back. A drag whose element leaves the plan (the derived road is replaced mid-drag) is finished by the window's pointer events. **Car parking** (4.6; port of Practiko's `calles.ts` and `cajonesDeTramo`): along every road cars use (not one for trucks alone, not an existing street), split into runs at bends over 20°, a row of stalls at right angles on one side (a ring's inside, toward its building) or both, from the road's edge outwards; a stall stays only whole inside the site and clear of the masses with a 1.5 m sidewalk, the courts, the gates' lanes, the roads and the other stalls (a separating axis test on convex shapes and a grid, where Practiko used booleans: the demo's 960 stalls derive in about 5 ms). The Circulation tab sets it (both sides · one side · none, the stall's width and depth) and counts the stalls; its four totals are KPIs the strip can show (`dock-doors`, `truck-courts`, `road-area`, `stalls`) *PE.2: the frontage is gone (Decision *No frontage*): any street is a street the site faces, a saved frontage is an existing street, and a gate stands on the boundary or on either side of any street (Decision *Gates on any street*); the Frontage tool, its Layers row and its rows in Access no longer exist.* |

Firefox and Safari have no lock: there Escape still leaves. The levels rail has a **+** above its list (a level on top of the highest) and one under it (a basement), as tall as the building's level height, the same as the Buildings tab's *+ Level above* / *below*; the plan goes to the new level, one undo step. |
| 2026-10-15 | Circulation stress pass | (4.7, asked by the user: "an exhaustive stress test of everything about the parking, different sites, stress test, shrinking parts of the site, turned masses, closeness, streets duplicated when they come near, roads that make no sense, the circulations' optimization and recalculation, placement logic, many connected masses…", with a picture of two rings running side by side between two warehouses) `engine/__tests__/circulation.stress.test.ts` audits about two hundred projects (`stress/scenarios.ts`: sites of many shapes, two warehouses 0–120 m apart in one building or two, turned masses, a corner pulled in and a site shrunk round its warehouse, grids up to 6×6 and seeded random layouts, dock walls facing each other 24–140 m apart, roads that make no sense, gates in odd places, extreme standards, odd masses, a drag step by step) against what derive() must never produce (`stress/audit.ts`: a stall off the site or on a mass, a court, a lane, a road, an approach or another stall; a derived road over a mass or out of the site; two roads on top of each other, or side by side with no room between; a road deep in a truck court; docks no gate reaches with no Issue saying so; a NaN; two answers for one project); the same project far from the origin (UTM, as Phase 5's georeferenced sites) and turned 37° gives the same; time budgets. **Rings** (`engine/circulation/rings.ts`, revises *Exterior circulation* 4.4 (1)) are settled with each other, of one building or two: with no room for a road between them and both clearances, two rings merge into one round both (dock walls 40–88 m apart thus share one truck court, entered from its sides); two whose sides would run with less than two rows of stalls between them both move to the line halfway and share one road (a turned or stepped side straightens onto it); a ring's side is cut where it would run over a mass, deep into a truck court, or share its paving with another ring's side or a road the designer keeps (one drawn on a ring's side replaces it); points the booleans leave on a straight run (within 1 cm) are dropped. **Joins**: every part no gate reaches, the nearest first (Prim's way, so no join runs along another part), not six at most; a part that cannot be joined no longer stops the others. **Links** keep out of the courts' depth when they can (through them when there is no other way), and a straight link gives way to the shortest one when it is more than 15 m longer. A court is **served** at its mouth (a shared road may run up to half the room for two stall rows off: the gap is paved, its *approach*) or from its side (the ring clearance between, paved); approaches are drawn as courts and take no stalls. Two roads whose paving touches are one part of the network. **Roads by hand**: a repeated point is dropped, a line of no length is no road. **Speed**: the paving's booleans use one outline per gentle stretch (`stripOutlines`) and one union of all of them, not one per piece; a bevel closes a gentle bend, a disc a sharp one (a bevel sharing an edge with the rectangles made Clipper loop forever: it stands a hair off them); the parking's grid appends in place (it copied each cell's list); a route clears the nearest target first. The 6×6 grid went from 213–277 ms to about 60 ms a derive; a 200-point curve's paving from 24 to 2 ms. |

The Brief's rows have a **grip** on their left: dragged up or down, a line shows where the row lands (on top of the row it is dropped on when it goes up, under it when it goes down); ↑ ↓ on the grip move it too. | 2026-10-16 | Saving | (**superseded 2026-11-12 by *Save in the app***: Save keeps the project in this browser and checks it, no file; the file is Export › Project (.gft)) (asked by the user: "enable Ctrl+S, and autosave … autosave by default, it can be turned off, the user is told with an icon that it is on, and told when Save saves"; agreed: the browser and a file) Until now nothing was kept: a reload opened the demo and *Save .gft* downloaded a copy. **Autosave** (on by default, remembered in this browser as `gfit.autosave`) keeps the project, all its options, and the option in view in this browser 0.6 s after the last change (a drag once, on release; `io/browserCopy.ts`, localStorage `gfit.project`, written as a .gft so an old copy is migrated and checked as a file is); the app opens with it, else the demo. **Ctrl+S** (even while typing: the field commits first; never the browser's Save page) keeps the copy too and saves the **.gft**: in Chrome and Edge the first time asks where (the File System Access API) and then writes that same file again, *Open .gft…* picks the file Ctrl+S then writes; elsewhere it downloads one. **Ctrl+Shift+S** (*Save as…*) asks again. A notice says where it went: "Saved “plan.gft”: all its options, Option 1 in view". The **cloud** after the option switch says it: a check and "Saved 4:12 PM" while autosave is on; struck through, "Autosave off", with a red dot while changes are not kept; a click turns it off or on (on, it keeps them at once). Off, leaving the page with changes no file has asks first. Replacing the project (open, new, the demo) asks first when it has work no .gft has, which now includes a project this browser opened (`saving.ts` `unsavedToFile`); a replaced project forgets its file. `views/layout/browserIo.ts` holds the download, the file reading and the pickers. **Opening** (asked next: "the projects I save on my disk, is there no way to bring them back? dragging them, or a button"): besides *Open .gft…* in the ⋯ menu, **Ctrl+O**, and a .gft **dragged onto the window** opens (a sheet says so while it is over it; `FileDrop.tsx`; in Chrome and Edge its handle is kept, so Ctrl+S writes it again) |
| 2026-10-16 | Rotate handle | (asked by the user: "a little white dot when I model, that lets me rotate the geometry; rotating normally by angle jumps, maybe 15 by 15 (or propose the step), with Shift free") A selected mass (not locked) has a **white dot on a stem** beyond it (`views/plan/MassHandles.tsx`, `editing.ts` `rotateHandle` / `startRotateDrag`): a rect's stands beyond the side its local up faces, so it turns with it; a free shape's stands above it. Dragged round the mass's pivot (a rect's centre, a free shape's centroid) it turns it by **15°** steps, the step of the drafting tools (24 to a turn, so 30°, 45°, 60° and 90° are all on it): a rect to whole steps of its own angle (0°, 15°, 30°…, so a rect typed at 7° goes to 15° or 0°, not 22°), a free shape by whole steps of the turn. **Shift** turns it freely, to a tenth of a degree. The angle shows by the dot while it turns ("45° (+30°)"). Its followers turn with it (`rotateMasses`), the whole turn is one undo step and Escape puts it back. The dot stands 56 px out, past the wall's pill and its docks' mark (26 px), which it would otherwise cover (found trying it on a warehouse turned round). **Several selected** (asked next: "selecting two objects should let me rotate too, the dot appearing so they turn together without losing their alignment"): a dashed frame round them and one dot above it; dragged, they turn as one piece about the frame's centre (`rotateMasses`), by 15° steps of the turn (Shift freely), the frame and the dot staying where the drag began while the dot goes round with the pointer; none while one of them is locked. R (90°) and the Rotation field in Properties stay |
| 2026-10-16 | Road snap | (asked by the user: "the roads do not respond to the snap controls between themselves, nor to other geometries") A road drawn (the Road tool, the Site step's Existing road tool) or dragged (a whole road, a derived one becoming the designer's, or a point of one drawn by hand) now goes through the snap, as the drafting bar's toggles say (F3 off, its kinds, ortho F8, the grid F9); **Shift** is free. What attracts it: the masses, the site and the setback line, as for a mass, and **the roads** (every centreline of the option, existing, drawn and derived, but the one dragged) and **the gates**. A snap source can now be an **open line** (`Source.open` in `views/plan/snap/points.ts`: its points and the middles of its segments, no edge back from its end, no rounded corners; a gate is a line of one point), so a road's end lands on another's end or on any point of its centreline (nearest, perpendicular), and where two centrelines cross is an intersection. A point drawn or dragged takes perpendicular and ortho from the point before it (`roadCommands.ts` `startRoadVertexDrag`); a whole road snaps as a shape, its points and segment middles onto the targets' points, its segments onto parallel lines (`startRoadDrag`, `snapMove`). The masses do not snap to the roads: the rings are derived from them and would pull them to where they were *PE.1: an existing street's end only snaps (Q5); only a created street's end links.* |

Now (`modules/docks/engine/docks.ts`) a program's docks **never go on a wall another mass stands against** (less than 1 m of room; the room is measured to the masses, none where one stands against it, and to the boundary apart); *one long* takes the building's **front**, the side of the first long wall along its outline (a rect's local bottom, so they **turn with it**), unless its court has no room there (40 m before the next mass, 40 m and a road before the site's edge, where the ring passes) and the back's has; neither has room, the roomier, the front on a tie; both covered, **no docks**, and an Issue says so ("Warehouse 2 has no free wall for its docks", `Circulation.covered`). The demo's Warehouse 2 is turned round (180°, the same outline) so it still docks north. **By hand**: a selected mass that docks (its own docks or its program's) shows a mark beside each wall that can take them, filled where it docks; a click turns a wall on or off, and a filled mark **dragged onto another wall** moves its docks there, the wall it lands on drawn while it is dragged; either makes them the mass's own (one undo step). The stress test gets the group *warehouses joined* (two along their long walls, in one building or two, on sites shallower than a neighbour is deep; three stacked; end to end; turned) and the check `dock-covered` |

A click on a card opens it; a click on a folder's name over its modules groups it (the outer one wins: `views` swallows `views/plan`). **Only event** (by *Zoom to event*): only the modules the event touched stay, not those it merely loaded (what *Zoom to event* frames); following live, the map changes with each event. The map reframes itself when its modules change. The filters are remembered in this browser (`gfit.state-graph.view`) |

| 2026-10-16 | Alt+drag copies | (asked by the user: "for every element that can be dragged, roads, masses, the tree's or the rules', drag + Alt copies it") **Alt+drag copies what the drag would move, where it is dropped; the original stays**, one undo step, the copy picked. On the plan (`useDrag`: a `Mover` says `copy`, the cursor shows a plus while it lasts, and its `done` runs when the drag is kept): a **mass** copies the selection it is in, else its group (`actions.copyMasses`: the copies keep footprint, height, program and dock walls, never locked; a group's copies are a group of their own), snapped as a move with the masses copied attracting too; a **road** (derived or drawn) leaves a copy drawn by hand; a **gate** (Site step) a copy with its width, radius, flow…; a docked wall's **mark** docks the wall it is dropped on too. Alt+click (no move) still picks one mass of a group alone, and a mass picked so now **drags alone** (a press on a selected mass drags the selection; on one not selected, its group). In the panels (the browser's drag and drop, Alt or Ctrl as the system's copy): the **tree**'s masses onto a building → copies in it, in place, on its level of that name or height (as *Assign*); a building onto an option or before another → `actions.copyBuilding`, a building of its own (not linked), "<name> copy" in the next colour (its levels, masses with their groups, dimensions); an option onto an option → *Duplicate (linked)*; a **Brief** row → `actions.copyProgram` where the line shows ("<name> copy", the same brief in a colour of its own, without masses or rules); a **Rules** bubble → that copy, pinned where it is dropped (a dashed bubble shows where while dragging). Handles (walls, corners, the rotation dot, a road's points, a frontage's ends) and a dimension are not elements: Alt changes nothing there. A measure typed after an Alt+drag moves the copies, as an edit of its own *PE.2: no frontage ends are handles any more; an existing street's body drags and Alt+drag copies it, and a gate copies through Transform's copy (Decisions *Existing streets transformed*, *Gates on any street*).* |

The plan menu has both, *Paste* and *Paste here* (centred where it was right-clicked). The clipboard holds masses or a **road** (the picked one, drawn or derived: Ctrl+C keeps its line, width and vehicles; pasted, it is drawn by hand in the active option and picked). "Free" is read as wherever the pointer is: the copy is not pushed off what it lands on (an overlap is an issue, as for any mass) |

| 2026-10-16 | Optimization pipeline | (asked by the user: "the circulations cannot be placed from the start next to the masses… a multi-objective optimization after rules and before massing… then an optimization of the circulation, with the analysis KPIs as objectives"; the discussion is `docs/ideas/optimization-pipeline.md`; revises *Priority* and the order of Phases 7 and 8) **Masses first, then circulation**: Site, Brief and Rules are the inputs (gates, dock presets, starting parameters); ▶ the masses are optimized against the mass and site KPIs; Massing keeps one and edits it (the courts and a *road room* reserve space for the circulation, roads are not drawn there); the circulation and the analyses move to the last step, named **Flow** (circulation, routes, clearance, simulations), where the circulation is optimized against their KPIs; when no circulation can fix it, back to Massing. The heuristics become **seeds**: *Solve* seeds the masses' run, today's derived network the circulation's. Phase 7 is now the circulation's optimization (the optimizer's *Circulation* template, after Flow and the analysis tools); mass optimization moved into **Phase O**, built first; Phase 8 is MCP. **The mass optimizer** moves, turns and changes the proportion of the masses at their area, within the brief: a program whose size the brief fixes keeps it. **Every variable goes in steps** (fewer specimens, each one visibly different, a result sooner; the designer refines the one kept): position every **1 m** (as the grid snap), proportion every **1 m** of width at its area, rotation every **15°** from the site's main axis, each step set in the *Optimize* tab; later the **z axis** too, in steps (a mass's height, its floors, the level it stands on: detailed in O.3). The dock wall is chosen with the masses |
| 2026-10-16 | Optimizer plugin | (asked by the user: "it should not modify my app's architecture… available by default, like a plugin, that can be hidden or turned off, or more plugins added without harming the software's architecture") The optimizer is the first **plugin** (`src/plugins/`): the core never names a plugin, as the engine never imports `typologies/`; `plugins/index.ts` is the only list. A plugin adds tabs at the bottom of each side panel's rail, a preview in the viewport, and later KPIs and MCP tools, nothing else; it reads the `Project` and `Derived` and changes the project **only through `actions`** (undo, the state graph and the checks see it as any edit), through `plugins/host.ts`, never the store or a view. Its data is `Project.plugins?.[id]` (optional, no `version` bump, checked by the plugin's own zod schema, kept and saved even when the plugin is off). On / hidden / off per browser (*View → Plugins*); off = never loaded (its own lazy chunk). A test fails if the core imports `plugins/` (the registry and its slots aside) or a plugin imports `state/` or a view. **Always optional and never edits the design**: a run reads the active option and writes nothing until a specimen is kept, which makes a new option; *Replace* keeps the replaced design as an option. **One engine, many templates**: NSGA-II with constrained domination (feasible first; between infeasible ones, the smaller violation), seeded, steps and choices kept, never sees what a mass or a road is: a **template** says what changes (variables), what is measured (objectives) and what must hold (constraints), plus the budget. Built in: *Masses*, *Circulation*, *Masses + Circulation* (slower: each evaluation builds the network); the designer duplicates or makes more, saved in `Project.plugins.optimizer`. The template, never the step, says what is optimized: the tabs and the run are the same in every step. **Two twin registries**: objectives and constraints are any KPI of `engine/kpis.ts` (a KPI a later phase adds is an objective at once); variables come from a **parameter registry** (`read`, and `write` through an action), one entry per kind. Templates store ids, so a missing KPI or parameter shows as missing. Limits: variables cost evaluations, not code (workers, the split `derive()`, later a surrogate); NSGA-II reads well up to 4–5 objectives, beyond them NSGA-III or fewer objectives (constraints, groups, the ones that always move together) |
| 2026-10-16 | Optimizer layout | (O.0, agreed with the user, who asked for it "like Grasshopper": settings on the left, the specimens on the right; see the draft's *O.0 layout proposal*) **Optimize** (bottom of the Parameters rail): a first card in each project says what it does, what it changes and how to go back, then *Turn on for this project*; then the template (▾, *Duplicate*, *New*), *What may change*, *Objectives* (minimize / maximize, each with its value on the design as it is), *Must hold*, *Budget* (population, generations, seed, an estimate) and a sticky ▶ Run. **Specimens** (bottom of the Summary rail): it opens by itself when a run starts, its icon shows the progress; progress and *Stop*, a scatter of two objectives (the front, my design, the infeasible ones), parallel coordinates (a range dragged on an axis filters), the list (front · all, top N, sorted by an objective; a bar per variable); picked ones: *Compare* (B.5), *Keep as options*; *Discard run*. ⤢ puts it in the viewport's place, wide (as Galapagos). A specimen's **preview** is read only: a bar over the viewport (*‹ ›*, *Keep as option*, *Back to my design*) and the KPI strip with ▲▼ against my design; to edit it, keep it first. *Keep* makes *Option 1 · #23* (buildings unique); *Replace* asks and keeps *Option 1 · before optimization*. The last run's specimens stay in the browser (IndexedDB, by project); the `.gft` keeps the templates and settings (the seed makes a run repeatable). The rails are the plugin's only door: no button in the tools bars |
| 2026-10-16 | Optimizer engine | (O.1) `plugins/optimizer/engine/`, pure, no import from the app yet. **Genes**: `real` (bounds, optional `step`, `wrap` for an angle: 0..360 by 15 is 0 … 345) and `choice` (an index); every value is repaired into its gene (wrapped or clamped, on its step, whole; not a number = the gene's first value). **Ask and tell**: `start(genes, settings, seeds)` → `state.pending` (the genomes to evaluate) → `tell(state, evaluations)` → the survivors, the next `pending` and the specimens just `born`; the caller evaluates where it wants (a worker, several), so the engine never waits on one. The state is plain JSON (Mulberry32: its random state is one number), so a run stops after any generation and resumes exactly from a copy; `optimize` / `resume` drive it on one thread with `onGeneration` and `stop`. `generations` counts the first population: population × generations evaluations. Objectives are all minimized (a template negates what it maximizes); an evaluation that is not a number becomes the worst there is (finite, so JSON and crowding hold). **Children**: binary tournament, SBX and polynomial mutation (Deb's), a stepped gene a mutation leaves in place moves one step, a choice takes another option. **Twins**: a child already in the population is mutated again, then drawn at random; twins survive only when there are not enough distinct specimens. **Seeds**: the first population starts with them; a full front can still crowd one out of the population, so the guarantee is on the archive: every specimen is kept, and the front of all of them keeps a seed unless one dominates it. Tested on ZDT1 (the front within 0.05, spread over f1 0–1), a corner where nothing random is feasible, 15° steps with a choice, a stop and resume through JSON, seeds, NaN |
| 2026-10-16 | Plugin seam | (O.2; refines *Optimizer plugin*) **Files**: `plugins/index.ts` is the core's only door (the frame imports it; the engine, model, state, files and typologies import nothing of plugins); `registry.ts` the list (a folder left out fails a test); `slots.tsx` what the frame shows; `settings.ts` on · hidden · off per browser (`gfit.plugins`, absent = on); `host.ts` a plugin's only door to the app: `useProject`, `useDerived`, `useActiveOption`, `apply` (one undo step) with `actions`, `pluginData` / `usePluginData` (read through the plugin's own zod schema: not valid = null) and `setPluginData`. So with the floors the design's seed is the brief's, not the design's own footprints (`measure(p)` still gives the design's ◇). A level is a floor of its building: floors on a level that the office shares with the warehouses multiply them too, so an office with floors of its own stands in a building of its own. The *Masses* template leaves both kinds out by default (the *Optimize* tab, O.4, turns them on). **Groups** (Decision *Groups*, made on the working branch the same day): a group goes as one, carried by its first mass (its rotation and centre), turned about its bounds' middle and moved whole (`rotateMasses`, `moveMasses`); no member changes its proportion or floors' footprint; a group with a locked member stays as it is |

It is saved **at most once a second** as it changes (a generation, a stop, a kept option; its progress alone, every 100 ms, is not: a debounce on it never saved a run that goes on), and when the page is left. A run that was going comes back **stopped**, its last generation's state kept, so *Resume* goes on as if it had never stopped (the engine is seeded). Another project opened: its run leaves the tabs (it could not be kept into this one) and that project's own comes back. *Discard run* forgets it. The `.gft` keeps none of it: the templates and their settings (the seed) make a run repeatable. `run/saved.ts` (`keepRuns` over a `Shelf`: IndexedDB in the app, memory in the tests), `run/lastRun.ts` (loaded with the plugin's button and tabs: a plugin that is off keeps nothing), `host.ts` `subscribeProject`, the run store's `load` |

no rooms (interiors stay Phase 11). **The Hole tool** (H) stands right of Mass in Massing's tools bar and draws as Mass does (a drag a rectangle, clicks point by point, Enter closes), with handles as a mass's. **Merge** (H.2, asked by the user: "not only group: a boolean union, so the elements are not split in halves"): masses that touch become one, their outlines united (a group still keeps them apart); *Merge* after *Group · Ungroup* in the plan menu, Ctrl+J (Rhino's Join), greyed out with why (fewer than two, a locked one, two levels, not all touching); the verb `mergeMasses` (and the tool) unites their outlines with their holes (`engine/geom/merge.ts`: the union, without the corners and spikes left where they met, so three halves of a rectangle make a `rect` again), keeps the first picked's id, name, program, height and group, sends its dock walls back to its program's, and curved walls come out as chords. **Point by point for an agent first** (H.0): `addMassFromPoints` and `setMassOutline` take the corners in order (either way round, the first may repeat; four on a square box are a rect, as the Mass tool closes them: `engine/geom/draw.ts`, moved out of the tool so the plan and the MCP make one shape from the same points), `getMassOutline` numbers a mass's corners and walls, and the corner verbs (`moveVertex`, `insertVertex`, `removeVertex`, `setCornerRadius`) are tools now; an edit the geometry rejects says why instead of *Nothing changed* |
