| 2026-10-19 | MCP server | (8.2, asked by the user: "more important, so Claude Desktop can run it"; 8.1b waits) **`mcp/server.ts`**, run by Node as it is (TypeScript without a build, Node ≥ 22.18): Claude Desktop starts it and speaks **MCP over stdio** (JSON-RPC, one message per line; `src/api/mcp/protocol.ts`, pure: initialize with the server's instructions, ping, tools/list, tools/call; written by hand, no SDK, no new dependency). It lists the app's tools (`api/tools/`, the same as the Agent tab: `AGENT_GUIDE` tells both agents the same) and **runs each call in GFit's open tab, not on a file**: the project the designer sees changes, each edit one undo step, logged `by: agent`. **The bridge** (`mcp/bridge.ts`): HTTP on 127.0.0.1:4590 (`GFIT_MCP_PORT`); the tab listens on `/events` (server-sent events: hello, then each call) and posts each answer to `/reply` (text/plain, no preflight); a call waits 60 s; no tab: the answer says how to connect it; a newer tab replaces the older (*Another GFit tab took the connection · Connect here*). Only pages of this computer (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`) by their own host name (no DNS rebinding). **In the app** (`plugins/agent/mcp.ts`): **Claude Desktop (MCP)**, on or off in the Agent tab under where the model runs (per browser, `gfit.mcp`; off by default), with how it stands (waiting · connected, its calls and the last one · elsewhere); the Agent button gets a look of its own that loads the bridge with the frame (so it listens with the tab closed) and shows a **dot** while Claude Desktop is connected. | 2026-10-19 | Agent in the app | (8.1; tried with the user's LM Studio and `qwen3-vl-8b-instruct`) **The providers behind one interface** (`plugins/agent/wire.ts`): our own turns (user · assistant with its text and tool calls · tools with their answers) go out in each one's format, called from the browser: **chat completions** for LM Studio (its address as it shows it, `http://127.0.0.1:1234`, /v1 added; no key) and OpenAI, **messages** for Anthropic (with its header for a key in the browser), **generateContent** for Google (the tools as `parametersJsonSchema`). A model's own content goes back as it came (`raw`), so a model that signs its thinking gets it again. **The models are listed by the provider** (`/models`; not chat ones left out: embeddings, speech, images), the first one picked when none is; ↻ lists again. **Settings per browser** (`gfit.agent`): where it runs, the model of each provider, each key, LM Studio's address; never in the project or its file. A provider out of reach or refusing says so (LM Studio: is its server running, with **CORS** on: its *Developer* settings). **The loop** (`chat.ts`): the message, then the model answers or asks for tools; each call runs through `host.callTool` (an edit one undo step, logged `by: agent` with its verb) and its answer goes back (cut at 24 000 characters), until it answers in words, Stop (calls not yet run are answered "stopped", so the conversation can go on) or **16 rounds**. The system prompt: change the design only through the tools, getProject first, never invent a figure, check Issues or KPIs after editing, ask before removing things or a much bigger change, answer briefly in the designer's language. **A tool takes a name where an id is asked** ("Warehouse 1": the first try of a small model), and a wrong one lists the ones there are. **The tab**: Runs on · Model ▾ ↻ · Server or API key (hidden as typed), the note or the provider's error; the conversation (what you wrote, its text, each tool with ✓ or ✕ and why, the call and its answer on hover); the message at the foot: Enter sends, Shift+Enter a new line, Stop while it works, *New chat* (what it did stays). In memory: a reload starts a new conversation |
| 2026-10-19 | Agent tools | (8.0) **`api/tools/`**, core and pure: one list (`TOOLS`) for the agent in the app (8.1) and for MCP (8.2). A tool is a name, a description for the agent, a kind (**read** answers, changes nothing; **edit** is one undo step), the verbs of `actions` it calls, a **zod input** (its JSON Schema is what the agent is given: `toolSchemas`) and `run(project, input, ctx)` → the project after it and an answer. `ctx` is the option and level in view (MCP: the first option's ground floor); an input may name another. **Readings**: `getProject` (ids and names: site rules, gates, programs and brief, buildings and levels, options, rules, the option's masses with size and area: every other tool takes these ids), `getKpis` (every KPI, unit, limit, status, what it measures) and `getIssues`, all from the engine: the model reads the figures, it never sums them. **Edits** (39): masses (add, update with a rectangle's size, move, rotate, a wall, copy, remove, lock, group, level), the brief's rules and ▶ Solve / Arrange / Add from brief, programs and targets, options, buildings and levels, site rules, gates, ring roads, standards, the KPI strip, the name. Named by the verb, so the decision log says the same. **`callTool`** checks the input, runs it and settles it as the store does (locks, anchored dimensions); a wrong input, an id that is not there (it lists the ones there are), a refused edit or an unknown tool comes back as its reason, the project unchanged. **`LEFT_OUT`**: every verb that is no tool, with why (a helper, covered by another tool, drawing by hand, annotations 10.D, the Site step 5, roads by hand C, buildings arranged by hand, the optimizer's own); a test fails on a verb that is neither, so a new verb is a decision. **In the app**: `host.callTool` runs an edit inside `apply` (one undo step, logged `by: agent` with its verb), a reading on the project as it is. The optimizer's run and keep come as **plugin tools**, in the manifest, with 8.1 (a run is long and lives in a worker) |
| 2026-10-19 | Decision log tab | (R.1; the layout agreed with the user: a list with a summary, the name on top, undo counted, CSV in two places) **`Project.log`** (optional, no version bump, as `plugins`): one entry per committed change: `at`, `author`, `by` (designer · solve · optimizer · agent), `verb`, `what`, `option`, `issues` before → after (the option in view). **The store writes it, never a verb**: `apply`, a drag's `commit` (one entry for the drag; a cancelled drag, none), `undo` and `redo`; a change that changes nothing, none. **Undo is an entry of its own** ("Undid: Move mass: Warehouse 1"): a proposal of the optimizer or an agent that the designer undid is one rejected, what the thesis measures; the log itself is never undone (undo and redo carry it forward). **What an entry says** comes from the verb and what it touched, so a new verb is logged without a line: `traced` names each verb in every build (`recordVerbs`; the state graph's frames stay dev only), and the project's diff names the masses, buildings, programs and options (up to three, then +N). **Who**: the caller's `by` (`apply(f, by)`, through `host.apply`: the optimizer's Keep and Replace), else Solve for its verbs (`solve`, `solveIssue`, `arrange`, `addFromBrief`), else the designer. **The author**: a name once per browser (`gfit.author`), typed at the top of the tab; none, "Designer"; Sign in (12) will give it. **The tab** (Summary rail, clock icon): *You*, then how many changes and how many each one decided (a chip filters), then the list newest first by day (the time, the author's name for the designer's own or Solve / Optimizer / Agent, what, the Issues when they moved: ▼ fewer, ▲ more), 100 at a time; **Export CSV** at its foot and *Decision log (.csv)* in Export (the project's id on each row, so logs can be put together for R.2). A look in the viewport is not ended by the log's entry of a plugin's own data |

| 2026-10-19 | Flow step | (C.0, the layout agreed with the user; carries out *Optimization pipeline*) **Flow** is the fifth step, after Massing, and holds the circulation. **Its left panel** is a rail as Massing's: *Properties* (of the road or gate picked) · **Circulation** · **Parking** (the typology's, `parametersOf`); those tabs leave Massing's rail. **Its tools bar**: Select │ **Road** · **Gate** │ Dimension · Ruler │ the analyses shown off (Clearance, Routes, Vehicle fit, Visibility: Phase 6), which leave Massing's bar; then Plan · 3D · Split and Compare. The Gate tool stays in the Site step too; in Flow it works as there, on the active option's gates (its own copy when it has one, else the site's; *Own gates for <option>* stays in the Site step's Access section). **Massing without roads** (C.3): masses, docks and their courts, and the **road room**, a faint dashed band round each building where its ring road will need room (road width and clearances), so whether it fits reads without a road drawn; no roads, parking or gates' approaches. **No approve button**: Massing → Flow is the step bar's (Site keeps its *Approve site → Brief*). **What answers in Flow** (C.2): the masses are drawn as in Massing (colours, labels, courts) but answer nothing, on the plan and in the 3D (no pick, handles, window, plan menu or dock marks; R, Delete, Ctrl+A/C/V/D leave them; the rules' arcs go); the roads do (a click picks one, its points and itself dragged, Alt copies) and the gates (the handle picks and slides one; Delete removes it); Properties shows a road, a gate or a dimension, else says what Flow picks. Each step has its own plan tools (`toolState.ts` `toolsOf`: Massing Select, Mass, Hole, Corner, Dimension; Flow Select, Road, Dimension; their keys too): leaving a step lets a tool it does not have, and the Gate, go. A building's *Ring road* moves from its Properties to the Circulation tab, *Ring roads*, a box per design building of the option. **Massing without roads** (C.3): its plan draws no roads, stalls or court approaches (the site's existing roads and streets stay: they are the site's) and its 3D paves the courts and the site's roads alone; the **road room** takes their place (`engine/circulation/rings.ts` `roadRoom`, in `deriveMassing` per option: round each building whose ring is on, its rings' centreline before they are settled, a road wide; drawn from Design on, faint, dashed, under the masses, `RoadRoomLayer.tsx`). What a step tells (`views/step.ts`): before Flow, the Issues without the circulation's (`FLOW_ISSUES`: gate, access, road) in the Issues tab, its badge, the chip, the plan's markers and the strip's Issues; the strip without the KPIs of the roads (`KpiDef.step: 'flow'`: road-area, stalls). Flow tells and draws everything. Compare stays whole (it compares the options, whatever the step). **The standards are the site's** (C.5, done before C.4 so the court depth has a home when the Docks tab goes): the Site panel's *Circulation standards* (`StandardsSection.tsx`, after Access): road width, ring road clearance, a new gate's width and turning radius, the truck court depth (a typology with docks) and the stall's width and depth (one with parking); still `Project.standards`, one undo step each, until 6.0's standards panel. Flow keeps an option's decisions and what the project chooses of its roads: Circulation the ring roads, the road area and *Restore removed roads*; Parking the side the stalls take and their count. **The docks are the brief's** (C.4): a program's docks are a column of the Brief, *Docks · a door per*, after *New mass* (`brief/columns.tsx`, a column with `when`: only where the typology has docks): a box (on or off; turned off and on in one sitting it keeps its doors), the number and m² of floor or m of wall; open ground shows —. The Docks tab leaves the rail (the Parameters rail is now Properties · Brief · Buildings in the steps before Flow); a mass's own docks stay in its Properties and on the plan's marks, in Massing, since they reserve its court. The dock doors and the court area stay KPIs the strip can show. **Back to Massing** (C.6): in Flow an issue of the masses (not the circulation's, about masses: a court with no room, an overlap, a broken rule…; `issueCommands.ts` `ofMasses`) has *← Massing* in its ▶ Solve's place (Flow moves no masses): Massing, its masses picked, the plan pointed at them. The agent is told the same: `getIssues` gives each issue its **step** (massing or flow: what settles it), and the guide (`api/tools/guide.ts`) the order Site → Brief → Rules → Massing → Flow, masses before roads, and that an issue of massing is settled by the masses, never by drawing roads round it. Not chosen: one panel of its own as Site's (the rail already holds the tabs and Properties); only masses and courts in Massing (whether a road fits would be found in Flow only); ghosted roads in Massing (they are derived from the masses and would jump with every edit, unreadable as ghosts). **The engine in two parts** (C.1, `engine/derive.ts`): `deriveMassing` (site, masses, per option its metrics, relations, its **docks and courts** (`DockSet`) and the masses' Issues, the courts' (`court`) among them: Massing draws the courts, so it tells what they break) and `withCirculation` on top (per option the frontage, gates, roads and stalls, and Flow's Issues: `gate`, `access`, `road`, put before the brief's, which stay last); `derive` = both, the same `Derived` as before, so no view changed. The masses' part never reads a road (a ring off or a road drawn leaves it equal: `engine/__tests__/split.test.ts`). `withCirculation(…, { network: false })` gives the access alone (frontage, gates, docks; no roads, stalls or Flow Issues): a KPI or an optimizer measure that reads the roads says so (`flow: true`: `road-area`, `stalls`, `issues`, *Circulation issues*), and a template with none of them derives the masses and the access alone on every specimen, about ten times faster on the demo; a test reads every measure not marked both ways and fails on one that differs *PE.2: the frontage is gone: the access is the gates and the docks.* |
| 2026-10-19 | Ribbon | (**superseded 2026-10-26 by *Areas rail***, except its KPI strip sizes, which stand: the ribbon went with Q.8; U.0, the layout agreed with the user: "a tools panel like Revit's, where every tool that exists today goes… the main categories as larger icons and, picked, their sub tools come out"; revises *Workspace layout* and *Optimizer button*) **The ribbon** is one row of the frame between the top bar and the KPI strip, in the panels' paper. **Left, the categories**, each a large icon over its name: the steps Site › Brief › Rules › Massing › Flow (› Interiors, off until 11), a tablist (picked: that step, as the workspace switch was); a separator, then the plugins' (**Optimize**, **Agent**: their action, which opens their tabs as before and keeps the step; their progress or connection dot on the icon). **Then, the picked category's tools**, in groups with their caption under them, as Revit's panels: Site: *Boundary* (Import DXF, Draw: off until 5) · *Access* (Frontage, Gate, Existing road); Brief: *Programs* (+ Program) · *Check* (Against the brief); Rules: the rules' tools; Massing: *Pick* · *Shape* (Mass, Hole, Corner) · *Measure* (Dimension, Ruler) · *Show* (Geometry · Graph); Flow: *Pick* · *Circulation* (Road, Gate) · *Measure* · *Analyses* (off until 6); a plugin: its tabs (Optimize: Settings · Specimens · ⤢ Wide; Agent: Chat; a tab's `tool` name in its manifest). The plugins draw no button of their own any more, only a **badge** on their category (`PluginAction.badge`: a run's progress, the connection dot); the frame lends them its parts (`RibbonParts`, plugins/slots.tsx). Picked while its tabs are open (from the rails), a plugin's category shows its tools first; picked again, the tabs that were open come back; a step picked shows the step's tools again. **Right, always: View** (Plan · 3D · Split, Compare). The tools bar docked to the viewport goes (the drafting bar stays); the top bar keeps the file, the option and the frame's buttons. Short of room, the tools and then the categories show their icons only (the name in the tooltip), as the bars did; a phone has the categories and View only. **The KPI strip has three sizes** (the user: "dragged up from below they shrink and their data moves… by jumps of size"): *Full* (as before: name, figure, the line under it) · *Medium* (no line under, a smaller figure) · *Compact* (one line, name and figure side by side); its bottom edge is a grip: dragged up it goes a size smaller per jump, down a size larger; ↑ ↓ on the focused grip, a double-click back to Full; the View menu has the three; remembered per browser (`gfit.layout`). |
| 2026-10-19 | Revit bridge | **Withdrawn 2026-11-04** (Decision *No Revit bridge*): kept as history. (asked by the user: "like Speckle, with sync, upload…") **A live bridge from GFit to Revit, one way**: GFit *publishes* a version of an option (the active one, or one picked) to GFit's server; a pyRevit extension *fetches* the versions, shows what would change, and *builds* the chosen one as **native Revit elements**: levels, floors, walls, a top slab; rooms, the site and roads after. Revit → GFit is **out of scope** (Revit's elements have no order GFit could read back); only a receipt goes back (which commit was built in which model). **A plugin** (`src/plugins/revit/`: the exchange and *Publish*, through `host.ts` only), **a server middleware** (`server/bridge.ts`, versions on disk in `.gfit-bridge/`, as `server/anthropic.ts`; works with `dev:lan`, Azure later, 12) and **the extension** (`bridge/revit/GFit.extension/`). Built on its own branch (`feat/revit-bridge`) while other agents build the core: the core changes by one line each in `plugins/registry.ts`, `vite.config.ts` and `.gitignore`. Our own bridge rather than Speckle's: GFit decides which information reaches Revit alive, which is what the thesis measures (see *IFC export*). Phase V |
| 2026-10-19 | Revit bridge engine | **Withdrawn 2026-11-04** (Decision *No Revit bridge*): kept as history. **IronPython 2.7, pyRevit's default engine; Revit 2024 to 2026.** Lessons of the user's Fluence extension (CPython 3 there, for `openpyxl`): the embedded CPython needed six repairs (no stdlib on `sys.path`, `print()` kills the script, `pyrevit.forms` refused, no Python class can derive from a .NET interface, `DataStorage` cannot be imported, rocket mode off). The bridge needs no third-party library (JSON and HTTP come with it), so IronPython spares all six, and Build needs an `IFailuresPreprocessor` (a class deriving from an interface) to silence wall-join warnings. The pure modules (`lib/gfit_bridge/`: the exchange, units, the diff, the plan) are written in the subset common to Python 2 and 3 and tested with `pytest` outside Revit; **only `lib/gfit_revit/` imports `Autodesk.Revit.DB`** (a test checks it). Across versions: `ElementId.Value` (2024+, `IntegerValue` is gone in 2026), `UnitTypeId`, `Floor.Create`; nothing that only .NET Framework has (2025+ runs on .NET 8). **One transaction group per Build**: "it failed" and "nothing was made" are the same sentence. The link to GFit's server is stored in the `.rvt` (Project Information), so it travels with the model |
| 2026-10-19 | Revit bridge identity | **Withdrawn 2026-11-04** (Decision *No Revit bridge*): kept as history. **Each element Build makes carries its GFit id and a hash** (Extensible Storage), as Speckle's `applicationId`: a re-sync updates what has the same id, makes what is new and removes what is gone (asking first). **Levels are updated in place, never remade**: removing a level in Revit removes its views. A mass whose hash changed is replaced whole in v1 (its floors and walls; updating them in place, so tags and dimensions survive, comes later). **Coordinates** (revised in V.1): the exchange keeps the site's own (DXF coordinates can be kilometres from Revit's origin) and offers an `origin`, a whole-metre point at the middle of the site; **a model keeps the origin of its first Build** and subtracts it from every point. Local coordinates in the exchange would move whenever the site does, and every mass would be rebuilt. North comes with the georeference (5). The format: `docs/revit-bridge.md` |
| 2026-10-19 | Revit bridge layout | **Withdrawn 2026-11-04** (Decision *No Revit bridge*): kept as history. (V.0, agreed with the user) **In GFit, only the slots plugins already have** (no core change): a **Revit** tab at the end of the Summary rail (outputs; not in the Site step) and **Revit** in the plugins' chip (*Optimize / Agent / Revit*), which opens it. The tab, top to bottom: **the server** Revit connects to (its address on the network, which the server tells: a page opened as `localhost` cannot know it) with a dot (reachable or not); **Publish**: the option (the active one by default, or another), a message, *Publish to Revit*; **Versions**, newest first: number, option, time, author, message, and under each the models that built it (✓ *Built in Plaza.rvt (2024)*, from the receipts) or *not built yet*. **In Revit**, a **GFit** tab: panel *Bridge* with **Connect** (server and project), **Fetch** (pick a version; the model's own version and what would be new, changed or removed, per kind: levels, floors, walls; *Cancel* or *Build this version*) and **Build** (the fetched version, else the latest, after the same summary); panel *Setup* with **Settings** (the wall, floor and top slab types) |
| 2026-10-20 | Site files | (Phase 5, asked by the user) **A site comes from a DXF or from a geolocated file; KML / KMZ first** (Google Earth's, the usual way a parcel is sent), GeoJSON and Shapefile later (5.6) through the same path (a Shapefile read with shpjs, which reprojects its `.prj` with proj4). A geolocated file sets the site's georeference by itself: its polygon is projected to local metres around its centre, which becomes the plan's origin. A DXF comes in local coordinates and is placed once over the map |
| 2026-10-20 | Site step layout | (5.0, agreed with the user) **Ribbon, Site step:** *Boundary* holds one **Import** (any supported file: `.dxf`, `.kml`, `.kmz`, later `.geojson` and `.zip`; GFit tells the format) and **Draw** (the boundary, on the map); a new **Map** group holds **Map** (background on / off) and **Place** (move and turn a DXF over the map); *Access* follows. **Left panel:** a **Location** section first, before *Site*: the file it came from, *Paste a Google Maps link*, lat / lon, north, the map's source (Street · Satellite) and opacity. A file with several shapes: the designer clicks the boundary on the plan. Importing replaces the boundary (undo brings it back). The map's on / off and its opacity are the viewer's, not the project's (localStorage, as in Practiko): they are no undo step and do not change the file |
| 2026-10-20 | Shared keys for GPT and Gemini | (asked by the user) **The shared key, for the three vendors**: GFit's server passes on `/api/openai` (`GET /models`, `POST /chat/completions`) with `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `/api/google` (`GET /models`, `POST /models/<model>:generateContent`) with `GEMINI_API_KEY`, as `/api/anthropic` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; one middleware for the three (`server/anthropic.ts` became `server/sharedKeys.ts`). The server's key goes in a header (Google's too: `x-goog-api-key`), a browser's own key or header is dropped, each route says which key is missing (503) and lets nothing else pass (404). Every key is optional: a vendor without one just answers how to set it. **In the tab**: *Runs on → GPT (GFit's server)* and *Gemini (GFit's server)*, no key to fill; they speak as OpenAI and Google do (Gemini's content goes back as Google's, so a conversation goes on across the two). The keys live in `.env.local` (or `.env`, both git-ignored); `.env.example` lists them. **Gemini offers only its best candidates** (here and through the server; `Provider.models`): `gemini-3.8-flash` to start (tools well done at a Flash price), `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (the cheapest, for simple asks), `gemini-4-argon` (the hardest asks); those Google lists, in that order, all of them if it lists none. A model chosen before that is not a candidate goes back to 3.8 Flash. **GPT and Claude too**: `gpt-6.1-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-6-astra`; `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, `claude-opus-5-5` (the balanced one first, then the cheapest, then the most capable). LM Studio offers all it has. |
| 2026-10-20 | Agent usage | (asked by the user: "a line of usage, to know how much I have used of the APIs; Gemini for now, ready for the others") **A line *Used* in the Agent tab**, under the provider's note: the tokens in and out (thinking counts as out) and their cost (≈ USD) so far, for the provider chosen, with *Reset*. **Per browser** (`gfit.agent.usage`, `src/plugins/agent/usage.ts`): each designer counts their own; through GFit's server its owner pays for everyone's, which no browser sees. **The cost at each answer's price**, added when it comes (`PRICES`, USD per 1M tokens as listed on 2026-10-20; a model without a price counts its tokens and the line says so). **Gemini first**: its answers bring `usageMetadata` (`Reply.usage` in `wire.ts`); another vendor counts once its answers fill `Reply.usage` and its provider is in `METERED`. |
| 2026-10-20 | Agent rounds | (asked by the user, after Flash-Lite stopped at 16) **No limit of rounds**: the loop (`chat.ts`) runs until the model answers in words or the designer presses **Stop** (at the message's foot while it works; what it did stays, each edit one undo step). Replaces the 16 rounds of *Agent in the app*. A model that loops runs on and costs until stopped: the *Used* line (Decision *Agent usage*) shows it |
| 2026-10-21 | Agent usage for everyone | (asked by the user: "that everyone sees what everyone uses") **GFit's server counts** the tokens of every answer through its keys (`server/usage.ts`): by vendor and model, in `.gfit-usage/usage.json` (git-ignored), each add written in turn; `GET /api/usage` reads it. Only tokens: the app prices them (`PRICES`, as one's own). **The tab shows two lines** for a provider through GFit's server: *You* (this browser, with Reset; Decision *Agent usage*) and *Everyone* (the server's, all its users together; asked again after each answer and every 30 s). No Reset for everyone's (anyone on the network could): to start again, delete `.gfit-usage/` on the serving computer. Gemini first, as *Agent usage*: another vendor counts once `tokensOf` reads its answers |
| 2026-10-21 | Agent tab, folded | (asked by the user, on a screenshot: less text, the rest on demand) **The tab shows only what is used**: Runs on, Model (and Server or API key), the provider's error; **▸ Details** (folded, kept per browser, `gfit.agent.details`) holds the provider's note, how the agent works (undo, the log, figures not guesses: off the chat's intro) and the lines *You* / *Everyone*. **Claude Desktop is a choice of *Runs on***, not a section with its own On/Off: picked, the tab listens to GFit's MCP server (`gfit.mcp`, as before), shows how it stands on one line (*Status*: waiting, connected with its calls, another tab has it · Connect here) and, in the chat's place, *Ask in Claude Desktop*; another provider picked, it stops listening. So the chat and Claude Desktop no longer run at once |

The optimizer varies a platform's level in a step the designer sets (0.5 m by default). **KPIs**: cut, fill, balance (m³), the largest step between neighbouring platforms, the slope over the buildable area, with Flow the steepest road; being KPIs they are objectives or constraints of any template at once, which is what makes the terrain a constant. **Rules**: ground steeper than a set slope is not buildable; a road's maximum grade. **Banks** (E.5): a cut or fill slope at the platforms' edges (the typology's ratio), their volume, an Issue when one leaves the site. Every earthwork figure is an estimate (prisms on the surface) and says so |

| 2026-10-22 | Terrain surface | (E.1a; carries out *Terrain*) **Model**: `Site.terrain?` (no `version` bump): `contours` (points in site coordinates, `closed`, `z` absent when the file drew it flat), `points` (x, y, z), `given` (the Heights tool's, by contour index, over the file's), `source` (file, layers). Verbs `setTerrain` (an import replaces, null removes) and `setContourHeight` (null takes it back), left out of the agent's tools for now. **Engine** (`modules/terrain/engine/`, pure, made once per terrain object: an edit of the design leaves it): the contours **sampled** so no stretch is longer than the spacing (1 m, coarser so a file gives 60,000 samples at most), a closed one walked counter-clockwise; a **Delaunay triangulation** (`delaunator`, the one d3 uses: a dependency added) of the samples says which contours are neighbours (two edges at least) and on which side (left / right of the way a contour runs; a closed one's left is its inside), so it holds whichever way an open contour was drawn. **The count**: a contour's height is the given one, else its file's, else counted out from the known ones, nearest first, from a known neighbour C through another known neighbour R of C: across C from R the ground goes on (C + (C − R)); on R's side it is R's; nothing to count from (one height given, past a ridge where it may go either way) stays null. Where the ground turns (a valley, a second hill) the count can go wrong: a height given there and the rest is counted again; the interval is the least step between the heights. **The surface**: the samples of the contours of known height and the points, triangulated; the elevation is a triangle's plane, the slope its rise over run; null beyond the samples; triangles found through a grid of cells. Schematic: three corners on one contour make a flat triangle, so the ground steps a little at a contour's bends |

| 2026-10-22 | Banks | (E.5; carries out *Terrain*) **The standards gain `bankRatio`** (n of 1 : n, run over rise; 2 by default and the industrial typology's; *Banks 1 :* in the Terrain section). **The banks are walked out on the earthwork's grid** (`walkBanks`, in `derivePlatforms`): from every graded cell (a platform's masses or courts) at its graded height, the cells beside it nearest first (a side one cell, a corner √2: a chamfer distance, within a few per cent of the true one), each taking the nearest edge's height and platform; at `s` m from the edge (the distance less half a cell, the edge being half a cell from a graded cell's middle) the bank stands `s / n` above that height where the ground is higher (cut) or below where it is lower (fill); **where it meets the ground the walk stops** there, so a dip beyond never gets a bank. A bank cell is `EARTH_BANK`, its platform's, its depth the bank's less the ground's; the grid now reaches round what is graded as far as a bank may (n × the ground's range + 5 m, 200 m at most). **Their cut and fill are the platform's** (and the option's: Cut, Fill, Earth balance), told apart in `DerivedPlatform.banks` with **how much of them is outside the site**; the balanced level stays its masses' and courts' (a bank never moves it). Banks that meet make no neighbours (the largest step reads the platforms' own cells). **A platform whose banks leave the site** (5 m² or more outside) is an Issue (`terrain`, a warning, naming its masses). **Shown**: Cut & fill draws the banks' cells with the rest (plan and 3D); the 3D's graded ground runs through each bank cell's middle at its height (the ground's own points inside a bank left out), so a platform's edge slopes down or up to the ground; Properties says a platform's banks' cut and fill, in its figures. Issues name a building's platform by its buildings (*Building 1 + Building 2*), a step by its masses. About 5 ms more per derive on the demo |

Limited as before: as far as the shape still holds, a step at least 1 cm long |

UI state, this session (\`useMassProgram\`). A mass drawn takes it; one dropped from the Brief tab or placed by its rules takes its own, as before. **The select shows what Draw makes (2026-11-13, a bug older than AS.2)**: with nothing picked yet and the brief's first program defined by a warehouse type, that type is what Draw makes (`drawnType(project, typeId, programId)`: as if its ▦ option were picked), so the select and the tool agree (the select had shown *No program*: the program's only option is its type's). Once anything is picked (a program, a type, *No program*) the pick rules; a picked type the project no longer has is no pick. |

| 2026-10-22 | DWG as DXF | (asked by the user: "the import panel should let me import AutoCAD too: transform it to DXF") **Import takes a .dwg**: LibreDWG compiled to WebAssembly (\`@mlightcad/libredwg-web\`, pinned at 0.7.15) reads it in the browser and its entities are turned into the ones dxf-parser gives for a DXF (\`io/site/dwg.ts\` \`asDxfEntity\`: LibreDWG's closed flag is 512, an extrusion of (0, 0, 0) is up), so the drawing goes through the same \`drawingOf\` (\`io/site/dxf.ts\`): the user's Quezaltepeque survey reads the same from its DWG as from its DXF (175 shapes, 2,481 lines, 4,904 points, 1,716 spot heights). The library (200 kB) and its 9.5 MB of WebAssembly load only when a DWG is opened (a Vite alias, \`libredwg-wasm?url\`). **Its licence is GPL-3.0**: shipping GFit to others with it carries the GPL's terms (to be weighed before GFit leaves the team; an ODA SDK or a server-side converter are the ways round it) |
| 2026-10-22 | Survey points and roads | (the stress test's proposal: "read the DXF's height texts as points and a layer kind for existing roads") **More of a survey is the ground's**: a block placed at a height is a point (a surveyor's point block: its insertion's z, else an attribute ELEV, Z, COTA, NIVEL…; dxf-parser drops a DXF's attributes, LibreDWG keeps a DWG's), a 3D face's corners are points once each (a TIN), and **a text that is a number is a spot height** (\`heightOf\`: "452.5", "+452.50", an MTEXT's formatting taken off; "N 300166.700" is not one): a Points layer brings them at their place. **A layer may be *Existing roads***: each of its lines becomes one of the site's existing roads (its centreline, the standards' road width), in the same undo step as the rest; *EJES*, *C-ROAD-CNTR…*, *centerline* guess it. The names *tin*, *cota*, *elev* guess Points |

| 2026-10-22 | Balance with banks | (Stress test's proposal; refines *Banks*) The balanced level is a platform's masses' and courts' alone, so its banks' earth is left over. **Balance with banks** (a button in a building's Platform, and in each step, while it has banks and is off by a cubic metre or more) **types the level at which its cut and fill balance, its banks counted**: `bankBalancedLevel` (`modules/terrain/engine/platforms.ts`) halves between the ground's lowest and highest (± 10 m) to the centimetre, deriving the option's platforms with that one at a trial level (`derivePlatforms`' `trial`: its fill less its cut grows with its level); the verb `balanceWithBanks` (`modules/terrain/actions.ts`, one undo step) derives the option's masses once and types the platform (`setPlatformLevel`) or the step (`setStepLevel`). Typed, it is the designer's: a change of the masses leaves it, and *Balanced* lets it go. On a ridge the banks are fill both sides, so it types lower than the balance |
| 2026-10-22 | Terrain smoothing | (The user's stress test: the ground's triangulation artefacts) **Where a contour bends, Delaunay makes flat triangles** (all three corners on one contour): the ground steps there, terraces a contour wide (on the user's surveyor's file, 9.7 % of the triangles). **Evaluated on that file** (170 contours, 4,481 points, 0.5 m interval): *rounding the contours* (Chaikin twice before sampling) left more flat triangles (11.6 %), three times the triangles and as many terraces, so it was dropped; *smoothing the whole surface* (a local plane fit on a grid) moved the measured heights a lot (0.8 m on average, up to 16 m) and was dropped too. **Chosen: the flat triangles alone** (`modules/terrain/engine/smooth.ts`): each flat triangle's middle and its chords' middles (a side across the bend, between samples not next to each other on the contour) are added to the triangulation at the height of the plane that best fits the ground round them (Gaussian weights, σ half the contours' spacing: the interval over the median slope, 30 m at most; separable moments on a grid of about 60,000 nodes), **kept within one interval of the triangle's height and on the side the ground goes** beyond its sides (read across them, through flat neighbours); a hill's flat top or a pit's bottom (inside a closed contour with no other inside) and a flat triangle on the ground's edge are left flat. Every sample keeps its height (on the file, 2 cm at most, at a few added points between close ones), flat triangles 9.7 % → 5.8 %, about 0.7 s once per terrain. **The control**: `Terrain.smoothing` (`'off' \| 'surface'`, absent: smoothed, the default), *Ground: As triangulated · Smoothed* in the Site tab's Terrain section (`setTerrainSmoothing`, one undo step). Points alone (a TIN) have no terraces and are never smoothed |
| 2026-10-22 | Graded roads | (Stress test's proposal; carries out *Roads on the ground*) **Each design road gets a profile of its own** (`modules/terrain/engine/roads.ts`, in `withCirculation`, per option: `roadGrading`, null without ground), read every 5 m along it: **no steeper than the standards' max. grade**, the middle of the steepest-allowed profile under the ground and the one over it (where the ground is gentler the road lies on it; on a climb it cuts the top and fills the bottom, about evenly), **held where it runs onto a platform's masses or court** (at its graded height; a bank is not a pin). **As one network**: the roads' ends are junctions (one where they meet; an end on another road's side, a T), given heights first, each from its ground (a court's level, fixed) and brought within the grade of the others round by round along the roads between them; then each road is graded held at its junctions, so every junction is one height. Pins that no profile can join within the grade: each at its level, the road straight between them, an **Issue** (`road`: *A road climbs 9.2 % here, over the 8 % max. road grade: the levels it joins are too far apart for its length.*). **Its cut and fill** are its width times the depth along it (no banks of its own), shown in a road's Properties (*Steepest grade* of the max., *Cut · Fill*) and **in the option's Cut, Fill, Earth balance and Earth moved** (and the Cut & fill legend's totals, the roads' among them); **Steepest road** reads the graded roads. **The 3D**: the graded ground leaves out the ground's points on each road (its half width and 0.5 m) and runs through its middle and edges at its profile, so the paving, laid on the graded ground, follows it. The site's existing roads lie on the ground as they are. An estimate: no cross-fall, no banks |
| 2026-10-22 | A building's masses together | (Stress test's proposal: the optimizer took building 1's office off its warehouse and set it against warehouse 2) **A design building's masses that touch in plan are one unit** for the optimizer (`unitsOf` in `plugins/optimizer/engine/params.ts`, since M.2 `modules/vary.ts`, as a group is): a wall against a wall, or one over the other, within 0.5 m. The unit is carried by its first mass in the project's order (it alone gets genes), moves and turns whole, and its members keep their shapes (no proportion, as in a group). Masses of one building that stand apart still go each their own way, and masses that touch across two buildings are not joined: what goes together is said by putting it in one building (*To Building N* in the plan's menu) or in a group. Not chosen: a whole building as one unit (most projects keep every mass in Building 1, so the optimizer would have nothing left to move), nor a constraint that the masses stay near (a search hardly lands on walls exactly against each other) |

| 2026-10-23 | Stages layout | (P.0, agreed with the user from a screenshot of the optimizer, its empty right panel crossed out: "the optimizer is this way, always: the specimens and all below, and in the right panel the state graph or similar; its parameters are edited in a pop-up window, since you don't need the viewport to set them"; settles *Stages*' "how it shows"; revises *Optimizer layout*, *Optimize tab*, *Specimens tab*, *Keep a specimen* and *Stages*, whose stage overrode its template; a first take, a stages bar under the ribbon and a ⤢ board, was not kept) **Always stages**: a single run is a plan of one stage; no *One run* mode. **The frame**: the ribbon's *Optimize* opens **Stages** in the right panel and **Specimens under the viewport** (always there while the optimizer is open: no ⤢, a divider shares the height; ⤡ folds it to its line of state); the left panel keeps the step's tab; pressed again, what was open comes back. The *Optimize* tab goes: its sections move to the stage's window. Below 880 px Specimens is a tab beside Stages. **The Stages panel** (the plan, where you are, and the decision): the activation card while the optimizer is off; then the **active option's plan**, one line seed to leaf (no branches); each stage a node: its number, name, who works it (optimizer · agent · by hand, an icon) and its status (○ to do · running, its % · ● to review · ✓ approved · ! to review again, after an earlier stage was reopened). The picked stage opens in place: its **attempts** (1 · 2 · [3]: a *Redo* is a new attempt, one is approved); its **review**, the pick against the previous stage (*pick #8720 vs ①*): its objectives first, then the KPIs that changed ▲▼ (green better, red worse), the rest folded (*n unchanged*); **what moved of earlier stages**, each approved variable that changed within its margin (*bay 12 → 13 m, ±1*); and its buttons by status: to do *▶ Run* · *Edit…*; to review *Approve #8720* · *Redo* · *Edit…*; approved *Reopen* (the later ones go to review again). The plan is edited there: a node dragged reorders, *+ Stage* (from a template), a node's menu (rename, duplicate, remove); the head's ⋯ has *Use the typology's plan* and, with P.4, *Save plan as template*. Turned on, a project's plan is **one stage** (*Masses + Circulation*, today's run); the typology's plan is offered: Grid → Buildings → Docks → Roads (Parking with Phase 7). **The stage's window** (*Edit…*; a window over the app as the Brief and Rules, two columns): its head the name, *by* ▾ and *from <template>*; the parameters are **the stage's own copy** of its template (what the window shows is what runs; *Save as template* to reuse them); left *What may change*; right *Objectives*, *Must hold*, **Margin over earlier stages** (a row per kind of variable the approved stages varied, ± steps, one by default, 0 = frozen; none in the first stage) and *Budget* with its seed; the foot *Save as template* · *Cancel* · *Save* · *▶ Run*. Worked by the agent: the objectives are its aim, typed, and ▶ is *Ask the agent* (the Agent tab); by hand: *Must hold* and the margin only (the review checks them), and *Start by hand* makes a copy of the previous stage's option to edit. **Approve** keeps the pick as an option (*② Buildings · #8720*), the next stage's input; a plan's options are **grouped** in the option switch and the tree, *Stages (V2)* under the option the plan started from, the latest first. **Attempts** keep their parameters, seed and picks; the last attempt's specimens stay in the browser (as *Last run kept*, by stage); an earlier one's come back by running it again (its seed repeats it). Specimens' *Keep as option* stays (an option outside the plan); the preview bar adds *Approve for stage 2*. **The seam** gains two things: a tab that stands under the viewport by default (Specimens), and a plugin's window (a lazy view in the core's window, as the Brief and Rules), through `plugins/index.ts` and `host.ts`. Built in P.1 (the Stages panel, the window, Approve, Redo and attempts, the grouped options) and P.2 (the review and what moved) |
| 2026-10-23 | Stage plan | (P.1a; *Stages*, *Stages layout*) **The data** (`plugins/optimizer/engine/stages.ts`, zod, pure): `Project.plugins.optimizer.plans?`, a **plan** per option it started from (`option`: the first stage's input) with its **stages** in order; a stage has an id, a name, who works it (`by`: optimizer · agent · hand), **its own copy of a template** (its id and name say which it came from), the agent's typed `aim`, its `margin` per kind (absent: one step; 0: frozen), its **attempts** and what was **approved** (`{ attempt, option, specimen }`), and `again` when an earlier stage changed after it was approved. An **attempt** is one ▶ Run: the template as it ran (margins and all), the option it ran on, the specimen picked (by hand: the copy worked on, `worked`). An option with no stored plan is in a plan of **one stage from the active template**, stored once it changes; an option a stage approved belongs to that stage's plan. **Status**, derived: to do · to review (it has an attempt) · approved · again (running is the run store's). The **current** stage is the first not approved or to review again. **Input**: the previous stage's approved option (the first: the plan's); none while that one is not approved, and then it cannot run. **The margin is the genes' window**: a stage's run template is its own variables plus each kind an earlier stage varied and it does not, with the latest earlier step and range and `margin` (a template variable's new optional field), a frozen kind left out; building the problem, each such gene is narrowed to that many steps either way of the value the input has, on steps from that value (so it stays one the gene can take; an angle's window does not wrap; a choice stays whole) (`genes.ts` `within`). So a stage's run is a template like any other: the worker, the runner and the decoder are unchanged. **Edits**: Approve records the option kept (the views make it); Reopen takes an approval back; reopening, adding, moving or removing a stage sends the approved stages after the change to review again (their input changed); a duplicate goes after its stage with its parameters, who works it, its aim and its margin, nothing run; the last stage cannot be removed. **The typology's plan** (`template.ts` `stagesFor`; since M.2 the typology's data, `typologies/plans.ts`, read by `data.ts` `stagesFor`): Grid (bays, modules, angle, origin and the masses carried onto it; the brief, free ground) → Buildings (the masses' kinds and the platforms' levels; the masses' objectives) → Docks (the door patterns; doors short, dock to gate) → Roads (gates, ring roads; dock to gate, roads, circulation issues); Parking with Phase 7 |
| 2026-10-23 | Stages in the views | (P.1b; *Stages layout*, *Stage plan*; revises *Optimize tab*, which goes) **The seam** gains two things: an action's `opens.below`, a tab that stands under the viewport while the action is open (on a wide screen; pressed again it goes, as the side tabs come back; a narrow screen reaches it from the tab it opens), folded to its first line by the tab (`PluginTabProps.folded`, `setFolded`; the viewport takes the height); and a manifest's `windows`, a plugin's lazy view in the frame's window as the Brief and Rules (`Dialog` takes `plugin:<id>/<window>`; `host.ts` `showWindow`, `closeWindow`; a plugin hidden or off closes its window), and `useWideScreen`. The rails stay the core's: *Optimize* opens **Stages** on the right and **Specimens** under the viewport, the left panel untouched. **Stages** (`views/StagesTab.tsx`): the activation card; the active option's plan, a node per stage (○ to do · ◐ running · ● to review · ✓ approved · ! to review again) whose click picks it and goes to its option (approved, else worked by hand, else its input); the picked one opens: who works it and its template, its attempt, what it waits for, and by status *▶ Run* · *Stop* · *Approve #n* (the pick in Specimens, of this stage's last attempt's run) · *Resume* · *Redo* (its window) · *Reopen* · *Edit…*; its ⋯ duplicates, moves up or down, removes (asking when it has attempts); *+ Stage* from the project's templates and the typology's stages; the head's ⋯ *Use the typology's plan* (asking when anything ran) and *Turn off*. **The stage's window** (`views/StageWindow.tsx`, its sections in `views/stage/sections.tsx`, Optimize's moved): a draft until *Save* (one undo step), *Cancel*, ✕ or Escape let it go; *▶ Run* saves and runs, *Start by hand* saves and makes the copy, *Ask the agent* waits for P.3; its figures on the stage's input; *Save as template* adds the stage's own kinds as a template of the project's. **Commands** (`views/stage/commands.ts`), one undo step each, the plan written with what it changes: a run is an attempt and the run store's run carries its stage (`Run.stage`: plan, stage, attempt); **Approve** imports the specimen as an option named *① Masses · #433* (logged as the optimizer's) and records it, the app going there; **by hand** imports the input as unlinked copies, *② Buildings · by hand* (the attempt's `worked`), approved as it is; **Reopen** and the typology's plan go back to the stage's input, or the plan's option (an option no stage approves is not the plan's). An option worked by hand belongs to its plan too. The preview bar adds *Approve for ① Masses* while a stage's last attempt is in view. The engine's `ran` goes: an attempt is to review once started, running is the run store's. Specimens says which stage and attempt its run is, folds, and on a narrow screen goes back to Stages. Drag to reorder, the grouped options and the runs by stage are P.1c |
| 2026-10-23 | Stage options | (P.1c; *Stages layout*) **Grouped options, a core field**: `DesignOption.group?: { under, name }`, an option shown folded under another in a group of that name; generic (the core knows nothing of stages), no `version` bump. `engine/buildings.ts` `optionGroups` lists each option on its own with its groups after it, the latest first; an option grouped under one that is gone, or under a grouped one, stands on its own. (2) **Hall**: one warehouse's length × width, in bays, the perimeter (end) bays not standard, taking what is left to reach the size; length and width may be ranges the optimizer searches, always on the grid; (3) **Columns** at every grid intersection; (4) **Envelope**: a wall of variable thickness (per side); (5) **Morphology**: bays that come out or go in (the "bumps"), facade offsets, cut corners, level steps on grid lines; **each part may take a program** (two bumps at the front, an office and another), so every variant has them; (6) **Elements on the grid**, equidistant: docks (e.g. 4 centred on a bay), level doors and accesses, and a generic pattern the designer names; their placement may be optimized. **Lock** a step and go on; **reopening** an earlier one sends the later locked ones to lock again (as *Reopen* in Stages); each step's settings are kept **in bays and modules**, so a new grid re-applies them. **Then the site**: the copies placed by hand (drag, move, turn) or by the optimizer; | 2026-10-26 | Circulation optimization | (7.0, agreed with the user; carries out *Optimization pipeline* without 6) What the optimizer may change of the circulation is what an **option** holds, so *Keep* carries it: its gates (along, and now for whom: **gate per flow**), its ring roads, and, new, its **parking** (the option's side, each building's ring's) over the standard. **Road width by load** is derived, not a gene: a ring side or a link no truck takes from a truck gate to a dock is a car road, its width the standard `carRoadWidth`. The objectives are today's KPIs: stalls (max), road area, dock to gate and circulation issues (min). The *Circulation* template moves these alone, the masses frozen; the typology's plans end in a **Parking** stage. **Out of 7, waiting for 6**: other link routes (6.2's routes) and a load measured in trucks an hour; when 6 comes, its KPIs become the template's objectives. **The way back to Massing**: a circulation stage whose best keeps an Issue only the masses can fix offers to reopen the masses' stage |

The **Site step's Circulation standards** gain *Car road width* (after *Road width*) and *Parking along the roads*, the site's side, moved from the Parking tab (after the stall's size). **Car roads** show as they are: narrower on the plan, no colour of their own; a picked derived road's Properties says *For: Cars: no truck takes it to a dock* (or *Trucks and cars*); the **Circulation tab's totals** are *Truck roads*, *Car roads* and *Roads* (the whole, the `road-area` KPI) |

Its review lists **what only the masses can fix**: a truck court with no room (`court`), masses that overlap or stand outside the buildable area; the brief's and the rules' Issues are not listed (the masses' own stages weigh them). Under them, **← Reopen** the last earlier stage that moved the masses (its approval goes, the stages after it to review again); the agent's `evaluateStage` says the same, and the designer reopens it |
| 2026-10-26 | Flow stays core for now | (the M.8 stop: the split was proposed — what the designer draws is core, frontages, gates, roads by hand, existing roads, paving, the widths' defaults; what the engine derives or varies is flow, the network of rings and links, the circulation's Issues, the Circulation tab, the road-width search, the optimizer's circulation kinds — and the user, asked to weigh it critically, agreed to defer it) **The circulation stays in `src/engine/circulation/` as core; M.8 is Deferred until project 3 (simpler modelling).** Why: (1) flow would be on in almost every project (the engine derives a ring round every built building), so as a module it would hide nothing; (2) the core's `derive()` builds the network on every edit and docks and parking read it, so a `modules/flow/` folder would be imported by the core every time and be no easier to work on alone than today's folder, already guarded by the stress test; (3) project 3 changes this very part (buildings first, then the circulation chosen; parking as a program; docks by click), so today's line would have to be drawn again. Phase M *PE.2: the frontage is gone (Decision *No frontage*); gates and existing roads are what the designer draws.* |
| 2026-10-26 | Modules | (brainstormed with the user, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-modules-and-catalog-design.md`: the app "grows noisier with every feature", and "if the optimizer and the circulation were two plugins, every change to the circulation would mean updating the optimizer; the optimizer should read whatever comes in, natively") **Three rings, not more plugins: the core, the modules, the plugins.** The **core** models and measures anything, simply (site, masses, levels, buildings and options, brief, rules, simple roads, the grid, files, the generic KPIs, the agent's tools and MCP) and is always on. A **module** is a part of the design some projects have (warehouse, docks, terrain, parking; flow deferred: Decision *Flow stays core for now*): core code in a folder of its own (`src/modules/<id>/`, its `module.ts` manifest; its engine, verbs and views move in with M.4–M.7), listed once in `modules/index.ts`, **offered** by the typology (`Typology.modules`; a typology this code does not know offers every module) and **on** when the project has something it acts on (`present`, in any of its options: switching options never makes controls appear or vanish). A **plugin** works *on* the design (optimizer, agent, Revit) and never names a module. `requires` is a code dependency the guards check (warehouse requires docks: its copies use the dock walls), not a visibility rule. Guards: `src/modules/__tests__/boundaries.test.ts` (and the plugin seam's). Phase M |
| 2026-10-26 | Catalog | (same; "fragmenting is the problem") **The core and every module declare what they bring in one place, and `modules/catalog.ts` is the only door the rest of the app reads**: `isOffered` · `isOn` · `modulesOf`, and the KPIs: `kpiCatalog` (every module's, on or off: it resolves a saved id, as a stage's or a template's), `kpisOn` (what a list offers), `kpiOwner`, `chosenKpis` (the strip: the project's choice without the unknown ids and the off modules'), `readKpi`, `readKpis`; the optimizer's kinds (`paramKinds`, every one in the write order, to resolve a saved template; `paramKind`; `paramKindsOn`, what *What may change* offers) and measures (`measureCatalog`, every module's measures then every KPI as one, a measure's id winning; `measuresOn`, what *+ Objective* and *Must hold* offer), since M.2 (Decision *Optimizer reads the catalog*). Pure (no React, no store, no DOM), so MCP and the optimizer's worker read it. Outside `src/modules/` only `catalog.ts` and `types.ts` are imported, with three openings the moves of M.4–M.7 need: any file but a plugin's (the engine, the views, the state, the api, the io) may import a module's `engine/` (pure code), a file directly under `state/actions/` (the pure verbs: `index.ts` re-exports them, `programs.ts` composes the warehouse's) its `actions.ts`, `src/views/` its `views/` (as M.4 settled it: the engine alone was too narrow, the views and the verbs read the module's pure code too); a plugin gets none, and imports no other file of a module. `engine/kpis.ts` keeps the type and the core's `CORE_KPIS`; each module's list is in its folder: `modules/terrain/kpis.ts` (M.4), `modules/docks/kpis.ts` (M.5), `modules/warehouse/kpis.ts` (M.6), `modules/parking/kpis.ts` (M.7). KPIs are listed by module (core, warehouse, docks, terrain, parking), not in the old single list's order. Ids never change, so saved projects, templates and stages keep working. Phase M |
| 2026-10-26 | What is not there is not shown | (the user's rule: "what is not in the project is not shown") **Controls that create** (the Mass tool's Warehouse entry, the dock tool, Import…) **follow the typology** (offered); **controls that adjust or measure** (a Parameters tab, a KPI in the picker, a kind in *What may change*, an objective, a Show button) **follow presence** (on), so the designer can always start something. Presence: warehouse = the project has a warehouse type; docks = some mass gets dock walls (its own, a non-empty list, or with none of its own its program's); terrain = the site has a terrain; parking = a program asks for car or trailer stalls, or the site has a boundary (no boundary, no stalls) and an option has roads to park along (gates, its own or the site's; roads drawn by hand; the ring road the engine derives round each built design building unless the option switched it off: `hasRing` in `engine/circulation/rings.ts`) whose side draws, by the engine's own question (a ring's side, else the option's, else the standards', which default to `double`: a project with masses and no parking stated has it on); the core is always on. **Nothing is deleted when a module is off**: a KPI chosen for the strip stays in `project.kpis` (the strip's verbs count positions among the KPIs shown and leave the others where they are), a stage that uses `platform.level`, the module's data in the project: all come back with the module. M.1 applied it to the KPI strip and picker; **M.3 to the views** (`views/modules.ts`: `useModule(id)` gives `{ offered, on }` and reads the store's project, so an import, an edit or an undo turns controls on or off with no reload; a Parameters tab or a Site part that goes while open falls back to Brief or Site). **Offered** (what creates): the Brief's trailer-stall column and a program's Docks, the Standards' truck court depth, and a mass's own docks in Properties (docks); the Standards' stall fields (parking); the Mass tool's *New warehouse program…* and the Brief's palette of warehouse types (warehouse). **On** (what adjusts or measures): the Parking tab (parking; it also stays while an option keeps a parking side of its own, so *None* does not take away the tab that sets it back); the Site tab's Ground, a building's Platform, the Site step's Terrain group and Show's Terrain with its Vertical exaggeration (terrain: no ground, nothing shown, where they were dimmed or disabled; with ground the finer states stay: Heights needs contours, points alone give none, and the exaggeration is disabled while the show is off); a warehouse copy's section and the Program window's figures (warehouse). **The Circulation tab is the core's**, shown when some option has roads in the engine's sense: `hasRoads` (`engine/circulation/rings.ts`) is gates (its own or the site's), roads drawn by hand, or the ring road the engine derives (a project with masses and no gate has one); the parking module's presence asks the same function. It also stays while an option has a ring switched off (the tab holds that switch). `Typology.parameters`, `ParamsGroup`, `PARAMS_GROUPS` and `parametersOf` are gone. A mass's Docks section no longer shows in a typology that does not offer docks, even over a mass that has some (before: unless the mass or its program had them): the data stays and shows again with an industrial typology. Phase M |
| 2026-10-26 | Optimizer reads the catalog | (M.2; carries out *Catalog* for the optimizer: "the optimizer should read whatever comes in, natively") **The kinds and measures are the modules', and the optimizer reads them only through the catalog.** Each module declares its own in its manifest: `params` (its kinds of *What may change*, `ParamKind` in `modules/vary.ts`: the id that never changes, the `module` that owns it, its write `order`, its display `rank`, its stage `role`, the window's `label`, `help`, `fields` and `hint`, its step and range, `variables` and `write`) and `measures` (`Measure` in `modules/measure.ts`, each with its display `rank`; the core's in `core/measures.ts`, the docks' `dock-to-gate` and `court-clash` in `docks/measures.ts`). **Two orders**: a genome is written in `order` (warehouse types 10–20, the grid 30–60, dimensions, levels, the masses 70–120, platforms 130, doors 140, gates, ring roads, parking, road widths 150–190: today's write order; `paramKinds`, `paramKind`); the lists show `rank` (*What may change*: `paramKindsOn`, the stage window's order before M.2; the measures: `measureCatalog` and `measuresOn`, the optimizer's old order, then the KPIs by module), so nothing moved on screen. **A kind's stage `role`** (`masses`: it moves or reshapes them; `circulation`) is what `engine/stages.ts` reads (`kindsOf`) to tell a stage of the circulation and the last stage that moved the masses (Decision *Way back to Massing*). **The problem's context is split**: what every kind shares stays in `contextOf` (the option, the masses and their units, the levels, the buildable area, the axis, the grid, the gates, the buildings, the road-width ranges); what one module's kinds need is read once per problem by the kind itself (`prepare`: `warehouse.hall` and `warehouse.elements` (retired in BE.3) their types and copies, `platform.level` its platforms, `dock.pattern` its docking programs), its result given to `variables` and `write`. *What may change* lists `paramKindsOn`; *+ Objective*, *+ Constraint* and the usual must-holds offer `measuresOn`; a saved kind or measure of a module that is off is still named and kept, and its id still makes a problem (no `missing`). **The built-in templates and stage plans are the typology's data** (`Typology.plugins.optimizer`, the twin of `Project.plugins`, opaque to the core: `typologies/plans.ts`, today's, unchanged), read through `host.typologyData(pluginId, typology)` with the optimizer's `TemplateSchema` (`data.ts` `templatesFor`, `stagesFor`). **Which plan a project starts from is the data's too**: `byModule` names a module and its own plan (Industrial: the warehouse's, Warehouse type → Placement → Roads → Parking), taken when that module is on for the project (`isOn`); else the typology's `stages` (`stagesFor(project)`, `planTemplatesOf(data, project)`: the Stages panel no longer reads `warehouseTypes`). Blank gives the templates and the stages, no module plan (it does not offer the warehouse); a typology without data gives no templates (turned on, a project starts from a blank one) and no plan. **What the optimizer still names**: the usual must-holds and budget of a new template of the designer's (`data.ts` `USUAL`, equal to industrial *Masses*': `outside-buildable`, `overlap`, `required-gap`, `coverage-over`, `road-room`, the core's, and `court-clash`, the docks', shown only while docks are on or the template holds it), a new template's first kind and aim (`mass.position`, `brief-gap`) and the stage window's count of gates and ring roads (`gate.position`, `ring.road`): all core but `court-clash`. The worker reaches the catalog, never the store or React. Phase M |
| 2026-10-26 | Areas | (Phase Q.1; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-quiet-layout-and-optimizer-design.md`, "Areas for the catalog") **Site, Massing and Flow group the left rail, the panel and the optimizer's lists, and the catalog declares them.** Each kind (`ParamKind`), measure (`Measure`) and KPI (`KpiDef`) says `area: 'site' | 'massing' | 'flow'` (`Area` in `engine/kpis.ts`, re-exported by `modules/catalog.ts` with `AREAS`, the rail's order), required on every declaration, offered or not, so the compiler checks the mapping; a module declares it next to its `rank`. The mapping is the spec's: grid and platform kinds and the ground's figures are Site; the masses', levels', halls' and doors' kinds, the footprint, coverage, GFA and door figures are Massing; gates, roads, parking and their figures are Flow; the per-program and per-building KPIs are Massing. **`aim: false`** (on `KpiDef` and `Measure`) marks what is never an objective: the KPIs the user keeps as KPIs (site-area, buildings, masses, corridor-area, wall-area, issues, brief), what the user hid (buildable-area, built-volume, columns; brief-gap, rules-gap, free-ground, circulation-issues, road-room, dock-to-gate, court-clash) and the fixed rules and COS (outside-buildable, overlap, required-gap, coverage-over). `objectivesOn(p)` is `measuresOn(p)` without them, in the same order, and is what *+ Objective* will offer (Q.4); `measuresOn` and `measureCatalog` return what they did, so the KPI strip, saved stages and a stage of the typology that aims at a hidden measure still resolve it. A KPI used as a measure (`asMeasure`) keeps its `area` and `aim`. *My call* on the KPIs the spec does not map: site-area and buildable-area are Site; corridor-area, built-volume, buildings, masses, issues, brief, columns and wall-area are Massing. Phase Q |
| 2026-10-26 | Fixed constraints | (Phase Q.2; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-quiet-layout-and-optimizer-design.md`, "Must hold") **Outside the buildable area, overlap and required gap are held at 0 in every run, and COS is strict unless the stage loosens it.** `engine/template.ts` exports `FIXED_RULES` (`outside-buildable`, `overlap`, `required-gap`), `COS` (`coverage-over`) and `withFixedRules(t)`: a copy whose constraints keep their order, each fixed rule forced to `max: 0`, with the fixed rules and the COS it lacks added at 0 (a COS the stage has loosened is kept as it is). It never mutates `t`. **Applied at run time**, where the engine reads a template: `setup()` in `problem.ts` (so the problem, its summary and the decoder) and `reviewOf` in `review.ts` (the review shows what the run held); **a saved stage is never rewritten**: its stored template stays as it was, so a stage from before Q without `required-gap` or `coverage-over`, or with `overlap` at 5, runs with all three fixed rules at 0 and a strict COS. The typology's templates already hold them, so nothing changes for them. *My call*: a stage without `coverage-over` runs with COS strict (the spec makes COS the one loosenable rule and *strict* its first state; otherwise a stage with no COS line would show "Strict" without holding it). Task 4 builds the stage window's locked line and COS control on `FIXED_RULES` and `COS`. Phase Q |
| 2026-10-26 | Stage window by area | (Phase Q.3; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-quiet-layout-and-optimizer-design.md`, "What may change") **A stage's window lists, under *What may change*, only the kinds the stage moves, grouped under small `Site`, `Massing`, `Flow` headings in the rail's order; every other kind is offered by one select, `+ What may change`.** `MayChange` takes `own`: the ids of the stage's own kinds, read from its **saved** template (`stage.template.variables` without `margin`) when the window opens, so unticking a kind keeps its row and it can be ticked back. A kind the draft ticks, or one chosen from the select, gets a row too and keeps it when unticked. The select offers the kinds of the modules that are on and are not listed, in `<optgroup>`s by `ParamKind.area` (`AREAS` order, an empty area left out); choosing one ticks it (its default step) and lists it under its area's heading. *My call*: other stages' kinds are not listed but can still be added, so no ability is lost (the spec says they are not *listed*). Rows keep their look, fields and tooltip; the counts line stays. **Objectives and Must hold (Q.4)**: `useSummary` returns `aimable` (`objectivesOn(project)`) in place of `offered`; **+ Objective** offers `aimable` minus the chosen, in `<optgroup>`s by area, the areas of the stage's own kinds first and then the rest in `AREAS` order, an empty group left out. A chosen objective whose measure has `aim === false` (the typology's `brief-gap`, `free-ground`, `dock-to-gate`) is kept with its ↓/↑ and ✕ and marked *from the typology*; once removed it is offered nowhere. **Must hold** is three things: one locked line (`Always held`: `Inside the buildable area · No overlaps · Required rules`, a lock, no controls, ✓ or *over* for the three together, from `FIXED_RULES`); the **COS** (`Coverage`), a *Strict | Allow +* control and, with *Allow +*, a `points` field (integer ≥ 1, default 10), stored as the `coverage-over` constraint's `max` = points / 100 × the site area (`figures.get('site-area')`) and read back as `round(max / site × 100)`, *Strict* writing 0, disabled with the title `COS needs the site's boundary` when the project has no boundary; and each other constraint of the template (`court-clash`, `road-room`…) as a line marked *from the typology* with its `≤` value, ✓ or excess, and ✕. *+ Constraint* and `MUST_HOLD` go. The agent's `described()` lists `withFixedRules(template).constraints`, so it sees what a run holds; the stage tools still accept any measure id. Phase Q |

It is a disclosure, not a popup menu, because Margin and Budget hold fields; it starts closed every time the window opens, and what is edited in it belongs to the same draft as the rest. *My call*: the spec's "Edit a copy, Send to the agent" are today's by-hand and agent actions, so they stay as the foot's one action; nothing else moves. | 2026-10-26 | Compact panel | (**its Tools part superseded 2026-11-13 by *Tools strip*, its one-group-open-at-a-time superseded 2026-11-13 by *Settings shown open*, its area panels and their settings groups superseded 2026-11-15 by *Panel tabs* and *Settings by what is picked***: Phase Q.7; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-quiet-layout-and-optimizer-design.md`, "The panel"; supersedes *Show fixed* and *Properties in the panel*) **Beside the rail, the area's panel: its tools, its settings, Show.** *Tools*: the ribbon's tools of that step as a grid of four (`ToolGrid`: the groups flow into the cells without captions, the group's name before each tool's tooltip, `Boundary: Import…`; the new mass's program on a row of its own under the grid); disabled in the 3D view or with Compare open, as the ribbon's were. The `Workspace` type is `'site' | 'massing' | 'flow'` and `View` is `'plan' | '3d' | 'split'`, and `SummaryTab` has no `'log'`: `setWorkspace`/`setView` ignore a value that is not built, a saved `summaryTab: 'log'` opens Overview, a saved `interiors` or `sheets` falls to Site and Plan, as before. **`siteTool === 'heights'` stays**: the terrain module's views (`HeightsLayer`, `TerrainLayer`, `SiteTab`) and their tests read it, only its button is not drawn. What becomes public is the built page, not the repo's source or history. The deploy runs no tests (it builds, which type-checks): the three checks stay on the developer before a commit |

A browser cannot write a `.rvt`, so **Revit** is that IFC with each mass an `IfcBuildingElementProxy` (Revit opens it as a Generic Model, seen in every view; it hides spaces) where the open one has `IfcSpace`; Revit opens or links it. | 2026-10-29 | Speckle publish | (the user: "a plugin to share with Speckle… just exporting from GFit is enough, I don't need to import anything") GFit **publishes** to Speckle and never loads from it: Speckle is one more **Format** in Export's *3D model…* dialog, beside Rhino, IFC and Revit, with the same **Include** (Geometry, Terrain or All), not a panel of its own. What it sends comes from the same gathering (`io/exportModel.ts`), so a Speckle version lands where the `.3dm` and the IFC do: each mass a mesh with its properties (program, area, levels, floors), the site's lines as polylines, the ground and its contours, in metres. The user signs in once (a personal access token first, Speckle's OAuth later), kept in this browser; picks a project and a model (Speckle 3's project → model → version) and a message; *Publish* uploads the objects and creates a version, and a link opens it in Speckle, where Rhino, Revit or SketchUp take it with their own connectors. The conversion is pure and tested; only the client speaks to the server. Loading from Speckle (context or a round trip) stays out. How it sends (checked against app.speckle.systems on 2026-10-29: it answers a browser's CORS): the objects' graph as Speckle's own senders write it (each object's id the md5 of its JSON, meshes and long lists detached and chunked, a `__closure` on each parent) to `/objects/<project>`, only what `/api/diff` says is missing, then `versionMutations.create`. Since 2026.9 Speckle marks that last call deprecated in favour of *model ingestions* with bundles (no browser SDK for them yet); it is still accepted, with no removal date, but the version only appears once the server has processed it, so GFit waits for it before it shows the link (the model's link while it is still processing). When Speckle stops accepting it, the client moves to the ingestion rail; the conversion stays |
| 2026-10-29 | Speckle sign-in | (the user: "log in to Speckle from GFit instead: take me to the auth and keep the session in GFit"; then "login only") Signing in to Speckle is Speckle's OAuth with PKCE, no token to paste and no secret in the page: GFit is registered on app.speckle.systems as an app (id `f5adce22b8`, *GFit Industrial App*, the scopes `user.profile.read`, `project.core.write`, `project.model.write`, `project.version.write`, `project.modelIngestion.write`), its one redirect the public link. *Sign in with Speckle* keeps a one-time verifier and where GFit was (this tab's `sessionStorage`), goes to `/authn/verify/<app>/<challenge>`; Speckle sends the browser back to the public link with `?access_code=`, which GFit trades at `/oauth/token` for a token, puts the address back as it was and opens the dialog again, signed in (the token in `gfit.speckle`, as before). The redirect is the public link only, so signing in works there and not on `localhost` (the dialog says so). A PKCE app gets no refresh: when Speckle stops taking the token the dialog is signed out and asks to sign in again (one click while Speckle's own session lasts). A workspace with SSO whose session has ended answers `SSO_SESSION_MISSING_OR_EXPIRED_ERROR`: GFit sends the browser through the workspace's SSO and back. The token field of SP is gone (the user: login only); another Speckle server would need its own app |

| 2026-11-01 | Release versions | (the user: "each push to main a version number somewhere in Home, the repository's Releases the same number; standard, as it is done in practice, and automated"; agreed 2026-11-01: Conventional Commits, semantic-release, the number at the foot of Home's sidebar) **SemVer** (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`), **0.x** while GFit is in initial development: one typology, file formats that change every few days, a prototype and a demo rather than a tool projects depend on (SemVer: "major version zero is for initial development; anything may change"; a beta would mean no new features, only stabilising). **1.0.0 is the user's approval**: when GFit is used on a real project and its formats settle, a commit marked `!` (a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer) says what is stable, and semantic-release releases 1.0.0; until then nothing is marked `!`. Every commit's title is a **Conventional Commit** (`type(scope): subject`, checked on each pull request by commitlint: `commitlint.config.js`, `.github/workflows/commits.yml`). **semantic-release** (`.releaserc.json`) runs in the Pages workflow on each push to main: from the commits since the last tag, `feat` → minor, `fix` and `perf` → patch, the rest none; a version is built first with its number (`GFIT_VERSION`, the exec plugin's prepareCmd: a build that fails releases nothing), then tagged `vX.Y.Z` and given a GitHub Release with its notes; no commit is made back to main, so the version lives in the tags (`package.json` says `0.0.0-development`). The app reads the same tags as it builds (`server/versions.ts`, `__GFIT_BUILD__`): its version (a build past its tag: SemVer's build metadata, `0.14.0+2.abc1234`) at the foot of Home's sidebar, and in About the version, the commit and every version with the titles of the pull requests it merged (the repository is private: the public link's users cannot open its Releases). The versions before (2026-11-01, tagged afterwards by the same rules on main's merges): v0.1.0 everything before pull requests (2026-10-27), then a minor per pull request that brought something new (#2 … #14 → v0.2.0 … v0.13.0), none for docs (#1, #5). |

Not chosen: sides named A and B in Properties (on segments drawn in different directions, A falls on different sides); a Parking tool that paints sides (one more tool to turn on and off) |

on the plan its ends drag along the wall, stall by stall. **Its depth in rows** (the user: "rows, but every size configurable in metres"): *To the limit* (as many as fit from the wall to the setback; Found in the app (the sample, on its 3 m modules): the grid's node came before the lines of the walls (Phase G.2b), and with 3 m modules one was always within the aperture, so no alignment ever showed; on a street a wall's line pulled the point off it (tracking came before nearest); the truck courts and the parking lots did not attract; a warehouse type was placed centred on the pointer, its walls lined up with nothing. The site's and the setback's edges and a street's pieces are no tracking lines (`Source.track: false`: long, often oblique, or many short pieces of a curve, they ran across the whole plan); they still attract by their points and edges, and a mass moved still lines up with them. The Mass tool with a warehouse type: before a press the type stands under the pointer, dashed, snapped as a whole (`snapMove`: its corners onto the others', its walls onto their lines), as the type's last copy stands (the option's first), R turns it 90° (and not the selection); a click puts it there. What snapped is drawn in its own colour (`--snap`), apart from the plan's greys |
