| 2026-11-05 | Units by the numbers | The user, 2026-11-05, importing a civil DWG (UTM in metres, its header `$INSUNITS` millimetres): "it became a chaos" (the parcel 0.3 m², the ground 1000 times flatter, the 3D's lines floating over it), and "after I cancel an import, any DWG says it could not be read". **The units first offered** (`survey.ts` `guessedUnits`) are the header's, unless the numbers look like UTM in metres and not in the header's units: then metres and UTM, and the survey window says why (*Read in m: its numbers look like UTM in metres, not in mm*); the designer can still pick any. **UTM is judged by the layers guessed something** (all, if none is): a title block near the origin no longer hides a site drawn in UTM. **A layer of annotations** (its name says TEXT, TXT, LABEL, ANNO, NOTAS) is guessed nothing: its leaders are no contours, its frames no boundary. **Each DWG read gets its own LibreDWG instance** (`dwg.ts`): its WebAssembly state does not survive a read; the bytes are fetched once |
| 2026-11-05 | Drawing layers | (**its tree in the Site panel (the Drawing layers section) superseded 2026-11-15 by *Layers***: the same rows are under Site › Drawings; DL.0, agreed with the user from three mock-ups; the user, 2026-11-05: "some sites have the parcel's perimeter and another layer of subdivisions inside it: they should be sub-parcels or similar"; "trees, buildings, streets, survey marks, notes could come as reference lines: a tree of these layers in the Site panel to turn them on and off, even if they are only lines"; "import two DWGs: one the terrain, and from another the reference lines"; "a control to snap on the reference drawings or on the terrain, in the Snap menu, yes or no"; refines *Site file survey*, whose "more kinds later" these are) **Two kinds more in *It is ▾***: **Reference** (its lines kept as drawn, flat, arcs and circles too, in the layer's colour: no ground, no boundary) and **Parcels** (its closed shapes, named by layer and number, with their area: drawn inside the site, not designed on; not *lots*, which are parking). A reference is guessed for a layer not guessed something else whose lines lie round what was chosen (a title block far off is not). **A second drawing for its references**: the survey window, on a site that has one, offers **Brings: Site & ground · References**; *References* leaves the boundary, the ground and the roads as they are, lets a layer be only *Reference*, *Parcels* or *Ignore*, and reads *As the site's* (UTM through the site's georeference, Local if neither). The same window opens from the tree's **+ Add drawing…** already on *References*. Importing a site again drops the drawings and parcels of the old one (another frame); a reference drawing of the same name replaces its own. **The tree**: Location's **Drawing layers**, a group per file (its name, remove) with an eye and the colour of each of its layers, and **Parcels** (an eye, the name, the area); shown or hidden is the viewer's (localStorage, as the map's), not the project's: no undo step. **The snap**: the Snap ▾ menu's **Snap to** ▸ *Reference lines* (called *Drawing lines* since 2026-11-15: Decision *Reference lines* gave the name to the guides drawn on the plan) · *Contours* (each yes or no, the viewer's, as the kinds): a reference attracts by its corners, middles and edges; a contour by its edges only (nearest, perpendicular: its thousands of corners would bury the rest); a layer hidden in the tree attracts nothing. **Blocks** (the user: "dale con todo"): a block placed that is no surveyor's point (trees, furniture, marks) is read as lines of reference: its definition's lines, arcs and circles scaled, turned and moved as its INSERT says (a DWG's rotation in radians, a DXF's in degrees), blocks inside it too (four deep), what is on layer 0 on the INSERT's layer; never a shape nor ground. **In the 3D**: the reference layers shown, in their colours, and the parcels, dashed, lie on the ground (draped on its surface while Show's Terrain is on). **Padlocks, Separate and parcels per file** (DM.4, Decision *Drawings move*): each layer has a padlock (pressed when locked) and the file's row **Separate layers** / **Join layers** (the project's, one undo step each, unlike the eye); a file's parcels sit under its group as one more row, **Parcels**, with one padlock and one eye for all of them (locked at first) and each parcel with its area and eye; a file that brought parcels alone has a group of its own (name, eye, Remove, Parcels; no Separate); a parcel with no file stays in the old *Parcels* group, with no padlock. A file's eye now takes its parcels too, so a parcel's eye key is told apart by file and name (`parcel:<file>/<name>`; a parcel with no file keeps `parcel:<name>`; a hidden list kept by an older version loses its parcels). Separating or joining a file lets go of its picked parts (`drawingPick.ts`). The names pick (`useDrawingPick`, Shift adds): the file's name picks the whole drawing, and once separated a layer's name picks that layer and the Parcels row the parcels; a locked one shows `Locked: unlock it in Drawing layers to move it` and picks nothing; the picked name button is `aria-pressed`; Shift+click on a locked part already picked takes it out |
| 2026-11-05 | Ground to the boundary | (the user, 2026-11-05, a civil drawing whose contours cover part of the parcel: 33 of its 43 corners had no ground and lay flat at the mean height) **A file imported carries its ground to the boundary**: where the boundary runs beyond the surveyed ground, points along it every 10 m at the height of the nearest ground surveyed (`Terrain.edge`, apart from the surveyed points; `modules/terrain/engine/edge.ts`), triangulated with them, so the surface reaches the whole site, held level past the survey. Made at import (the boundary and the ground of the file, or the site's boundary with a file's ground alone), not when the boundary is drawn again; schematic, as the ground is (Decision *Terrain*) |

revises *Block shape*'s elements by edge, whose rule, "bays 2, 3 and 5 from the low end" or "every nth bay", slid or changed in number when a wall was stretched) **Docks, doors and patterns are placed module by module on the edges that face outside, and each stays on its module when the block changes**: `HallElement` loses `bays`, `count` and `pitch` and gets `module`, one record per module, its index along its edge anchored on the block's module lines as the outline's corners are, so `engine/shape.ts`'s rules for a longer or shorter box apply to them; centred on its module (a pattern too); new modules come empty, modules that go take theirs; on another grid (step 1) each goes to the module whose centre is nearest where it stood, two of one kind that fall on one module: the one nearer keeps it, the other goes to the edge's free module nearest where it stood if its centre is within one module (the new module's width) of where it stood, otherwise they become one, so nothing spills past its run or into end bays that had none; the block editor tells how many merged, once, by kind ("2 docks merged where modules joined") (ruled 2026-11-05, BE.1 review; a neighbour only, and merges told, late review; the same on a relay, *End bays once*). **One frame**: a block's and a copy's corners, holes, zones and elements are counted on the box as it is now; a copy's own shape is one bundle (its own outline always with its own elements, holes and zones), drawn again on its box whenever that box changes; a type's shape edit that refits the type's box keeps each copy with its own shape as a size variant (its size pinned as it was, *Back to type* in Properties clears it), as a copy stretched on the site does, so nothing moves on the site (BE.1 reviews 2–5). **The tools place, step 6 only sets parameters** (one way to place, not two): with Dock, Door or Pattern on, a press on a module puts one or takes it away, a press outside the block in front of a bay fills every module of that bay (empties it when full), a drag along the wall fills or empties the modules it passes; with Select an element is picked alone or with others and Delete removes it. **Truck court is a tool** of the Elements group: a press on a wall without docks puts its court in front of it or takes it away (on a docked wall it only clears the wall's own depth: BE.4), its depth dragged with the pill as on the site. **A docked wall always has its truck court**, on the block and on the site, as today (the circulation does not change); the tool sets a wall's own depth and may put a court in front of a wall without docks; the type holds its courts by edge (an edge's own depth, or a court on an edge without docks; the depth and range one per block, as before, so nothing migrates: BE.2), and on the site a copy's own depth (`Mass.courts`) wins over the block's. **The optimizer leaves the docks alone**: its `warehouse.elements` variable goes (saved runs and templates that name it read without it), docks, doors and patterns being the designer's; it still varies the size, the court's depth (one variable per block, written to every court of it) and where its copies stand. **Nothing moves on opening**: `BLOCK_VERSION` 4 with its migration in `modules/warehouse/engine/migrate.ts` reads old types, `.gfb` files and projects, each rule turned into the modules it places today (`elementSpots`); a piece typed off a module centre goes to the module whose centre is nearest, two on one become one, an element wider than its module is allowed (it may overlap its neighbour; step 6 warns). **The block editor's plan handles like the main plan**, reusing `usePlanCamera` and `windowSelect` (no second copy), with Decision *Multi-selection* (window and crossing, Shift adds, column lines, elements, courts, zones and holes, moved together module by module, Delete for all), and its bottom bar keeps what applies to a block alone (Snap, Align, Ortho, Grid, Dimension, Ruler, Plan · 3D · Split, Detail). Specified in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-block-editor-tools-design.md`; BE.3 is built before BE.1, because it removes the variable that reads the old model. Project B (the blocks in the main viewport, with these tools there to change a placed block's variant) and C (DXF export) follow **D.2 (2026-11-17)**: an element's `module` is still counted on the runs of the box the outline was drawn on, but it stands only on an edge along the grid (`ShapeEdge.axis`): an angled wall refuses a dock, door or pattern, an edit that leaves one there drops it with a note, and the migration of free masses freezes their docks onto the nearest module of a wall along the grid (*Clipped grid*, *Free masses migrate*) (D.3: docks, doors and patterns are edited by the On edge engine, the same verbs in both viewports; *On edge engine*) |

Along each axis: when the length is whole bays, no end bays; otherwise what is left is split between both ends, equally; an end bay shorter than *Join under* joins the bay next to it. The button rewrites the stored layout (`hall.ends`, `endsWidth`, `shortEnds`, `joinUnder`, which stay in the data) and clears the axis's own lines (`grid.lines`); at the same length the box is relaid in place (`relaidShape`): each corner, hole and zone side on the module line nearest where it stood, every element on the module whose centre is nearest, and a relay keeps every hole and zone at least one module wide (two lines that fall on one kept apart: `relaidLines`; residual review); after it the designer moves the lines by hand as today, and a box made longer or shorter (a stretch, a variant, the optimizer) lays its bays from that layout as before, the designer's own lines counted from their nearer wall. Old blocks keep their `ends` (`start` and `end` too) and nothing re-lays them on opening. The sentence that says how many bays and end bays an axis has ("Along the length: 11 bays of 15.85 m, end bays 12.83 + 12.83 m…") is a `Note`, shown only with Help |

With Select a left drag on empty ground is a window or a crossing (`windowSelect.picks`, the shared `SelectionWindow`), Shift + a window adds, Shift + click adds or removes one. `useWarehouse.picked` is a list (`setPicked(list)`, `togglePicked(one)`): column lines by their bubbles, docks, doors and patterns by their point on the wall, truck courts (by edge), zones and holes (`views/blockSelect.ts` `pickedInBox`); edges and stretches are picked one at a time as before. Step 6 with some picked applies to those only, the kinds with none picked waiting (their fields off); with nothing picked, every kind to all of its kind. |

A lot's corners and edges already attracted (`sourcesFor`); a lot dragged now names its own id in `exclude`, so it is not attracted by itself |
| 2026-11-06 | Street edges snap | (quick fix; the user, 2026-11-05: "A street's edges snap too, not only its centre line and its start/end points"; revises *Road snap*) **Each road's two edges attract wherever the roads do** (`roadSources`: a street drawn, a street or its node dragged): the sides of the paving the plan strokes, from the same centreline and width (`r.points`, `r.width`), moved half the width either way (`roadEdge`): at a bend the outer side is a bevel through the plan's round join, never a mitre past the half-width (but within 1 mm, so a curve keeps one point per point), the inner side meets where the two offsets cross within both pieces, else is bevelled too; open lines and no tracking lines, as the centreline. The keys are T and C because G is the graph and R turns the selected masses by 90°; Select stays V; each key is in its chip's title. `GENERAL_TOOLS` (`toolState.ts`) are in every step's `toolsOf`, so a step change keeps them; no area panel lists them. **Nothing picked: the chips are off** (*Pick something first*) **and the key does nothing**, as there is no "select objects" step; in 3D they are off too (*· on the plan*); Ctrl+C stays the clipboard's (only a plain `c` starts Copy). **Base point**: it snaps to everything, the selection too, the roads and gates as well. **Target point**: what attracts is built once as the step starts, from the plan at that moment, without the picked masses, lots, streets and gates (`sourcesFor` given their ids, `roadSources(derived, without)`), so the moved things' own corners never pull the pointer (a picked road of another kind than `street` does not move, so it still attracts: GT.5); the base is Ortho's start and the command line's (`12`, `12<30`, `@5,3`). The pointer previews with the store's `preview` (`movePicked` / `copyPicked`), a dashed line from the base and its length beside the pointer; a click or a typed point commits it as **one undo step**, and each reason a verb left something alone (`skipped`) is told once in a toast. **Move goes back to Select**, the moved still picked; **Copy stays on for another copy from the same base**, the originals still picked (`setPicked`), and the copy just made attracts the next; Enter ends Copy. Escape, Backspace or Ctrl+Z at the target drop the preview and go back to the base; Escape at the base goes back to Select. A tool started with nothing picked goes straight back to Select. **The preview stands between two clicks, unlike a drag's**, so it is never made a step half-way: the pointer leaving the plan drops it (a panel's edit, a typed point start from the project as it was), in the target step the app's keys that edit the project (R, S, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+V, Ctrl+G, Ctrl+J, Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+Shift+Z) do nothing, the store's `undo` and `redo` cancel a standing preview first (as `apply` commits one), and a preview committed or cancelled by anything else sends the tool back to its base step. A typed number at the end is the angle (counter-clockwise; `CommandContext.angleOnly`: a bare number is an angle, as `<a`, and the command line hands it to the tool instead of fixing the next segment's). Escape steps back end → start → pivot → Select; the standing-preview guards (`useStandingPreview`, shared with Move and Copy) hold in the end step. **One start for the bar and the keys**: `startGeneralTool(t)` (`ToolsBar.tsx`, beside `selectTool`) turns the ruler, an access tool and a site tool off, the same from a chip and from T, C or O. |

| 2026-11-07 | Streets stay connected | (SE.1; spec *Streets editing*, the user, 2026-11-06: a street dragged by its body came apart from the streets connected to it) **A street end on another street's paving joins it**: drawn there (`drawStreet`'s first and last points) or dragged there (`moved()`, behind `moveStreetNode`, `moveStreetNodes`, `turnStreetNodes`: a moved node left with one segment), the end goes to its nearest point on the other's drawn line and splits that segment there (a T; near that segment's own end, it joins that node), by `Draft.onPaving(q, types, skip)`. The paving is read as every point within `segmentWidth / 2` of the segment's drawn line (centred until SE.2 gives it a side), so past a free tip of a street it reaches half a width beyond the end node too, round it (an end dropped there joins that end node); the nearest paving wins; a point on a node or a drawn line (within `JOIN`) joins it as before. **Ends only**: the points between are never pulled onto a paving, so a street drawn parallel and close to another splits nothing of it; a copy (`copyPicked`, drawn segment by segment: its inner nodes are no ends) passes `drawStreet`'s `opts.paving: false` and never joins a paving. A dragged end skips only the run it ends (`streetRoads`) and the segments that moved with it (a node of theirs moved), so it may still join another run of the same network beyond a junction; nor does it join the node its own segment starts from. Reproduced first: a street that shares its nodes with others already takes them along, stretched, when dragged by its body (whole or one run of its own width); the ones that came apart only touched, an end on the paving with no node shared. **Old projects** join when such a street is next drawn or dragged; nothing is migrated. Not chosen: joining any point inside a paving (it would split a parallel street at every point); a tolerance of its own instead of the paving's width *PE.1: a created street's end on an existing street also links to it (*Street links*); the shared node stays the rule between streets.* |

Every consumer (paving, issues, parking, lots, 3D, export) keeps reading `points` as a centre line; `nodePoints` stay the drawn line (the handles, the snap); `DerivedRoad.side` says which. A centred run's points are exactly the old ones (`offsetLine` by 0 is the line itself). Read the side too: `Draft.onPaving` (the paving about the centre line), the parking's stretches and pills, `removeStreetNode` (two segments merge only with one side along the street, turned to the new a → b) and the agent's `site` (each street's `side`). Kept by every copy: `drawStreet`'s `opts.side` (each segment as drawn), `copyPicked`, Alt-copy and the clipboard. Verbs: `setStreetSides(p, optionId, sides)` (each segment's own) and `setStreetSegmentWidth(p, optionId, segIds, width | null)` (null, or the type's: the type's), behind Properties' *Side* (`Left`, `Centre`, `Right`, read along the street) and *Width* (emptied: the type's; labelled *Width (its own)* while the street has one, as *For* and *Turning radius* say theirs); left out of the agent's tools. A verb that changes nothing returns the project itself. **The skip lines** (`skipped`): a whole drawing moves its unlocked layers and parcels and says `N layer(s) stayed: locked` for the locked ones (its parcels count as one); a layer or the parcels asked for alone that are locked do not move and say `Locked: unlock it in Drawing layers to move it`; the agent's tools answer with `skipped`, or with that line as the reason when nothing moved. **Hidden is the view's**: the verbs have no eye, so the view lists the layers its eye leaves out of the parts it passes; MCP, the agent and the API move every unlocked layer. `traced` leaves a module's constants (`LOCKED`) out of `actions`: they are no verbs. **The read tools list them** (final review): `getSite` and `getProject` carry `drawings` (`drawingsOf` in `api/tools/site.ts`), per file (those with layers, then those that brought parcels alone) its `layers` (`name`, `lines`: their count, `locked`), `split`, and its `parcels` (`name`, `locked`), so MCP and the agent can name a layer. **A name that is not there is an error**, as a missing id is (`need`): `moveDrawings` and `turnDrawings` check each part (the file, its layer, its parcels), `setDrawingLayerLocked` the layer, `setParcelsLocked` that the file brought parcels, `setDrawingSplit` that it has layers; the `ToolError` lists what there is (`No layer "X" in "a.dxf". There are: Walls, Axes.`). **A preview rebuilds what moved only**: `carry` keeps the objects it does not move, so the plan keeps each layer's path (`WeakMap` per `DrawingLayer`, `linesPath` in `views/plan/site/linesPath.ts`) and each parcel's outline per object, and the 3D builds each layer's geometry in its own memoised `LayerLines` (`views/scene/Ground.tsx`); a pick rebuilds no path, only the mark |

"Editing engine" is the user's "motor nativo"; `src/engine/` stays the pure code. | 2026-11-12 | Save in the app | (the user, 2026-11-12: "when I save the project, or press Ctrl+S, I want the app to save the project and confirm that the progress is saved" and "the manual save will no longer download a local .gft file"; asked for "a complete check that guarantees the app does save the complete progress"; revises *Saving*, QF.4) **Save is the browser's.** Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Shift+S and ⋯ › Save commit the field being typed, keep the project (all its options, the option in view) in this browser, **read the copy back and compare it with the project in memory**, and only then say *Project saved*; no file is written or downloaded and no picker opens. When the browser would not keep it, or the copy read back is missing or differs, the notice is an error (*Could not save: this browser's storage is full or blocked. Export › Project (.gft) keeps a copy.*) and the indicator reads *Not saved*: it never says saved when it is not. **The check lives in `keep()`** (`views/layout/saving.ts`), so autosave is verified too: after `writeBrowserCopy` it reads `library.read(project.id)` and compares it with the project (`io/same.ts`: keys in any order, an undefined key is none, -0 is 0) and with the option in view; reading normalises (an empty building goes, the levels its masses make, a grid), so a project that differs only so is compared with the project as it opens. It costs about 1 ms on the demo (measured), so autosave runs it whole. Autosave's one-time error notice stays and points to Export. **One notion of saved:** what this browser keeps. `useSaving` is `autosave`, `savedAt`, `kept`, `failed`, `setAutosave`; **gone**: `file`, `safe`, the file handle, `adoptFile`, `unsavedToFile`, *Save as*. `unkept()` (the project is not `kept` and has changes; opening is not editing) decides when replacing a project or leaving the page asks (autosave off, or a keep that failed). A file opened or dropped is read, never written again. **In the block editor and the terrain editor** Ctrl+S does what the editor's own Save button does, with its notice (the block: its primary choice, *Overwrite* when linked, else a new block; the terrain: *Save to library*); the editors register through `onEditorSave`. **The indicator** (`SaveStatus.tsx`, right after the project's name): *Saved <time>*, *Unsaved changes*, *Not saved*, *Not saved yet*; its icon and click stay autosave; its tooltip says the copy lives in this browser, at this address, that clearing the browser's data removes it, and that **Export › Project (.gft) is the backup** (the project's file moved there, QF.5; Load project brings it in). **The check is tested** in `io/__tests__/persistence.test.ts` (the demo with plugin data and a log, and every project of the circulation stress, equal after a .gft and a library round trip) and in `views/layout/__tests__/saving.test.tsx` (full storage, a copy that reads back different, a typed number, a reload, the editors, autosave and Save agreeing). What stays true: the copy is per browser and address, about 5 MB for all projects, undo does not survive a reload.. **Review fixes (2026-11-12):** a loaded or dropped .gft keeps its project's id, so when this browser keeps a *different* version of it (`sameData`) the file asks first (after the question about unkept changes in the open project): *Replace it with the file* or *Open the file as a copy* (a new id, named "<name> (copy)"); Cancel does nothing, and an equal version or none opens without a question. **Ctrl+S in an editor never skips the editor's own question:** a block with no change since its save says it is already saved (no new version on every press), a block linked to the library opens the Save button's *Overwrite or a copy* question, only a block not linked yet is saved at once; the terrain editor likewise says "already saved" when nothing changed. An editor's round trip gives back the outer project's history, `failed` and `savedAt` (`holdProject` / `restoreProject`), and the editors show no save indicator. The read-back compares with the project passed only through the non-schema normalisers (`normalised`, `io/gft.ts`), so a key the schema drops is a failed keep; a pending autosave is cleared when another project opens; `forgetKept` forgets `savedAt` |
| 2026-11-12 | App structure | (the user, 2026-11-12: "all the tools centralised in one place", "a top menu, where you control the general aspects of the app… every feature of the app that applies, add it there, to avoid duplicates", "the Brief menu should be called Layers", "each user must be able to configure their own" shortcuts; the layout agreed with the user that day) The app is no longer organised by area. **A command is defined once**, in the commands' registry (`src/views/commands/`): its label, its key, whether it is enabled, what it runs; the **top menu** (File · Edit · View · Site · Design · Tools · Help) lists every command, each in one place, and a strip, a panel or a right-click menu shows a command by its id. **Tools** is a narrow strip, always visible and movable, with six groups (General, Massing, Elements, Streets, Parking, Annotations), one ▾ each for the group's parameters; one tool in hand, one owner; the workspace (site / massing / flow) goes. The panel beside it has three tabs: **Layers** (in place of the Brief: one tree by the kinds' groups, an eye and a padlock per row; a lock is project data, what is hidden is view state kept per project in the browser; *On top* for drawings and reference lines), Blocks and Properties, which shows the settings of what is picked (the site, the terrain, a street). **Shortcuts** are looked up by one dispatcher, redefined by CAD habit (M move, C copy, R rotate, F corner, T ruler…) and remapped by the user (`gfit.shortcuts`). **One right-click menu** built from the kind of what is picked (`src/kinds/`; Decisions *Kinds*, *One menu*, *Padlocks by kind*, *Hide for one object*, *Blocks are picked*). Undo and Redo keep their arrows beside the menus. Six parts, AS.1 to AS.6 (AS.6, no text selected by a drag, asked by the user on 2026-11-13). Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-app-structure-design.md`. Revises *Tools bar*, *General tools* and *Bottom bar* as each part lands |
| 2026-11-12 | Commands and keys | (AS.1, and the review of it, 2026-11-12; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-app-structure-design.md`, *One rule for the whole project*, *The top menu*, *Shortcuts*; revises the keys of *Toolkit*, *General tools*, *Steps change the tools* and *Brief and Rules as windows*, which say where they were, not what they are now) **A command is defined once**, in the registry (`src/views/commands/`: `types.ts`, `registry.ts`, the definition files by menu, a module's `views/commands.ts`): its label, its key, where it answers, when it is enabled (`true`, or why not in a short sentence) and what it runs, by the function the control it stands for calls, never a copy of its body. The top menu, the phone's sheet, a title, a notice, the right-click menu and the shortcuts window read it by its id (`commandOf`, `runCommand`, `keyHint`, `keyOrMenu`, `titleWith`); a control that does something else keeps its own run and shows no key. **One dispatcher** (`dispatch.ts`, mounted by `useShortcuts`) turns an event into a chord and looks it up for the *place* the user is in (`placeNow`: a project, Home, the Rules window, the block editor, or `dialog`, which any other window, an open menu and any `aria-modal` window is: only Undo, Redo, Save and Load answer there); in a field a plain letter is the field's, a command that answers while typing (Save, Load) only to a chord with Ctrl or Alt or an F-key; a held key runs nothing again but Undo and Redo; a disabled command's key is prevented and says why in a notice, unless the command is `native` (Copy, Paste, Select all: the browser's again when there is nothing to do) or `quiet` (Delete with nothing picked), and not over a question that is waiting. What stays local is `LOCAL_KEYS` (`keys.ts`): Escape, the keys of a tool that draws, a drag, the command line, a menu, a window; a guard fails when a view listens to `keydown` and is not named there. **The user's keys** are kept per browser in `gfit.shortcuts` (only what differs from the defaults), given in Edit › Keyboard shortcuts…, which refuses `RESERVED` chords (the browser's and the system's) and asks before taking a key another command has in a place both answer in; conflicts and the guard "no two defaults share a key in one place" read every command the registry was given, offered by this project's typology or not. **A window's own commands** (`window: 'rules' | 'block-editor'`: the Rules window's R, S, Delete and the block editor's D, T, F3, F8, F9) answer in that window alone and stand in no menu, because the window covers the menu bar; the shortcuts window lists them under *Rules window* and *Block editor*. Every command stands in the top menu exactly once, except those. The keys that changed: Move T → M, Rotate O → R, Rotate 90° R → Shift+R, Corner Q → F, Ruler M → T, Lot L → P, Plan / 3D P and 3 → Alt+1 / Alt+2 (Split Alt+3), Full screen F → Ctrl+Shift+F, Solve S → Ctrl+Enter (S stays the Rules window's), the graph G → no key (G is the Gate), Merge Ctrl+J → Union U |
| 2026-11-13 | Tools strip | (AS.2; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-app-structure-design.md`, *The Tools strip*; the user, 2026-11-12: "all the tools centralised in one place"; replaces *Tools bar*, *General tools*' bar and the tool rows of *Compact panel*; **its groups' lists revised 2026-11-15** by *Mirror, Array, Align*, *Reference lines* and *Notes*: General now holds Select · Move · Copy · Rotate · Mirror · Array · Align · Group, with a ▾, and Annotations Dimension · Ruler · Reference line · Note) **Every tool is in one strip, always on screen, in six groups: General (Select, Move, Copy, Rotate, Group) · Massing (Draw, Hole, Corner, Union) · Elements (Dock, Door, Pattern, Court: the warehouse module's) · Streets (Street, Gate, Existing road, Connect) · Parking (Lot: the parking module's) · Annotations (Dimension, Ruler).** The strip is read from the commands' registry (`views/tools/strip.ts`, `ToolStrip.tsx`): a tool is one command with `tool: { group, icon, hint, once? }`, so a new tool is a command and stands in its group, and a typology without a module has no group of it. A button reads its command (label, key, `enabled()` with the reason as its title, `checked()`) and runs it through `runCommand`; Union, Connect and Group do something once (`once`) and are never in hand. **One ▾ a group, only where the group has a parameter today** (`GroupOptions.tsx`): Massing's *Draw makes* (the new mass's program) and Streets' *New street* (type and side), and, since AS.5, General's (*Mirror keeps the original*, Array's *Count* and *Rows*, Align's *Edges / Centres*); the others have none (*Only what works is shown*), and Flow's settings (Circulation's totals, Parking's side, lots and totals) stay in the panel until AS.3. **Layout**: one column of icons (`--tool-strip-w`), two when short of height, scrolling when still short; flat (one row that scrolls sideways) above or below the viewport and always below 880 px. **Five docks** (`stripDock` in `gfit.layout`: left · panel · right · top · bottom; **it starts above the viewport**, flat, the user, 2026-11-15 (first it started at the window's left): a layout with no `stripDock` (saved before AS.2, or never moved) reads `top` and shows the strip whatever its old `show.tools` said, and a layout that holds one keeps it, so whoever moved the strip keeps it there): by its grip (drag, or the arrow keys), by its right-click menu or by View › Bars › Tools strip; View › Panels › Tools shows or hides it, and *Default layout* puts it back. The user decided, 2026-11-13: **Plan · 3D · Split goes to the right end of the drafting bar** (it also stays in View and on Alt+1 / Alt+2 / Alt+3). Left to the windows: the Rules window's tool and the block editor's tools (and its Dimension and Ruler chips) are theirs, as before. Files `views/tools/` (`ToolStrip.tsx`, `strip.ts`, `stripDock.ts`, `GroupOptions.tsx`, `MassProgram.tsx`) |

| 2026-11-15 | Layers | (AS.3; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-app-structure-design.md`, *Layers*; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-13-phase-as3-layers.md`; the user, 2026-11-13: "a few rows a branch"; the spec: "the Brief menu should be called Layers"; replaces *Brief tab*, *Buildings tree*, *Brief tiles* and the Drawing layers section of *Drawing layers*) **One tree, Layers, shows what the option in view holds: seen, hidden, locked and picked from there** (`src/views/layers/`: `tree.ts` the pure builder, `LayersTab.tsx`, `Row.tsx`, `layerPick.ts`). **Six branches**: *Site* (Boundary, Buildable area, Terrain with the module on and ground, Map when the site is located, Drawings ▸ each file ▸ its layers, and its Parcels ▸ each parcel) · *Reference* (Grid; Reference lines once the project has one, since AS.5: Decision *Reference lines*) · *Buildings* (each building of the option ▸ its levels ▸ the masses on each; a placed block's masses under its building) · *Circulation* (Streets, Existing roads ▸ each, Gates ▸ each, Truck courts) · *Parking* (Lots ▸ each, Street parking; where the typology offers the module) · *Annotations* (Dimensions, Labels, Notes since AS.5: Decision *Notes*, Issues, Rules, Program graph). **A few rows a branch**: a row of its own for each building, each drawing file, each gate, existing road and lot; the streets and the dimensions are one row each, with their count; no search field. A row with nothing is left out, and a branch with no row. A row: a fold arrow, its name, its count and a quiet detail (a gate's width, a lot's rows, a level's elevation), a padlock where its kind has one and an eye; a branch's eye acts on every row under it; hidden rows read dimmed. **One thing is picked**: a click picks the row (Shift and Ctrl pick among siblings), an object's row picks the object (a locked one too), any other row is held by `useLayerPick`; picking on the plan lets the row go, picking a row lets the objects go, Escape lets it go, and the row of what is picked on the plan is marked. The folds are kept per project with what is hidden; the tree opens with Buildings unfolded and the rest folded. **What the Brief tab alone did, and where it went**: *New building* → Design › New building and the ＋ of the Buildings branch; *New building from selection* → the plan's right-click *To a new building*, where the masses are picked (no Design item for it, and no *New building* button in the Buildings Properties: *New building* stays in two places, Design › New building and the ＋ of the Buildings row); a building linked or copied into another option → its menu (*Link to* and *Copy to*, a row per other option); a program given to a mass → Properties' select and the plan menu's *Program ▸*; the options → Design › Options and the top bar's switch; the block types → the Blocks tab; *Colour by* → View › Colour by. **Let go, said to the user**: the program palette's click and drag (a program is no longer dragged onto a mass of the site), deleting several options at once and the drag of a building between options, the Levels tab's thumbnail of each level and its *Building* filter, the Site tab's Site / Ground / Access switch and the closed groups' one-line values. **A right-click picks what it is on**, as a click does but for the masses: a mass, a level and a drawing file not among the picked are picked alone first, and so is a building, **without letting go of the masses selected** (so *Assign selection to <building>* works from the tree, and the menu, the marks and Properties name the same building); a row among the picked keeps them all and opens the menu of the several (buildings, levels, masses). *In another option ▸* lists **every** other option (*Link to* and *Copy to*, a row each: the drag between options went, so there is no other way to the seventh option), and the plan's *Program ▸* every program of the brief (a long menu scrolls inside the window). A building's *Delete* (and a delete of several, and *Remove N from* when those buildings would go) is off, with the reason, while a locked mass stands on it. **The building the plan is on stays drawn on the plan when it is hidden** (it is hidden in the 3D and as context), as before. **Level planes** and **Issue count on the chip** stay in View › Overlays alone: they are not rows. The Brief's menu entries and its window stay (Design › Brief…, Rules…) *PE.2: no Frontage branch or row (a saved frontage is an existing street: Decision *No frontage*).* |
| 2026-11-15 | Hidden per project | (AS.3; the plan's call, said to the user; replaces the five flags that kept a "shown" state: buildings, drawings, the map, the dimensions and the program graph's markers and arcs) **What is hidden is one store, per project, in this browser** (`views/layers/layerView.ts`: `useLayerView` with `hidden`, `onTop`, `folded`; `isHidden(key)` outside React, `useHidden(key)` in a view; kept in `gfit.layers` as `{ [projectId]: { hidden, onTop, folded } }`, read when the project's id changes, written on each change inside try/catch). The block editor's and the terrain editor's pseudo-projects are kept in memory only, entries of projects no longer in the library are dropped when it loads (never the open project's nor the sample's, which is in the library only once edited), and the old keys (`gfit.drawings.hidden`, `gfit.labels`, `gfit.dims`, `gfit.map.visible`, the `markers` and `arcs` of `gfit.graph`) are read no more and removed once; no migration, everything shows until it is hidden in each project. `views/layers/keys.ts` makes the keys: the kinds (boundary, buildable, map, labels, dimensions, issues, rules, streets, existing roads, frontage, gates, courts, lots, street parking) and an object's own where it has a row (`building(id)`, `drawingLayer(file, layer)`, `parcel(name, file?)`, `gate(id)`, `existingRoad(id)`, `frontageOf(id)`, `lot(id)`). **Hidden is four things**: not drawn in the plan or the 3D, not picked by a click or the selection window, not snapped to, and **still counted** (the figures, the issues and the export read the project, never the store); a tool that draws a kind shows it again. **Three eyes run a switch that exists, and are not keys**: the *Grid* (the snap's switch, F9, `view.grid`), the *Terrain* (its look: off, or the last look chosen in View › Terrain) and the *Program graph* (a mode: it ghosts the masses, `view.overlay.graph`). Every other eye is a key. **A branch's eye acts on the keys under it alone** and reads shown, hidden or mixed from those keys only: the three switch rows keep their own eye and a branch never flips them (the Reference branch has the eye of its reference lines when it holds some, since AS.5, and none while it holds only the Grid). **What is hidden, on top or folded for an object that is gone is dropped** (`views/layers/gone.ts` says what stands: an object counts while it is in any option; `layerView.ts` prunes the open project's entry from a subscription to the store, never during a drag's preview nor for another project's entry, and the entry of a project that opens is loaded first), because ids come back (`newId` gives the first free one) and a new building or gate must not be born hidden; an undo of the delete brings the object back shown. A row picked in Layers for a deleted object is let go the same way (`layerPick.ts`). **A hidden building hides whole**: its masses, and its truck courts and dock ticks on the plan, its dock doors, court paving, block fixtures and graph bubbles in the 3D, its courts as snap sources, and the group members the plan does not draw are left out of a click's and a window's pick; the one rule is `buildingDrawn` / `massDrawn` in `views/buildingView.ts` (the plan keeps the building it is on drawn, even when its eye is off; the 3D does not), and the figures do not change. **On top**, a third toggle on a drawing layer's row, a file's Parcels row, a file's row and the Drawings row (`useLayerView.onTop`, per project, never an undo step): in the plan the layer is drawn over the masses and under the dimensions (`DrawingsLayer` mounted a second time), in the 3D its lines are drawn through the model (`depthTest` off, `ON_TOP_RENDER_ORDER` 8); picking and snapping do not change, and a hidden layer on top is still hidden. Reference lines take the same toggle since AS.5, one for the kind (`reference-lines`): Decision *Reference lines*. **The keys of AS.5**: `reference-lines` and `reference-line:<id>`, `notes` and `note:<id>` *PE.2: no frontage key or eye; `gone.ts` lets go of an old one.* |

A click, a window, Select all and the 3D's pick skip a locked mass and say "Locked: unlock it in Layers" (the notice `LOCKED`, a locked drawing layer's too); Move, Rotate, a typed dimension and the verbs the agent's tools call leave it. **`state/settle.ts` keeps every field of a locked mass but `locked` itself** (its name and its program, its docks, courts, door pattern and exceptions, zones, part, group, level: an edit that changes one is refused whole, with "<name> is locked: unlock it to change it"; deleting it is refused too; only a group that goes with its partner and a `part` that goes with its copy's hall are let through, being the app's own clean-up), so the verbs, the agent's tools (which say the mass is locked, `notLocked` in `api/tools/edit.ts`) and the optimizer are held by the one rule; `updateMass`, `assignMasses` and the like skip it besides, so a lock among several masses leaves the others to change. **The controls that edit a mass do not act on a locked one**: its dock marks, door slots and court pills are not drawn (its docks and courts are), Go to an issue and *Select the masses* skip it (all locked: the notice), a right-click on it opens no menu (the notice alone), the tree's Delete is off while it would go, and a warehouse copy's hall stays locked when its type is edited (an edit that would change it is refused); its fields in Properties are read-only, all but the padlock; Edit › Lock (Ctrl+Shift+L) locks and unlocks what is picked, and undo puts a lock back. **A dimension's lock is not the padlock**: it fixes its length and drags a mass along, keeps its behaviour and is called *Fixed length* in Properties and on its glyph ("Its length is kept: moving one of its masses drags the other"); the Dimensions row has no padlock. **Padlocks stand where a lock exists today**: masses, levels and buildings (a building's and a level's act on every mass they hold and read *mixed* when some are locked), drawing layers, a file's parcels, a file and Drawings (the same); the rows of the other kinds (streets, gates, lots, the boundary…) had none until AS.4 gave them the data (**superseded 2026-11-15 by *Padlocks by kind***: a street, a lot, a gate and a building have one; the boundary and the other rows have none, *Only what works is shown*) |

The building row's padlock turns the flag on; turning it off also frees the masses locked one by one, so "unlocked" reads unlocked; the row reads *mixed* when the flag is off and some masses are locked; a level's padlock stays the sum of its masses. **A street's padlock is its segments'**, since the street a person sees is the derived road, whose id changes when it is split: a street is locked when all its segments are and *mixed* when some are; a locked segment holds its fields and the place of its two nodes, and a drag of a mixed street moves its free nodes only. **`state/settle.ts` holds the five kinds with one check** (`lockedChange`): every field but `locked` the same and the object not deleted, in the words "<name> is locked: unlock it to change it." and "to delete it."; so the verbs, the agent's and MCP's tools (`lockMasses`, `lockBuildings`, `lockStreets`, `lockGates`, `lockLots`, whose refusals name the tool that frees: `docs/mcp.md`) and the optimizer are held by one rule. `kinds/locked.ts` (pure) says whether a `Ref` is locked. What they share with Move, Copy and Rotate is `views/plan/tools/standingPreview.ts`. **Mirror** (`LineTool.tsx`, `Mirror.tsx`): two clicks give the mirror line, the picked things are shown reflected from the first click on, and the second click (or a typed angle) sets them; Ortho steps the line by 15° (Shift is free); **it keeps the original by default** (the reflection is a copy, as CAD does), and off, the thing itself is reflected. **Array** (`ArrayTool.tsx`): a base point, then the place of the first copy, and that vector is the spacing; the copies go on along it; with *Rows* over 1 a third click gives the place of the second row; a typed number is the spacing along the pointer's direction, and **a typed `x5`** (also `X5` and `×5`, read only while Array is on: `CommandContext.count`) sets the count; each copy is what Copy makes (a new thing, born unlocked; a group's copies are a group of their own, each array copy its own); the tool lets go when the array is set. **Array's caps: 50 in a row, 50 rows and 100 copies in all**, held by the view (the fields and the settings store; the verb has none), because every copy is a whole Copy made again at each pointer move. **Align** (`LineTool.tsx`, `Align.tsx`): two clicks give the line (they snap, so a wall, a reference line or the boundary gives it; the second takes Ortho's axes), and each thing moves square to the line until it touches it: by its *Edges* (the side nearest the line, the default) or its *Centres*; a group moves as one, and a mass that moves with a picked one by a rule moves once (by its own unit's shift when it is picked too, else by its leader's); it aligns masses, free lots, reference lines and notes, and says once what it skipped: *Streets are not aligned: move their nodes*, *Gates are not aligned: they stay on the boundary*, *Dimensions are not aligned*, *Drawings are not aligned*, and an anchored lot alone (*<lot> follows its wall: pick its mass to align it*); spreading things evenly (*Distribute*) is not built. **The General group has a ▾** (`views/tools/GeneralOptions.tsx`; it had none): *Mirror keeps the original* (on), Array's *Count* (3) and *Rows* (1) and Align's *Edges / Centres*; it is view state kept in the browser (`gfit.general`, `plan/tools/generalSettings.ts`, as the snap's switches are), never in the project or the undo history. **The snap's *Align* switch (View › Align, `view.align`, no key) and the tool *Align* (`tool.align`, `Shift+A`) are different commands in different menus.** **How a mass is reflected** (`engine/geom/reflect.ts`, pure; `mirrorMasses` in `state/actions/masses.ts`): its footprint and holes are reflected; a contour's vertices are put back counter-clockwise, an arc keeping its bulge's value on its edge's new index, **and started again so that its walls come in the mirror of their old order**: a reflection reverses the outline, which would make another wall the first, and what reads the first wall (a program's docks take the side of the first long wall along the outline, `firstLongWall` in the docks engine; an anchored lot's box is framed by the first edge) would find the wrong one. The new first edge is the mirror image of the old edge `k`: the one the first long wall lies on when the mass's docks come from its program (no docks of its own, and a program with docks), else the first edge (`reflectContour`'s `first`, `edgeToStart` in `masses.ts`); the arcs and the radii go with their edges and corners, and mirrored twice a contour is the same shape with its vertex order turned; a rectangle's rotation becomes `2α − rotation + 180` when it is wider than deep and `2α − rotation` when it is deeper than wide (α the line's angle), so its local bottom stays its bottom and **a program's docks follow**; what the mass keeps by its place along the perimeter (`docks`, `doorsAt`, `courts`, `blockCourts`, `doorFlips`) goes to the same walls seen in the mirror, each `t` by the reflection of the point it names, found on the new outline (`doorsAt[].at` becomes the wall's length − at, `doorFlips[].module` becomes n − 1 − module). A street's nodes are reflected and each segment's `side` (and its parking's) changes hand; a free lot by its rectangle, an anchored lot goes with its mass against the same wall, and alone it is skipped, as in Rotate; a dimension by its two points and the sign of its offset (freed from its anchors, as in Rotate); a reference line and a note by their points (a note's angle too); **gates stay** on the boundary and imported drawings are not mirrored (both said). **A warehouse block's copy is not mirrored**: its place has a position and an angle and no hand, so a reflected hall would be made again un-mirrored the next time its type changes; Mirror leaves it where it is and says *A block is not mirrored: its type has one hand* (mirroring a block belongs to project D, blocks on the engines). **Array along a path is not built**: it needs the path engine of project E (streets). **Known limits**: a `doorPattern` that is not the same from both ends (an `every` over 1, an `offset` or a `mask`) is kept as it is, so it starts from the wall's other end; `doorFlips` of a mass with no grid are kept as they are (with a grid, one past its wall's modules is dropped). **A mirrored copy is what Copy makes, reflected**: `copyMass` carries `docks`, `holes`, the door pattern and its exceptions, and not the mass's own court depths (`courts`: no test or Decision says Copy leaves them out, so it may be an oversight, left as it is), nor `doorsAt` and `blockCourts` (a block's: a block is copied as a copy of its type and is not mirrored); a copied street has no stalls of its own (`copyStreets`). **More known limits**: where a mass has docks from its program and a lot anchored on an irregular outline, the docks win (the outline starts at their wall), so the lot is framed by the new first edge and may land against another box than the mirror image of its old one (pinned in `selectionTools.test.ts`); an exact square (width = depth) has no long direction, so its docks' front comes from the order of its hull's rectangle and not from its local bottom, and does not always follow a mirror (nor a turn); Mirror without the original is refused while a locked lot is anchored to the mirrored mass and its anchor would change (*Lot 1 is locked: unlock it to change it.*: a stretch `from` / `length` is measured from the other end), where Move and Rotate pass because the anchor's fields do not change; in the CAD plan a note's lines step 1.5 × the text height, and the plan's screen step is 1.25 em. **Locked things**: a locked thing is not picked, so it is not transformed; a copy of a lot, a gate, a note or a reference line is born unlocked. Tests `engine/geom/__tests__/reflect.test.ts`, `state/actions/__tests__/selectionTools.test.ts`, `views/plan/tools/__tests__/mirrorArrayAlign.test.tsx` |
| 2026-11-15 | Groups of any kind | (AS.5; the plan's call, said to the user and open to a no; revises the groups part of *Plan menu and groups*, whose `Mass.group`, Ctrl+G, Ctrl+Shift+G and *Group · n* stand for masses) **A group may hold a mass, a street, an existing street, a lot, a gate, a dimension, a reference line and a note**, the eight kinds the transform tools move (`GROUP_KINDS`, `engine/groups.ts`; the existing street since Phase PE.2, with `ExistingRoad.group?` kept on the site, so in every option: Decision *Existing streets transformed*); not an imported drawing (it has no id of its own), not a building or a level. **Where it is kept**: an optional `group` field on each object, as a mass keeps it; **a street's is on each of its segments**, and a street is in the group of its first segment that has one, so a split street keeps both parts in the group and a joined one takes its first tagged segment's. **One id names the group across kinds** (`g1`, `g2`…, the first free among every kind's, whoever makes it: Group, the copies of a group, a copied building, a block's masses made again). There is no list of groups: **a group is what shares the id in the option in view** (masses, dimensions, reference lines and notes are the project's; streets, lots and gates the option's), and **a group of one is none**: `settle` strips a lone id, and a locked thing may lose its group so, never gain or change one. `groupRefs(p, option, refs)` and `ungroupRefs` (`state/actions/groups.ts`) take `Ref`s of any kind and are refused whole when a member is locked or something cannot be grouped; `groupMasses` and `ungroupMasses` are as they were (the optimizer and the agent read the masses' `groupMembers`). **The pick**: a click on a member picks the whole group, whatever its kinds; Shift adds or takes it out whole and the selection window takes whole groups; Alt+click picks one member alone; a member the plan cannot pick is left out: a locked one, a hidden one, a note or a dimension of another level than the one in view, a mass that is neither of that level nor of a building the option shows (the store's own rule for what can be picked together, `joinable`) (`groupOnPlan` and `pickGroup`, in the bridge `plan/mix.ts`). **A click never moves the plan to another level unless the thing clicked is a mass**: then it goes to that mass's level, as a click on it always did; a click on a note, a line or a lot leaves the level where it is, and Ctrl+G picks the group it makes without moving the plan. A street with only some segments locked is picked (its free segments move and turn), as Shift+click and the window pick it; a street with all of them locked is left out. A Shift+click on a group keeps a segment picked on another street. **The whole-pick drag** (`plan/pickedDrag.ts`, `wholeDrag`): when what is picked holds more than one kind, or the pressed thing is in a picked group, a drag moves everything picked through `movePicked`, not the kind pressed alone. **Copies are a group of their own**: Copy, Array (each copy its own) and Mirror keeping the original give the copies of a group's members, when two or more are copied, one new group across kinds; the frame is the box of what turns (the nodes a locked segment holds left out), its centre is the pivot of the dot's turn, and the dot turns what Rotate turns. **The commands' reasons** (`plan/massCommands.ts`): masses alone keep the masses' words, when none of their groups holds another kind (a mass whose group holds a lot or a note acts with the whole group: Ctrl+G joins the group whole to what is picked, Ctrl+Shift+G frees every member, through `groupRefs` and `ungroupRefs`);
