| 2026-11-19 | Lanes | (PE.2; the plan's call, said to the user and open to a no; Q8 and Q9 are the user's) **A stretch has lanes, and its width is their sum.** `lanes?: number[]` (m, left to right walking the path) on a street segment and on each stretch of an existing street (project 20), adding up to `left + right` within 1 mm (the schema refuses a list that does not: "The lanes add up to the street’s width"); `Standards.laneWidth` (3.5 m, the typology's, the *Lane width* field of the Brief's Standards, 2 m at least) is a new lane's width and the unit of the count below. The arithmetic is pure in `engine/path/lanes.ts` (`LANE_WIDTH`, `MIN_LANE` = 2 m, `lanesOf`, `storedLanes`, `laneLines`, `pulledLanes`) and **five ops of `runPathOp`** reach it through the one `editPath`: `lanes` (±1, the pill), `line` (a lane line dragged by an amount: it clamps at 2 m and says so), `lane` (a lane's width typed: under 2 m it refuses), `equal-lanes` (n equal lanes, n kept unless given) and `lane-widths` (every lane given, adding up to the width: the agent's); the drag clamps and the typed number refuses, as for the sides. A kind says whether it has lanes (`Kind.path.lanes`: street and existing road), else "A Street has no lanes". **Unstored lanes follow the width (Q8)**: a stretch with none stored shows `max(1, round(width / laneWidth))` equal lanes, a side pulled or a width typed leaves them unstored, and any lane op stores them. **`+` puts a lane on the side farther from the drawn line (Q9)**, half a lane on each side of a centred stretch so the axis stays; `−` takes the lane at that same end (a side that would pass 0 stops there, "That side is the drawn line now", and the other gives the rest; one lane cannot go: "A street has one lane at least"). **A side pulled with lanes stored**: out, the edge lane grows; in, the amount is cut from that edge, whole lanes going while it lasts, and an edge lane left under 2 m merges into its neighbour ("The lane on that edge went"): never a refusal. *Equal sides* keeps the lanes, `setStreetEdges` and `setStreetSegmentWidth` scale them to the new width, a mirror reverses them, a split copies them, merging two segments (a node removed, by the verb or by the path engine) whose lanes differ along the walk, or one stored and not the other, stores none (review R4), and a street's run key holds the lanes (to the mm), so lanes stored equal and lanes shown are one run. **On the plan** (`views/editing/path/`): a pill `− 3 lanes +` (`data-path-lanes`) at 0.7 along each stretch (the diamond is at 0.5, the side pills at 0.3), clicks only, Shift every stretch, none on a locked stretch or on one under 80 px (the longest free stretch has it then); each lane line, dashed, 1 px, `--road-lane`, on every street and existing street shown and cut at a T like the axis (the 3D draws none), has a 10 px hit stroke: dragged, the lane beside it takes the drag in 0.25 m steps (Ctrl free, Shift every stretch) and its neighbour gives; typed, the number is the width of the lane on the side of the line the press was on. The side pill reads `Lane 4.5 m · 11.5 m` when lanes are stored. **Properties** shows the lanes as a read-out (`3 lanes · 3.5 + 3.5 + 3.5 m`, `Varies`), no button; *Equal lanes* is a verb of the menu of a street and an existing street (greyed "Its lanes are equal already"). The CAD plan and the model exports carry them (*DXF export, reference lines and notes*). Guards: `onePathEngine.test.ts` (the line drag is a handle start) and the stress test's `lanes-sum` |
| 2026-11-19 | No frontage | (PE.2; the plan's call, said to the user and open to a no; Q6 and Q7 are the user's) **The frontage is gone: any street serves as one.** It left in three steps, each green: its data (task 6), its controls and the agent's tools (task 7: the Frontage tool, its handles, its Layers row and key, `FrontageProperties`, its menu kind, `addFrontage`, `updateFrontage` and `removeFrontage`; `views/layers/gone.ts` lets go of a `frontage` key an older session stored) and its engine and schema (task 8: `Site.frontage`, `FrontageSchema`, `Circulation.frontage`, `DerivedGate.onFrontage` and `joinedPaving`'s `streets` option; `deriveGates(boundary, gates, hosts, roadWidth)`). **The migration** (project 20; `migrateSite` in `model/migrate.ts`, which a library terrain's site, `.gfs`, goes through too in `io/terrains.ts`): each saved frontage becomes an existing street whose drawn line is the run of the boundary it covered (`perimeterPath` from `from` to `to`, counter-clockwise, so its paving lies outside, on the right: `left` 0 and `right` its `streetWidth`, else the standards' `roadWidth`, else 8 m: Q6; 0.5 m at least), keeping its id (suffixed `-street`, then `-street2`…, when an existing road has it) and its name (else *Street*); a frontage with no boundary, or a run of under two points, is dropped. The migration's report says "The site's frontage became 2 existing streets." and "1 frontage with no boundary run to follow was dropped."; a street end of an option that lies on a new street's line is linked to it (`StreetNode.road`), so it follows when that street moves. **The main axis** (`mainAxis(boundary, runs)`) follows the longest stretch of the existing streets along the boundary (`boundaryRoads(site)`: every point, and the middle of each stretch, within 1 m of the outline), else of the boundary: a migrated project's grid angle does not change (`fitGrid` and `vary` read it). The optimizer's `gate.position` slides a gate along its host side, a street's or the boundary's. The sample and the demo have their south street as an existing road, *South street*. **Guards**: `views/editing/__tests__/noFrontage.test.ts` (no source names a frontage but `model/migrate.ts` and `views/layers/gone.ts`, each with its reason) and `views/layers/__tests__/noFrontageRows.test.ts` |
| 2026-11-19 | Gates on any street | (PE.2; the plan's call, said to the user and open to a no; Q7 is the user's) **A gate stands on the boundary or on either side of any street, on the On edge engine.** A gate has `Gate.t` (on the boundary, as before) or `Gate.on: { kind: 'street' or 'existing-road', id, side, at }`, exactly one of the two (the schema's refine): a street's `id` is a segment's, an existing street's its road's, `side` is left or right walking the host's drawn line (a segment from its node a; an existing street along its whole line) and `at` the metres along it; the opening lies on that side's edge and its lane, the way in, runs away from the street. `Kind.onEdge` of `gate` lists three hosts, `street`, `existing-road` and the new core kind **`boundary`** (the site's outline: no verbs and no Layers branch, as `edge` is), each with an adapter (`state/actions/gateEdges.ts`: `gateEdgeOf`, `editGateEdge`, registered in `state/actions/onEdge.ts`; `edgeOf` and `editEdge` take the option, as `editPath` does). An edge is one straight side: `boundary:<i>`, `<segment>:left` or `:right`, a stretch's side of an existing street; slots are 0.5 m (`GATE_SLOT`), a gate keeps its exact place (`t` or `on.at`) and its slot is the one under its middle; the sides are read by `engine/circulation/gateGeometry.ts` and `gateSides.ts` (`gateSideOf`, `gatePlace`). `put` takes one slot ("One gate at a time"), `count` is refused ("Gates are put one at a time"), a side shorter than the gate refuses ("That side is shorter than the gate"), and `slide` may be fractional (a typed distance in m ÷ 0.5) and refuses to pass the end of its side. **A gate slides along its side only**, half its width in from the ends (as the optimizer's `gate.position` always slid it), where it was a drag round the whole boundary: to go elsewhere it is moved by Transform (re-homed to the nearest point of the same kind of host within 5 m, else it stays and says so) or put anew; Alt+drag copies through Transform's copy; Rotate turns a gate only with the existing street it stands on, the others stay ("Gates stay on their side: Rotate leaves them"); a mirror swaps the side of the gates on the street it mirrors whole. **`followGates(before, after)`** runs in `settle()` after `followRoads`: a gate whose host is gone goes; with its host's point count unchanged `at` is kept, clamped half its width from the ends; with the host split, merged or its points changed it is re-homed to the nearest point of the same side of that street within its widest side + 1 m of where it stood, else it goes. **Where a new gate goes**: on an option's street, into that option's own gates (copied from the site's the first time, the note *Own gates for <option>*); on the boundary or an existing street, into the gates the option uses; *+ Gate* in the Circulation tab puts one at the middle of the boundary side nearest an existing street, else of the longest side. **The Gate tool** (`views/plan/access/gateHosts.ts`, `gateHostAt`): a press on a street's paving takes the side of the drawn line it lies on, else the nearest side of any street, existing street or the boundary within 12 px (the boundary wins a tie); the slide, the typed distance and the width are `views/editing/edge/gateSlide.ts`. A created street deleted toasts "Its gates went with it" when gates stood on it; a locked gate refuses a split of its street under it. **A new boundary** (`setBoundary`: a drawing, an import, the agent's tool) drops the gates on the old one (`t`, positions along its perimeter), the site's and each option's own copy; the gates on a street or an existing street (`on`) stay, they stand on their street (an option whose own list held only boundary gates keeps an empty list, not the site's street gates: review R3). **Moved with its street** (review E1, E2, E4): a street moved, turned or mirrored with its nodes takes its gate (`followGates` reads where the gate stood from the host's side with its nodes where they are now, so a knit or a joined end does not lose it; a gate picked with its street is not placed again by Move, Copy puts its copy where it would stand, found by position on the street as the copy was drawn, which a knit may have split (review R2), Rotate and Mirror do not say it stays); a node removed at a bend merges the two segments across it at any angle and the gate of either follows (review R1); it re-homes only on the street's own segments, and goes when its host is gone; a width changed alone is clamped like a place (review R6); a place given outside the street is clamped half its width in (`addGateOn`, `updateGate`). Guards: `oneEdgeEngine.test.ts` (its sixth rule) and the stress test's `gate-host` check |
| 2026-11-19 | Existing streets transformed | (PE.2; the plan's call, said to the user and open to a no) **An existing street is picked on the plan and moved, turned, mirrored, copied, locked and grouped like any object.** **The pick**: a transparent hit stroke in `RoadsLayer` (`<g data-existing-road>`, `data-road-kind="existing"`, `data-street-id="existing:<id>"`, under the created streets' strokes; a locked one shows its padlock and takes no press) in place of `AccessLayer`'s own; the access store holds gates and existing streets, and the mixed selection gains `Picked.existing` (`mix.ts`, kind `existing`: a click, Shift, the window, Layers, `pickRefs`); its body drags (Move), Alt+drag copies, and the clipboard holds `existing: ExistingRoad[]`. **The verbs** (`state/actions/circulation.ts`, called by `selection.ts`'s move, turn, copy, mirror and align): `moveExistingRoads` and `turnExistingRoads` (the points go, the stretches stay), `mirrorExistingRoads` (the points reflected, each stretch's left and right swapped and its lanes reversed, and the gates standing on it change side with it), `copyExistingRoads` (fresh ids, the name kept, no padlock and no group; a step of nothing copies in place) and `lockExistingRoads`. **Padlock and groups**: `ExistingRoad.locked?` and `group?` (project 20, optional); a locked street is held by `settle()` and by `pathOf` (every stretch held), Move, Rotate and Mirror leave it and say "1 existing street stayed: locked", a copy of it is free; a group takes an existing street (the site's, so in every option) beside the other kinds; the kind's base verbs gain `copy` and `lock`, and its own are Group, Ungroup, Equal sides and Equal lanes. **What follows it**: the created streets' ends linked to it (`followRoads`) and the gates standing on it (`followGates`) keep their place along it when it moves, turns or is mirrored; a gate picked with its street is not re-homed. **The agent**: `moveExistingRoad`, `rotateExistingRoad` (about x, y, else the middle of its bounds) and `locked` in `updateExistingRoad`; no mirror or copy tool. Left as it is: a double click on an existing street's body does nothing, and the path handles show for the first existing street picked. Guards: the stress scenarios `movedExisting()` and `views/plan/__tests__/existingPick.test.tsx` |
| 2026-11-19 | Version 20 | (PE.2; the plan's call, as *Version 19*) **`PROJECT_VERSION` is 20**: one version for the lanes (`lanes?` on a street segment and an existing street's stretch), `Standards.laneWidth`, `Gate.on`, `ExistingRoad.locked` and `group` (all optional: nothing is written for them, and the number rises so that an older GFit refuses a file whose lanes it would drop) and for the frontage's removal, which the step writes (`STEPS[19]`: each saved frontage an existing street, and the street ends on its line linked; Decision *No frontage*), with a report in the migration's notes. A stray `site.frontage` in a file of 20 is dropped. The stress test's group "frontages become existing streets (Phase PE.2)" (`frontageKept` allows a round join's difference, `asLegacy` writes the old frontage) and `model/__tests__/frontageMigration.test.ts` guard it |

## Data flow (one direction)

```
Project (serializable JSON — decisions only)
  ├─ site        boundary Contour + rules (setback, maxCoverage; spacing is the brief's apart rules) + (Phase 4) gates (on the boundary or, since Phase PE.2, on a side of a street), existing roads + (Phase 5) source, geo (lat / lon of the origin, north), map (the captured image)
  ├─ programs    uses (warehouse, office…), copied from a typology template; (Phase 4) a dock default
  ├─ buildings   (Phase B) name, colour, context, level height + its levels (name, elevation, height, ×N); at least one
  ├─ options     (Phase B) design options: name + the buildings they show (a building can be in several); (Phase 4) own gates (absent = the site's), manual roads, removed roads, ring road per building
  ├─ masses      footprint + program + level (which says the building) + height (absent = its level's); (Phase 4) own docks
  ├─ kpis        which KPIs the top strip shows, in order (optional; ids of the catalog, modules/catalog.ts; one of a module that is off stays in the list, unseen)
  ├─ dimensions  dimensions drawn by hand, each on a level (optional)
  ├─ units       (Phase 9) display units and currency (default m, USD); values are always stored in metres
  ├─ relations   (Phase 3) edges of the program graph: rules and links between masses
  ├─ standards   (Phase 6) analysis parameters: vehicle profiles, widths, radii, distances (copied from the typology)
  └─ plugins     (Phase O) each plugin's own data by plugin id (optional; the core keeps it, never reads it)
        ↓
derive(project, option)   pure: site → masses → per option: metrics, checks → per building: figures  (→ circulation…)
        ↓
Derived           polygons, metrics and issues of the active option, every option's, every building's figures
        ↓
Consumers: UI (2D plan / 3D / table) · MCP · optimizer
```

**Golden rule:** `engine/` never imports React, the store or `typologies/`. JSON in, JSON out.
That single rule is what makes MCP and optimizers possible later without rewriting anything.

## Layers (`src/`)

| Folder | Responsibility | Core / typology |
|---|---|---|
| `model/` | The data contract (`schema.ts`), validated with zod; `migrate.ts` brings an older file up to the current `version` (and, Phase PE.2, `migrateSite`, the one step for a site, a project's or a terrain's, which turns what older files kept along the boundary into existing streets: Decision *No frontage*) | core |
| `engine/` | `derive()` and its topic modules (`geom/` (and, Phase D.3, `geom/split.ts`, a line cutting a contour), `edge/` (the On edge engine: `onEdge.ts`, Decision *On edge engine*), `path/` (the Path engine, Phase PE.1: `pathOp.ts`, `join.ts`, `ends.ts`, Decision *Path engine*; Phase PE.2: `lanes.ts`, Decision *Lanes*), `circulation/` (Phase PE.2: `gateGeometry.ts` and `gateSides.ts`, the sides a gate stands on, Decision *Gates on any street*), `massing/`, later `graph/`, `circulation/`, `analysis/`…). Pure + tests | core |
| `typologies/` | Templates: programs and site rules per project type (`industrial`, `blank`…), and the modules each offers (`Typology.modules`) | typology |
| `modules/` | The catalog: `catalog.ts` is the one door the app reads (offered / on, the KPIs), `index.ts` the list of modules, `types.ts` the manifest's type; and one folder per module (`core/`, `warehouse/`, `docks/`, `terrain/`, `parking/`), each a `module.ts` manifest and, since M.4–M.7, the terrain's, the docks', the warehouse's and the parking's whole code: `engine/` (pure), `actions.ts` (its verbs), `kpis.ts`, `views/` (React), `params.ts`. The warehouse's `engine/` holds the block's pure core: `shape.ts` and `generate.ts` (the outline in metres, the grid clipped by it), `outlineOp.ts` (an operation run on the face and inset back), `blocks.ts` (a block from a footprint, shared by `drawBlock` and the migration), `massesToBlocks.ts` (the free masses of an older file become blocks), `migrate.ts` and `legacy/` (the module-line engine frozen for the steps of older files; only `migrate.ts` imports it). A module is core code, offered by a typology and on when the project has something it acts on. The manifests, the catalog and each `engine/` are pure; the `views/` are React. See Decisions *Modules*, *Catalog* | core |
| `kinds/` | The kinds of thing a person picks (Phase AS.4): `types.ts` (`Kind`, `Ref`, `LayerGroup`, the base verbs: types only, the one file a module may import), `core.ts` (the core's kinds), `index.ts` (`kinds()`, `kindOf`, `kindsOfGroup`, `ownVerbsOf`); a module brings its own through `modules/catalog.ts` (`moduleKinds`). `locked.ts` says what "locked" means for a `Ref`. Pure, like the catalog: only `model/`, the pure `engine/` and the catalog. See *Kinds* below | core |
| `state/` | Zustand store: current project, selection, undo; its verbs in `actions/` (Phase PE.2: `gateEdges.ts`, the adapters of a gate on a street's or the boundary's side) | core |
| `views/` | `editing/path/` (Phase PE.1: the path handles, pills and drags of streets and existing streets, guarded by `onePathEngine.test.ts`; Phase PE.2: the lane pill and the lane lines' hit strokes), `editing/edge/` (Phase PE.2: `gateSlide.ts`, a gate's slide, put and width on any side; `plan/access/gateHosts.ts`, where the Gate tool lands), `layout/` (the frame: the top bar with the menus (File · Edit · View · Site · Design · Tools · Help), undo / redo and the project; the KPI strip; the panel columns and sheets; UI state: no workspace; the left panel's three tabs, Layers · Blocks · Properties, with the plugins' tabs at the row's end: `PanelTabs.tsx`, Decision *Panel tabs*), `commands/` (the registry: every command once, its label, key and run; the top menu, the dispatcher, the shortcuts window), `tools/` (the **Tools strip** and its parts: `hand.ts` the one tool in hand, `strip.ts` the six groups read from the registry, `ToolStrip.tsx` with its five docks (`stripDock.ts`), `GroupOptions.tsx` the ▾ of General, Massing and Streets; Decisions *Tools strip*, *Tool in hand*, *No areas*), `layers/` (**Layers**: the tree of what the option holds, an eye and a padlock a row, *On top* for drawings and reference lines; `layerView.ts` what is hidden, per project; `LayerProperties.tsx` the settings of the row picked; Decisions *Layers*, *Hidden per project*, *One padlock*, *Settings by what is picked*), `show/` (the drafting bar's Detail select), `viewport/` (plan / 3D / split + what floats on it, the levels rail, the drafting bar with Plan · 3D · Split at its right end), `scene/` (the 3D view, lazy), `panels/` (Properties, which shows the settings of what is picked, and its sections: the site's, the building's, the circulation's, the gates', the parking's; Summary), `stats/` (KPI strip and picker, program bars, warnings chip), `plan/` (SVG plan of the active level and the others (`LevelLayers`, `reference`, `OtherBuildings`), editing: snap, handles, `windowSelect` (window and crossing), `tools/` Mass · Corner · Chamfer · Dimension · Ruler · Mirror · Array · Align, `command/` the command line, `dimensions/`, `annotations/` the reference lines and the notes (Phase AS.5), clipboard), `editing/` (the one right-click menu, Phase AS.4: `menu/` builds it from the kind and opens it; `refOps.ts`, `rename.ts`; and, Phase D.1, `shape/` the Shape engine's one set of handles for a mass, a hole and a block, `ShapeHandles.tsx` with `drags.ts`, `host.ts` and `labels.ts`, and `dimension/` the typed dimension, `typed.ts` the store, `DimensionField.tsx` the number beside the handle, `useTypedCommand.ts`, and, Phase D.3, `edge/` the On edge engine's views (`EdgeHandles.tsx`, `host.ts`, `edgeState.ts`, `labels.ts`; the warehouse's `blockEdgeHost.ts` and `SiteBlockTools.tsx` fill its host seam; guards `oneEdgeEngine.test.ts`, `oneShapeEngine.test.ts`; `modules/warehouse/engine/courts.ts` and `dockRule.ts` the pure court and dock rules); Decisions *Shape engine*, *Typed dimension*), `controls/` (shared: fields, `Icon`, `Segmented`, menus) | core |
| `io/` | Files: `.gft` (header + zod), the model export (`exportModel.ts`: `.3dm` with rhino3dm, IFC by hand), the CAD plan (`exportPlan.ts`: what a DXF plan carries; `planInterior.ts` a block cut at its floor; `planPaving.ts` the one paving and its curb returns; Phase PE.2: the lane lines on `C-ROAD-STRP`), the decision log's CSV; `site/` the site's files (DXF, KML / KMZ…) and where their shapes land | core |
| `devtools/` | Dev-only pages, never in the build: `state-graph/` (modules + live store calls) | core |
| `styles/` | Design tokens and base CSS | core |
| `api/` | The agent's tools (`api/tools/`: the verbs and readings, for the agent in the app and MCP) | core |
| `plugins/` | The seam (`index.ts` the core's only door, `registry.ts` the list, `slots.tsx`, `settings.ts`, `host.ts` a plugin's only door to the app, `types.ts`) and one folder per plugin: `optimizer/` (`plugin.ts` its manifest, `data.ts` its data in a project, `engine/` pure, `run/` its worker and the run's store, `views/` lazy). See Decision *Plugin seam* | plugin |

## Kinds (`src/kinds/`)

What the registry says (`src/kinds/core.ts`, and the modules' kinds through the catalog: the warehouse's `kinds.ts`). A kind is
a kind of thing a person picks: its id, its label, its branch of the Layers tree (`—`: none, the Blocks tab and the block editor)
and the base verbs it takes; a verb it cannot take is not in its menu. `__tests__/registry.test.ts` reads this table against the
registry (Phase AS.4, Decision *Kinds*).

| Kind (id) | Label | Group | Base verbs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mass` | Block | buildings | rename, duplicate, copy, delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `mass-hole` | Hole | — | — |
| `building` | Building | buildings | rename, delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `level` | Level | buildings | rename, delete, lock, properties |
| `street` | Street | circulation | copy, delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `existing-road` | Existing road | site | rename, copy, delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `gate` | Gate | circulation | delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `lot` | Lot | parking | rename, delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `dimension` | Dimension | annotations | delete, select-all, hide, properties |
| `drawing` | Drawing file | site | delete, lock, hide, properties |
| `drawing-layer` | Drawing layer | site | lock, hide, properties |
| `parcels` | Parcels | site | lock, hide, properties |
| `grid` | Grid | reference | hide, properties |
| `reference-line` | Reference line | reference | delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `note` | Note | annotations | rename, delete, select-all, lock, hide, properties |
| `boundary` | Site boundary | — | — |
| `block-type` | Block type | — | rename, duplicate, delete, select-all |
| `zone` | Zone | — | — |
| `hole` | Hole | — | — |
| `court` | Truck court | — | — |
| `block-court` | Truck court | — | — |
| `element` | Element | — | — |
| `edge` | Edge | — | — |

**The Shape engine** (Phase D.1, Decision *Shape engine*): a kind that has an outline the engine edits says so with `shape` and what it refuses (`without`), data only; the test of the registry says it, not this table (a column here would break the table's guard): `mass` takes every operation but `move` (a mass moves by Transform); `mass-hole` (core, label *Hole*; `<massId>/<k>`, because the warehouse's `hole` is the block editor's; since D.2 one writer serves both) all but `bump`, `radius` and `chamfer`; `block-type` (a block's mass the same, on the site) takes every operation but `move` since D.2: a block's **outline is free**, its module lines a snap (*Outline in metres*); a block's hole and the block editor's `hole` (all but `bump`, `radius` and `chamfer`) go through one writer. A truck court is a shape of its own (Phase D.3): `court` (the block editor's, `<typeId>/<edgeId>[/<courtId>]`) and `block-court` (a placed block's on the site, `<hallMassId>/<edgeId>[/<courtId>]`, label *Truck court* too) take every operation but `move` (a court moves with its wall), `split` among them (the only kind that does), through one writer; its wall side stays on its wall, and a rectangle stays a rectangle while only its depth moves.

## Engine pattern

Borrowed from Plaza-Practiko (`proyecto → derivar() → derivado`):

- **Project** (`model/schema.ts`): only what a person or optimizer decides. Never stores computed values.
- **derive()** (`engine/derive.ts`): the single entry point. Steps in fixed order; each reads only earlier steps.
  A failure in one mass becomes an Issue; the others keep going.
- **Derived** (`engine/types.ts`): everything the views draw. Rebuilt on every change, never saved.
- **Issues**: broken rules as data (`code`, `severity`, `massIds`, `message`). The UI lists them; an optimizer
  counts them as penalties.

```
engine/
  derive.ts            entry point
  types.ts             Derived, DerivedMass, Metrics, Issue
  geom/                polygon, contour (port), boolean + offset/inset (port), footprint, edit (port: walls, corners, radii)
  buildings.ts         which building a level or a mass is in, an option's masses (Phase B)
  levels.ts            spans, ground floor, a mass's level and height
  massing/             site (buildable area), metrics (areas, coverage), checks (rules → issues)
```

## Edit loop (how a drag reaches the screen)

```
press on a mass, a wall or a corner (views/plan/useDrag → editing.ts: startMassDrag; a wall, a corner or a hole: views/editing/shape/drags.ts, one start each)
  → build once what attracts: other masses, site, setback   snap/sources + snap/move.targetsOf
move
  → offset since drag start → snap                           mass: snap/move · wall: snap/handles · corner: snap/resolve (Shift: none)
  → store.preview(p => moveMass | editShape(ref, op))        pure actions, state/actions/ (null = rejected)
  → derive(project)                                          engine
  → PlanSvg + panels re-render; SnapMark draws sign, label, guides and tracking lines
typed number (D.1: beside the handle, during or right after the drag) → Enter → the same op, exact, one undo step
release → store.commit()   (the whole drag = one undo step)
Esc     → store.cancel()   (back to the project from before the drag)
```

- **Actions** (`state/actions/`) are pure `Project → Project`: the same verbs an MCP tool will expose.
  They never mutate and return the same object when nothing changes (no empty undo steps).
- **Store** (`state/store.ts`) is the only place where the pure world meets React.
- **Camera** (`views/plan/camera.ts`) is pure math between world metres (y up) and screen pixels (y down).
- **Snap** (`views/plan/snap/`) is pure too and lives in `views/`: it decides where the pointer *means*, not what
  the project is. `kinds` (vocabulary) · `points` · `walls` · `lines` (tracking) · `grid` · `move` (who wins for a
  mass) · `resolve` (for a corner) · `handles` (for a wall) · `sources` (what attracts) · `settings` (per-browser
  store) · `SnapMark` (drawing).
  An optimizer never needs it: it moves masses by numbers.
- **Handles** (`views/editing/shape/ShapeHandles.tsx`, Phase D.1): pills on walls, dots on corners, a chamfer's two ends,
  a hole's edge, double-click a wall to add a corner (a block: to pick its bay), Del or a double click removes the
  selected corner. One component for a mass, a hole and a block, on the site (live) and in the block editor (a ghost);
  it only draws and calls `actions.editShape`, and every drag is a typed drag (`views/editing/dimension/`). The
  rotation handle and the frame of several masses are Transform's (`MassHandles.tsx`).

## State graph (dev only: `npm run dev` → `/state-graph`, or ⋯ → "State graph" in the top bar)

```
code ──import.meta.glob (?raw, HMR)──▶ devtools/state-graph/map.ts ──▶ modules, exports, imports, slices each view reads
app  ──store door──▶ state/trace/tracer.ts ──▶ TraceEvent ──bus──▶ channel (BroadcastChannel 'gfit-trace') ──▶ page
         who called (stack) · verbs + engine/derive with args and ms (state/trace/calls.ts) · new slices · diff · issues
page ──hello──▶ app: current state snapshot          app reload / close ──▶ "App loaded / closed" in the log
```

- One event per store door (`apply`, `preview`, `commit`, `cancel`, `undo`, `redo`, `open`, `select`), even when it changes
  nothing: the log is what really happened. A drag's previews are grouped into one row.
- On the map an event lights its round trip: the view that called → `store.ts` → the verb modules and
  `engine/derive.ts` (dashed: engine modules they load) → the views that read a slice that is new (they re-render).
- What it shows: **layers** on or off (chips top left), **Group folders** (each folder under a layer is one card; a click on a
  card opens it, on a folder's name groups it) and **Only event** (just what the event touched). `view.ts` filters the map
  and `layout()` packs what is left.
- It keeps up with the code by itself: a new file, verb, import or `useApp((s) => s.x)` read shows up on save.
  A new action module needs one line in `state/actions/index.ts` (a test says so).

## Workspaces: from macro to micro and back (planned)

*(These are the design's stages, the plan of the phases. In the app the workspace is not a state any more: no tab or step changes the tools, the Tools strip is always on screen; Decision *No areas*, AS.2.)*

The viewport has one workspace per design stage. They all read and write the same `Project`, so you can go back to
any stage and the change flows to every other one (graph, masses, KPIs). Plan · 3D · Split stays a separate switch:
it is how you look; the workspace is what you edit.

```
Portfolio → Site → Brief → Rules → Massing → Analysis → back to Portfolio
 where      parcel  programs  relations  plan, 3D  routes,     compare
            rules   areas, $                       clearance   the options
```

| Workspace | What you edit | Phase |
|---|---|---|
| Portfolio | which projects exist and where; compare them | 9 |
| Site | parcel boundary, rules, georeference; gates, existing roads (4) | 3.14 (the step, on the demo site); 4 (access); 5 (boundary, map) |
| Brief | programs, target areas and counts, a new mass's size (3.0; 2:1 of the area per mass unless set by hand, *New mass size*), fixed or up to N:1 for the optimizer (O.3c, *Fixed size*); costs, rents, land price with the pro forma (3.18) | 3.0 |
| Rules | rules between programs, the relations they make on the masses, ▶ Solve (abstract diagram and 3D) | 3 |
| Massing | masses in plan and 3D; docks and roads (4) | 1–2 |
| Analysis | standards; runs the tools | 6 |

- **Baseline and deltas**: pin a version as the baseline and the KPI strip shows ▲▼ against it. Going back to the
  brief and changing one number shows what it did to everything downstream.

### Portfolio (out of scope since 2026-11-04)

- **Three levels**: portfolio (many projects) → project (one parcel + one typology) → versions (its history,
  as in Practiko). Design options live inside a project (Phase B); projects on the same parcel (another typology)
  are grouped under one pin.
- **Storage**: one designer at a time, in the browser, behind a `Library` interface that a shared Azure library can
  replace later (see Decisions).
- **Units**: metres and USD by default; each project can switch to ft or another currency (see Decisions).
- **Georeference**: a site has lat / lon and a north angle (Phase 5, `Site.geo`), so it sits on the world map. A
  geolocated file (KML, KMZ, GeoJSON, Shapefile) or a DXF in UTM brings it; a DXF in local coordinates is placed once
  (a pasted location, then *Place*).
- **Two views**: the **map** (true location, OSM basemap, nearby projects grouped) and the **lineup** (every project
  side by side at the same scale, however far apart). Plus charts: KPI against KPI, a ranking table.
- **KPI snapshots**: each saved version keeps its KPIs in the portfolio index (outside the `Project`, which never
  stores computed values), so the map and the charts do not recompute every project. Opening a project recomputes.
- **Comparable KPIs**: a common set for every typology (site area, GFA, coverage, FAR, cost, rent…) plus each
  typology's own. Comparing projects that use different standards shows a warning.
- **Parcel selector**: search a place, draw the parcel on the map, pick a typology → a new project.
- **Ask the LLM**: an assistant that reads the portfolio's KPIs through the same tools as MCP and answers "which one
  suits me better, and why". The API key never ships in the build (MELI's mistake): a small proxy or a key the user
  enters, decided in 9.5.
- Reference: OSMnx-data-scraper, ideas only (interactive basemap, side-by-side maps of distant places). The portfolio
  does not pull data about the site.

## Program graph (Phase 3)

A design mode where the project is read and arranged as a graph of programs, by hand or through MCP. It is core, so
every typology has it. Not to be confused with the dev-only *State graph*, which shows which actions and modules fire.

```
1 Brief (programs, targets, sizes) ── Approve brief ──▶ 2 Rules: diagram of programs (n of N placed)
                                                                 rules under it → each mass with its nearest partner
                                                                 → met / broken → Issues
                                     ── ▶ Solve (the brief's missing masses, then arrange) ──▶
3 Massing: edit as always   →  the pairs and the colours follow (arcs and chips on the plan; Graph over the geometry)
```

- **Node = a program** (the Rules step's diagram) or a mass (Massing's graph). A new mass gets its program's size (3.0; its area per mass at 2:1 unless set by hand, *New mass size*).
- **Rule** = a sentence between two programs, stored in `Project.rules` (Decision *Rules*). **Relation** = a rule
  applied to two masses: derived, never stored, so the plan and the graph can never disagree.

| Kind | Sentence | Pairs | Met (in plan, on the footprints) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `adjacent` | A must be adjacent to B | each A with its nearest B | they share ≥ `minShared` of wall (0: ≥ 1 m) |
| `near` | A within `max` m of B | each A with its nearest B | the gap ≤ `max` |
| `apart` | A at least `min` m from B | each A with every B | the gap ≥ `min` |
| `attached` | A moves with B | each A with its nearest B | always (a link: A follows B's moves) |
| `aligned` | later | | a wall of A on the line of a wall of B |

- How far two programs keep apart is an `apart` rule; the site has no spacing between masses (Decision *Spacing by rules*).
- `required`: broken, an error; otherwise a warning. `weight`: its pull in *Solve* and, later, the optimizer.
- **Who moves** (Decision *Solve*): of two masses, the smaller (floor area × floors; a stack of one building's floors
  is one, summed); the dominant one stays. Locked and context masses never move.
- `access` (4.4) is not a rule between programs: the circulation checks it for every mass with docks (a truck gate
  reaches its court by the roads) and a broken one is an Issue at the mass (code `access`). Named now, built later:
  `stacked` (levels).
- Typologies only add **data**: example rules between their programs (industrial: three).

| Edit | Effect on the geometry |
|---|---|
| Add or change a rule | nothing moves by itself; the relations are found and checked again. *Solve* moves masses to meet them |
| Move or turn a mass | its *moves with* followers come along; the pairs are found again |
| Delete or add a mass, change its program | the counts against the brief change: Massing's notice offers ▶ Solve or *Update brief* |
| − or + in the Brief tab | a mass of that program goes or is placed by its rules, and the brief asks for what the option has |
| ▶ Solve | the masses the brief still asks for are placed (biggest first), then the option's masses slide to meet the rules (`engine/graph/arrange.ts`), one undo step |

- **MCP**: the verbs (`addRule` · `updateRule` · `removeRule` · `addNode` · `addFromBrief` · `arrange` · `solve`) are the MCP
  tools (inputs = JSON Schema from zod), plus `getGraph` later (nodes with program, area and centroid; relations
  with status). Every call returns the new Issues, so an LLM can reconfigure geometry and rules and see what broke.

## Analysis tools (planned, Phase 6)

Tools that measure what the geometry *does*, beyond area, cost and distance. The program graph says what we
**want** (office `near` warehouse); analyses measure what we **got** (the real walking route is 140 m). The gap
between the two is what `reorganize` and the optimizers close.

Agreed (2026-10-14): start with **clearance and routes**. Flows: trucks, cars, pedestrians, fire access, egress.
No external data APIs.

**Result contract** (`AnalysisResult`, one per tool run):

- **KPIs**: numbers with id, unit and label. They join the KPI registry (2.3), so the strip, the compare table and
  the optimizers read them like any other metric.
- **Findings**: an Issue plus a fix (which mass, which way, how far). A fix is offered only after applying it to a
  copy and checking again: if it does not clear the finding, or breaks something else, it is dropped.
- **Overlays**: geometry to draw in the plan and in 3D (routes, a clearance raster, an isovist polygon).

**How they run:** cheap ones inside `derive()`. Heavy ones on demand, in a Web Worker, cached by a key of the
project. While dragging, a cheap version; the full run on release. Each overlay toggle shows its state: idle,
running, ready, stale (the project changed since the run) or error.

**Standards** (`Project.standards`, editable, because norms change with the site and the client):

- Vehicle profiles: pedestrian, car, van, box truck, semi-trailer, fire truck, forklift. Width, length, turning radii
  (inner / outer) and speed.
- Thresholds: road widths, truck court depth, fire lane reach, egress distance…
- The typology gives the starting set, each value with its source. A new project **copies** it, as it does with
  programs, so an old project gives the same results even if the defaults change later. The panel marks the values
  that differ from the typology's default.
- Later: save a set as a client preset and reuse it in other projects.

| Tool | What it measures | How | KPIs (examples) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Clearance | gaps between masses and to the site edge; road widths, pinch points | distance field over a raster that remembers the nearest mass; the ridge between two masses is their gap | min gap (m), pinch points, % of road narrower than the vehicle |
| Routes | shortest route per flow and vehicle: gate → dock → gate, gate → parking, parking → entrance, exit → assembly point | obstacles grown by half the vehicle width (configuration space), then A* weighted by metres on a visibility graph or grid | length (m), time (min), turns, "blocked at X" |
| Vehicle fit | can the design vehicle use the road and reach the dock | swept path at turns (turning radii), backing room at truck courts | docks reachable, court depth missing (m) |
| Safety | conflicts between flows; fire access | crossings of pedestrian and truck routes, reversing zones, dead ends; fire lane reach to every façade | crossings, dead ends, % of façade within fire reach |
| Visibility | what the gate, the guard house or a crossing sees | exact isovist (angular sweep against mass edges; masses always block); "mass X hides dock Y" | isovist area (m²), % of yard seen, hidden docks, sight triangle at the gate |

**MCP:** each analysis is a tool that returns its KPIs, the top findings with their fixes, and a short text. The LLM
reads the fix, calls an action, and runs the analysis again.

**Lessons from the references** (team_03 *Spatial Flow*, MELI; ideas only):

- "Shortest" means metres (Dijkstra / A*), not hops. One distance function for both the score and the drawing.
- The traveller has a width: clearance belongs inside the routing, not in a separate check.
- Distance fields in true metres (Euclidean), not grid steps.
- Show raw KPIs against their thresholds, not an opaque letter grade; a weighted score is only for the optimizer.
- One value per rule, with its source (team_03 had two different forklift aisle widths).
- A spatial index once the road network grows.

## Phases

Priority (2026-10-14): **site massing first**. Buildings govern exterior circulation; circulation is
derived from the masses and optimized afterwards. Interiors wait until massing is done (11).
The Practiko UI (2) comes first, so every later phase builds its buttons into the final frame. Buildings and options
(B) come next: the graph, circulation and the KPIs all need to know which masses form a building and which option
they belong to. The program graph (3) is massing too, so it comes before circulation; circulation can then read
which masses need access.
Priority (2026-10-22, Decision *Inside-out rules*): 8.1b, then **E. Terrain**, **G. Grid** and **P. Stages**, before
6 and 7.
Scope (2026-10-26, Decision *Scope without analyses*): **6** and **3.18** are **deferred**, skipped when looking for the
next step; 7 goes on with the KPIs there are; Interiors (11) stays off. Since 2026-10-26 too (Decision *Scope without
portfolio*): **R.2** is **deferred**; **9** and **10**, deferred then, left the scope on 2026-11-04 (Decision *Out of scope: portfolio and documentation*).
Steps marked **⏸ Layout** are design stops: we agree where the buttons go before writing code. Everything else
copies Practiko.

  - [x] 5.3 KML / KMZ import: each placemark's polygons (outer ring) and closed paths, named by it; projected to local metres round the middle of the shapes, which becomes the georeference; one polygon is imported at once, several are clicked on the plan (`io/site/kml.ts`; fflate for the zip)
